Asymptotic growth of translation-dilation orbits
Victor Y. Wang

Abstract
. By studying some Clausen-like multiple Dirichlet series, we complete the proof of Manin’s conjecture for sufficiently split smooth equivariant compactifications of the translation-dilation group over the rationals. Secondary terms remain elusive in general.



1991 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14G05; Secondary 11F72, 11G25, 11G50, 14M27

1.
Introduction

Even for rational projective varieties over
\mathbb{Q}

(e.g. the Fermat cubic surface
[
23
]
),
Manin’s conjecture (see
[
25
]
) remains extremely difficult in general,
due to the arithmetic complexity of height functions governing point counts.
Hope increases
in the presence of symmetry or other favorable structure.
The present paper concerns one-sided
equivariant compactifications
of a non-abelian algebraic group
G
;
these are defined to be projective
G
-varieties
\mathcal{Y}
equipped with a
G
-equivariant open immersion
G\to\mathcal{Y}
of dense image.

Cases of
two-sided
compactifications
(e.g.
[
47
,
46
,
29
]
),
and especially cases where the group in question is
abelian

(e.g.
[
1
,
12
,
9
]
),
have already received fairly comprehensive treatments,
thanks to the simpler (yet deep) representation-theoretic quantities involved.
This is explained in
[
52
,
50
]
.
For us, a certain infinite-dimensional representation (see
[
52
]
*Proposition 3.1; cf.
[
24
]
*§6.7, §7.6;
[
32
]
over
\mathbb{R}
) enjoys a subtle significance first explored, but not fully isolated, in
[
52
]
.

We build on
[
52
]
.
From now on, let
G=\{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\}\subseteq\operatorname{GL}_{2}
be the
ax+b
group,
viewed as an algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Explicitly, the group law on
(a,b),(u_{1},u_{2})\in G
is

(1.1)

(a,b)\cdot(u_{1},u_{2})=(au_{1},au_{2}+b).

Let
X
be a smooth, projective, right-sided equivariant compactification of
G
over
\mathbb{Q}
; so
G
acts on
X
from the right, and the inclusion
G\to X
is
G
-equivariant on the right.
See §
7
for some examples and constructions of
X
.
In this paper, we resolve key issues from
[
52
]
,
and establish Manin’s conjecture
(via the dense open set
G
)
for all sufficiently split
X
.

Theorem 1.1
.

Assume
X
is strictly split (Definition
2.3
).
If
\mathsf{H}
is a standard Weil height (Definition
2.4
) associated to the anticanonical line bundle
K_{X}^{-1}
,
then

(1.2)

\lim_{B\to\infty}\frac{\#\{x\in G(\mathbb{Q}):\mathsf{H}(x)\leq B\}}{B(\log{B})^{\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))-1}}=\mathcal{A}_{X,\mathsf{H}},

where
\mathcal{A}_{X,\mathsf{H}}>0
is Peyre’s constant (
2.9
).
Moreover, if
w\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
B\geq 2
, then

(1.3)

\sum_{x\in G(\mathbb{Q})}w{\left(\frac{\mathsf{H}(x)}{B}\right)}=B(\log{B})^{\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))-1}\left(\mathcal{A}_{X,\mathsf{H}}\int_{0}^{\infty}w(t)\,dt+O_{X,\mathsf{H},w}{\left(\frac{1}{\log{B}}\right)}\right).

Remark 1.2
.

We work in “geometric generality, realized arithmetically over
\mathbb{Q}
”
to keep ideas clean and clear.
But our arguments may well extend
to arbitrary number fields.
The splitness condition on
X
would then always be satisfiable after base change;
alternatively, it
might be directly removable with enough extra notation and combinatorial effort.

Remark 1.3
.

Generalizing our work to equivariant compactifications of homogeneous spaces of
G
(see e.g.
[
15
]
for the distinction) may require serious new ideas, because
(1) a quotient
H\backslash G
of
G
need not be a group (unlike in the case of abelian groups);
and (2) in general,
(H\backslash G)(\mathbb{Q})
includes Galois-invariant elements of
H(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\backslash G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, not just
H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})
.

To compare our Theorem
1.1
with
[
52
]
*Theorem 5.1, we need to elaborate on the geometry of
X
.
Certainly
X
is a rational surface, since it is birational to
G
.
View the coordinates
a
,
b
on
G
as rational functions on
X
.
For
f\in\operatorname{Rat}(X)
, let
\operatorname{div}_{0}(f)
,
\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(f)
be the zero and polar Weil divisors of
f
, respectively; so
\operatorname{div}(f)=\operatorname{div}_{0}(f)-\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(f)
.

The boundary
D\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X\setminus G

coincides with the indeterminacy locus of the rational map
(a,b)\colon X\dashrightarrow G
.
So by the algebraic Hartogs lemma
[
49
]
*
Tag 0BCS
, we have

(1.4)

D=\operatorname{Supp}(\operatorname{div}(a))\cup\operatorname{Supp}(\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(b)).

Write
D=\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}D_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
where the
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
are irreducible over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Then
[
52
]
*Theorem 5.1 proves our Theorem
1.1
for all split
X
satisfying the following conditions, which we list in roughly increasing order of significance:
(i) the divisor
D\cup\operatorname{Supp}(\operatorname{div}_{0}(b))
has strict normal crossings,
(ii) the divisor
\operatorname{div}(a)
is reduced,
and (iii) the mysterious condition
(for each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
)

(1.5)

\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)<0\Rightarrow\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b)<\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a).

We remark that for split
X
,
the condition (i) implies in particular that
X
is strictly split.

The condition (
1.5
)
is related to the positivity of
K_{X}^{-1}
;
see Proposition
3.1
below.
Similar conditions, involving variables and degrees, are familiar in the circle method.
Later (in §
3
) we will prove the following result, which we state now to give numerical context for (
1.5
):

Proposition 1.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{j}\in J
and
c\in\mathbb{Q}
.
Then
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)\leq\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)
.

When (
1.5
) fails, we seem to need a new idea.
The main culprit, revealed by geometric calculations going beyond
[
52
]
, turns out to be pairs of divisors at which (
1.5
) and a counterpart for
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)>0
fail.
Relevant here are the
special
divisors we now define.

Definition 1.5
.

Given
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, call
D_{\mathfrak{j}}

special
if

\max_{c\in\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)
.

Remark 1.6
.

Given
c\in\mathbb{Q}
and a compactification
i\colon G\to X
,
the
left
translate
i_{c}\colon G\to X
given by
({}^{\prime}a,{}^{\prime}b)\mapsto i((1,c)({}^{\prime}a,{}^{\prime}b))

satisfies
{}^{\prime}a=a
and
{}^{\prime}b+c=b
.
This explains why
\mathbb{Q}
-translates of
b
, not just
b
itself, matter in our work.

Suppose there are
\mathfrak{K}\geq 0
special divisors with
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)<0
, and
\mathfrak{L}\geq 0
special divisors with
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)>0
.
Then the main issue,
after new “complexity-lowering” non-archimedean calculations in §
4
(in the spirit of
[
33
,
27
,
54
,
56
]
)
relying on a new
G
-related source (Proposition
3.5
) of local coordinates and cancellation in complete exponential integrals,
is to appropriately bound a class of multiple Dirichlet series including,
roughly,

(1.6)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha=(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}})/(n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}):\\
\textnormal{pairwise coprime }m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{1},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathcal{F}(\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{0}\alpha)}{m_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma^{\prime}_{\ell}}},

for some constants
\mathsf{c}_{0},\mathsf{c}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}}\in\mathbb{Q}
and a suitable Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{>0}\to\mathbb{C}
of a reciprocal archimedean height function.
More precisely, a variant (
5.8
)
of the series (
1.6
)
will appear in §
5
,
but for now we remain somewhat vague in order to sketch ideas.

To analyze (
1.6
),
we first use a change of variables and a height derivative bound (Lemma
5.1
)
in order to prove a decay bound

(\alpha\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha})^{k}\mathcal{F}(\alpha)\ll_{k}(\alpha^{\xi}+\alpha^{-\xi})^{-1}

for all integers
k\geq 0
,
for some small real
\xi>0
.
We then decompose (
1.6
) into regions according to the quality of expected oscillation as
m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{1},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}
vary in dyadic intervals.

When
\#\{\mathsf{c}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}}\}\geq 2
, we find cancellation in certain correlations of Kloosterman fractions;
[
19
,
2
]
need not apply, but reciprocity plus a multivariate Weyl-type inequality (Proposition
5.5
) for monomials suffices, though just barely when in the most lopsided ranges.
Ultimately, for certain
\beta^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\beta^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{K}},\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\gamma^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{L}}=1+O(s-1)
, the series (
1.6
)

morally
has a pole of order
\leq\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))-1
at
s=1
.
Theorem
1.1
then follows.

In our setting, one could express (
1.6
) as a weighted average of Clausen zeta functions
\sum_{m\geq 1}\mathsf{e}(m\theta)m^{-s}
over a family of angles
\theta
.
Clausen functions for fixed
\theta
can be studied in depth (see e.g.
[
38
]
), but obtaining uniform results may be difficult,
since the complexity of
\theta
may vary wildly.
Our work smooths away such difficulties by
averaging.

It remains open to obtain a power-saving asymptotic expansion of (
1.3
) in general;
secondary terms (cf.
[
33
,
27
,
54
]
) elude us when
D
is sufficiently complicated.
In our approach, the key missing ingredient seems to be to
meromorphically continue (not just
bound
)
series such as
\sum_{m,n\geq 1}\mathsf{e}(m/n)\tau_{i}(m)\tau_{j}(n)m^{-s_{1}}n^{-s_{2}}
,
where
\tau_{i}
is the
i
-fold divisor function.
Existing work on multiple Dirichlet series, such as
[
18
]
,
might help.
Takloo-Bighash
pointed out to us that
Pi’s work in
[
44
]
,
which is based on the functional equation of the Hurwitz zeta function,
implies that
\sum_{m,n\geq 1}\mathsf{e}(m/n)m^{-s_{1}}n^{-s_{2}}
,
which converges absolutely for
\Re(s_{1}),\Re(s_{2})>1
,
has a meromorphic continuation to all
(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
.
This might generalize.

We remark that a homological stability result
for equivariant compactifications of solvable linear algebraic groups over
\mathbb{C}

is known
[
6
]
.
It would be interesting to try to identify any connections between
the methods of
[
6
]
and the present paper.

1.1.
Conventions

A
variety
is an integral, separated scheme of finite type over a field.
We let
\operatorname{Rat}(\Upsilon)
denote the field of rational functions on an integral scheme
\Upsilon
.
(If
\eta
is the generic point of
\Upsilon
, then
\operatorname{Rat}(\Upsilon)=\mathcal{O}_{\Upsilon,\eta}
.)
A reduced, effective Weil divisor on
X
has
strict normal crossings
if
its irreducible components
\Upsilon_{i}
are smooth,
the pairwise intersections
\Upsilon_{i}\cap\Upsilon_{j}
are smooth of dimension
\leq 0
,
and the triple intersections
\Upsilon_{i}\cap\Upsilon_{j}\cap\Upsilon_{k}
are empty.

Let
\mathbb{Z}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{p\mid n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{p\mid n}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathsf{e}(t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\exp(2\pi it)
.
For
y\in\mathbb{Q}_{n}
, let

\mathsf{e}(y\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{e}(y^{\prime}),

for any
y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}[1/n]
with
y^{\prime}\equiv y\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n}}
.
Define the additive automorphic character

\psi=\prod_{v}\psi_{v}\colon\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}

by
\psi(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{v}\psi_{v}(x_{v})
for
x=(x_{v})_{v}\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
where

\psi_{\infty}(x_{\infty})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{e}(-x_{\infty}),\quad\psi_{p}(x_{p})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{e}(x_{p}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

Here
p\in\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
denotes a prime, and
\infty
denotes the real place of
\mathbb{Q}
.

For
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\in\mathbb{C}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
we write
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\ll_{\mathscr{P}}\mathcal{Q}_{2}
,
or
\mathcal{Q}_{2}\gg_{\mathscr{P}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}
,
or
\mathcal{Q}_{1}=O_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathcal{Q}_{2})
,
to mean that
\lvert\mathcal{Q}_{1}\rvert\leq\mathcal{B}\mathcal{Q}_{2}

for a real constant
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(\mathscr{P})>0
depending on
\mathscr{P}
.
We write
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\asymp\mathcal{Q}_{2}

to mean
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\ll\mathcal{Q}_{2}\ll\mathcal{Q}_{1}
.
For a statement
\mathcal{S}
, let
\bm{1}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}1
if
\mathcal{S}
holds, and
\bm{1}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0
otherwise.

There are some pieces of notation, such as the letters
f
and
A
,
that we will sometimes number sequentially,
in order to avoid confusion between different contexts.

2.
Background

Recall the setting of §
1
,
with
G
,
X
specified as in the paragraph before Theorem
1.1
.
We need some background on geometry, heights, and analysis,
drawn mostly from
[
52
]
*§§1–3.

2.1.
Geometry

Let
g\in G
act on elements
f\in\operatorname{Rat}(X)
from the right:

(fg)(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f(xg^{-1})
.
There is an analogous left action on
\operatorname{Rat}(G)=\operatorname{Rat}(X)
,
but by default on
\operatorname{Rat}(X)
we act from the right.
These actions on functions induce corresponding actions on differentials.

Let
K_{X}
be the canonical line bundle on
X
.
It has two local sections of particular interest:
the right-invariant top form
\omega\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}db\,da/a
on
G
,
and the left-invariant top form
\omega/a
on
G
.
For each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, let
\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\omega)
;
then
-\operatorname{div}(\omega)=\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.

Throughout the paper,
a useful bookkeeping tool is
the
equivariant Picard group

\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{Z}D_{\mathfrak{j}};

see
[
52
]
*§1 or
[
34
]
*Proposition 2.12(2) for details, noting that
D_{\mathfrak{j}}G=D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.

Proposition 2.1
.

We have
\operatorname{Pic}(X_{E})=\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X_{E})/\mathbb{Z}\operatorname{div}(a|_{X_{E}})
for any field
E\supseteq\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{Pic}(G_{E})=0
,
the irreducible components of
D_{E}
generate
\operatorname{Pic}(X_{E})
.
(Here we identify a Weil divisor
W
on
X_{E}

with the invertible sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X_{E}}(W)\subseteq\operatorname{Rat}(X_{E})
.)
Relations correspond to
E
-morphisms
X_{E}\setminus D_{E}\to(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{E}
,
namely
ca^{k}
for
c\in E^{\times}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

An important combinatorial role will be played by
the following closed cone:

\Lambda(X)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\subseteq\operatorname{Pic}(X)\otimes\mathbb{R}.

(In fact,

\Lambda(X)
is the
cone of pseudo-effective divisors
of
X
,
by
[
52
]
*Proposition 1.1(3).)
By the following result,
K_{X}^{-1}
lies in
\Lambda^{\circ}(X)
, the interior of
\Lambda(X)
.

Proposition 2.2

(
[
52
]
*Proposition 1.2)
.

We have
-\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\omega/a)\geq 1
for all
\mathfrak{j}\in J
.

The
\mathbb{Z}
-module

\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
is finite free, since
X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is a rational surface over an algebraically closed field.
Also,
\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})^{\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}
by
[
39
]
*Remark 3.2(ii), because
X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\neq\emptyset
;
so in particular,
\operatorname{Pic}(X)
is free.
Say
X
is
split
if

\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})

[
40
]
*Definition 2.2(a).
By Proposition
2.1
,

X
is split if and only if each
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is geometrically irreducible.

Definition 2.3
.

Say a split
X
is
strictly split
if
there exists a composition
\mathcal{Y}\to X
of blowups with smooth
G
-invariant centers,
such that
\mathcal{Y}
is split and
\mathcal{Y}\setminus G
has strict normal crossings.

We can take
\mathcal{Y}=X
if
D
has strict normal crossings.
Split
\mathcal{Y}
help
in the final portion (§
6
) of the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
We do not know if all split
X
are strictly split (over
\mathbb{Q}
).

2.2.
Heights

Let
\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}\colon\mathbb{P}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
be defined by the formula

\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}([y_{0}:\dots:y_{n}])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(y_{0}^{2}+\dots+y_{n}^{2})^{1/2},

for coprime integers
y_{0},\dots,y_{n}
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(1)\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{\mathbb{Q}})

be the
hyperplane bundle
on
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Weil heights
(see
[
35
]
*§B.3)
are only unique up to bounded factors,
so Theorem
1.1
requires reasonable restrictions on
\mathsf{H}
.
For convenience, we impose smoothness, as in
[
10
]
.
1
1

1

However,
a referee pointed out to us that Manin’s conjecture for smooth heights is known to imply
Manin’s conjecture for continuous heights, by
[
43
]
*Proposition 3.3 (or
[
10
]
*Proposition 2.10).
Therefore, while smoothness of
\mathsf{H}
is convenient for proofs,
it is not necessary in the statement of Theorem
1.1
.

Call a function
\Delta\colon X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}

simple
if there exist smooth functions
\Delta_{v}\colon X(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, with
\Delta_{v}=1
at all but finitely many places
v
,
such that

\Delta(x)=\prod_{v}\Delta_{v}(x_{v})\quad\textnormal{for all $x=(x_{v})_{v}\in X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})$}.

For finite
v
,

smooth
means
locally constant
, as in
[
10
]
*§2.1.2.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(X)
.
Call
H_{\mathcal{L}}\colon X(\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}

a
standard Weil height associated to
\mathcal{L}
if there exist

(1)

morphisms
f_{1}\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{i}_{\mathbb{Q}}

and
f_{2}\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{j}_{\mathbb{Q}}
with
i,j\geq 0
;

(2)

an integer
k\geq 1

with
f_{1}^{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{i}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(1))\cong f_{2}^{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{j}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(1))\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\otimes k}
;
and

(3)

a simple function
\Delta\colon X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
;

such that for all
x\in X(\mathbb{Q})
we have

H_{\mathcal{L}}(x)=\Delta(x)\cdot\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{i}}(f_{1}(x))^{1/k}/\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{j}}(f_{2}(x))^{1/k}
.

Remark 2.5
.

The ratio of any two standard Weil heights associated to
\mathcal{L}
is the restriction to
X(\mathbb{Q})
of a simple function
X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

To tackle Theorem
1.1
analytically,
we want local heights
H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},v}
that are roughly inversely proportional to “
v
-adic distance” to
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.
The key constructions and formulas are given below
(following
[
53
]
*§3.2.1
or
[
52
]
*§2,
after tracing through the definitions).

Embed
X
in some projective space
\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
over
\mathbb{Q}
,
with
\mathsf{n}\geq 2
.
Let
\mathscr{X}
be the closure of
X
in
\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Scheme
\mathscr{X}
is integral, and the closure of any prime divisor on
X
is a prime divisor on
\mathscr{X}
.
For each divisor
W\in\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{Z}D_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
let
\mathscr{W}
be the closure of
W
in
\mathscr{X}
.
That is,
let
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
denote the closure of
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
in
\mathscr{X}
,
and define
\mathscr{W}\in\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{Z}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
in general
so that the map
W\mapsto\mathscr{W}
is linear.

The integral model
\mathscr{X}
is smooth over a dense open subset
\mathfrak{o}\subseteq\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Moreover, if
p\in\mathfrak{o}
and
x_{p}\in X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
then
x_{p}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

for every regular open neighborhood
\mathcal{V}

of the point
(x_{p}\bmod{p})\in\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
in
\mathscr{X}
.
Upon considering local equations of divisors on
\mathscr{X}
,
we are led to the following definition,
featuring a certain “distance” quantity
\lvert f(x_{v})\rvert_{v}
.

Definition 2.6
.

Given a divisor
W\in\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{Z}D_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
call a collection of functions

(H_{W,v}\colon G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0})_{v}

good
if there exists a simple function

\Delta\colon X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
for each element
f\in\operatorname{Rat}(\mathscr{X})

and regular open subscheme
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathscr{X}

with
\operatorname{div}(f|_{\mathcal{V}})=\mathscr{W}|_{\mathcal{V}}
,
the following hold:

(1)

For each place
v
and compact set
K_{v}\subseteq\mathcal{V}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,
there exists a smooth function
\varphi\colon K_{v}\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}

such that for all
x_{v}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\cap K_{v}
,
we have

H_{W,v}(x_{v})=\frac{\varphi(x_{v})}{\lvert f(x_{v})\rvert_{v}}.

(2)

For all
v\in\mathfrak{o}
and
x_{v}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\cap\mathcal{V}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
,
we have

H_{W,v}(x_{v})=\frac{\Delta_{v}(x_{v})}{\lvert f(x_{v})\rvert_{v}}.

Remark 2.7
.

If we fix
v\in\mathfrak{o}
and
x_{v}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, and we let
\mathcal{V}
and
f
vary,
then in condition (2),
the value of
\lvert f(x_{v})\rvert_{v}
is independent of the choice of
\mathcal{V}
and
f
,
provided that
x_{v}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
.
But condition (1) is more flexible, since the set
\mathcal{V}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
is in general larger than
\mathcal{V}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
.

Proposition 2.8
.

Let
\mathsf{H}
be a standard Weil height associated to
K_{X}^{-1}
.

(1)

There exists a homomorphism
\operatorname{Pic}(X)\to\operatorname{Fun}(X(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{R}_{>0})
,
assigning each
\mathcal{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(X)
to a standard Weil height
H_{\mathcal{L}}
associated to
\mathcal{L}
,
such that
H_{K_{X}^{-1}}=\mathsf{H}
.

(2)

For each
W\in\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)
,
let
H_{W}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}H_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(W)}
.
There exists a homomorphism
\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)\to\prod_{v}\operatorname{Fun}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{v}),\mathbb{R}_{>0})
,
assigning each
W
to a good collection
(H_{W,v})_{v}
, such that

(2.1)

\displaystyle\lvert a(g_{v})\rvert_{v}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},v}(g_{v})^{-\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)}

for all
g_{v}=(a(g_{v}),b(g_{v}))\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, for all
v
, and

(2.2)

\displaystyle H_{W}(g)

\displaystyle=\prod_{v}H_{W,v}(g)

\displaystyle\textnormal{for all $g\in G(\mathbb{Q})$}.

Proof.

This is all proven in
[
53
]
*first paragraph of §3.2.1,
up to differences in exposition,
so we will be relatively brief.

(1):
Use the fact that
\operatorname{Pic}(X)
is a finite free
\mathbb{Z}
-module.

(2):
Use Proposition
2.1
to choose very ample Weil divisors
W_{1},\dots,W_{\lvert J\rvert-1}\subseteq\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{Z}D_{\mathfrak{j}}
that generate
\operatorname{Pic}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.
For each divisor
W_{l}
(where
1\leq l\leq\lvert J\rvert-1
),
choose sections

\phi^{(l)}_{1},\dots,\phi^{(l)}_{\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))

that globally generate
\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l})
.
If
\phi^{(l)}\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{-1+\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}_{\mathbb{Q}}

denotes the morphism defined by the sections
\phi^{(l)}_{\ast}
,
then by Remark
2.5

for the line bundle
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l})
, we have

\frac{H_{W_{l}}(x)}{\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{-1+\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}}(\phi^{(l)}(x))}=\Delta^{(l)}(x)

for all
x\in X(\mathbb{Q})
,
for some simple function
\Delta^{(l)}\colon X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
But if
x\in G(\mathbb{Q})
,
then
\phi^{(l)}(x)=[\phi^{(l)}_{1}(x):\dots:\phi^{(l)}_{\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}(x)]
,
since
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))\subseteq\Gamma(G,\mathcal{O}_{X})
.
Thus

H_{W_{l}}(x)=\Delta^{(l)}(x)\cdot\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{-1+\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}}([\phi^{(l)}_{1}(x):\dots:\phi^{(l)}_{\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}(x)])

for all
x\in G(\mathbb{Q})
.
Now, let

H_{W_{l},\infty}(x_{\infty})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Delta^{(l)}_{\infty}(x_{\infty})\cdot(\lvert\phi^{(l)}_{1}(x_{\infty})\rvert_{\infty}^{2}+\dots+\lvert\phi^{(l)}_{\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}(x_{\infty})\rvert_{\infty}^{2})^{1/2}

for all
x_{\infty}\in G(\mathbb{R})
,
and for all
x_{p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
let

H_{W_{l},p}(x_{p})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Delta^{(l)}_{p}(x_{p})\cdot\max(\lvert\phi^{(l)}_{1}(x_{p})\rvert_{p},\dots,\lvert\phi^{(l)}_{\dim\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(W_{l}))}(x_{p})\rvert_{p}).

Then in particular, (
2.2
) holds for
W=W_{l}
,
in view of the well-known factorization

\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}([y_{0}:\dots:y_{n}])=(y_{0}^{2}+\dots+y_{n}^{2})^{1/2}\prod_{p}\max(\lvert y_{0}\rvert_{p},\dots,\lvert y_{n}\rvert_{p})

for
(y_{0},\dots,y_{n})\in\mathbb{Q}^{n+1}\setminus\{(0,\dots,0)\}
.

Given
(H_{W_{l},v})_{v}
, the relation (
2.1
) then uniquely specifies a homomorphism
\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)\to\prod_{v}\operatorname{Fun}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{v}),\mathbb{R}_{>0})
,
since
W_{1},\dots,W_{\lvert J\rvert-1},\operatorname{div}(a)
generate
\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.
The resulting
(H_{W,v})_{v}
are good.
Furthermore, by (1), (
2.1
), and the product formula
\prod_{v}\lvert a(g)\rvert_{v}=1
for
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})
,
the relation (
2.2
) extends from
W_{l}
to all
W
,
in view of the formula
\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)/\mathbb{Z}\operatorname{div}(a)
from Proposition
2.1
.
This completes the proof of (2).
∎

From now on, fix heights
H_{\mathcal{L}}
,
(H_{W,v})_{v}
satisfying Proposition
2.8
.

2.3.
Polar combinatorics

For combinatorial purposes,
we make the identification

\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathbb{Z}D_{\mathfrak{j}}=\mathbb{Z}^{J},

where
D_{\mathfrak{j}}\in\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)
is identified with
the
\mathfrak{j}
th basis vector of
\mathbb{Z}^{J}
,
for each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
.
Moreover, for any subset
I\subseteq J
, we identify
\mathbb{Z}^{I}
with
the subspace
\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\mathbb{Z}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{J}
.
We make similar identifications over
\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{C}
,
so that for instance,
\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)\otimes\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{C}^{J}
.
Finally, for each vector
\bm{z}=(z_{\mathfrak{j}})_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\in\mathbb{C}^{J}
,
let
\Re(\bm{z})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\Re(z_{\mathfrak{j}}))_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}

and let
\bm{z}|_{I}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(z_{\mathfrak{j}})_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\in\mathbb{C}^{I}
.

Let
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
with
A_{1}<A_{2}
,
and let
\operatorname{Int}(A_{1},A_{2})\subseteq\mathbb{R}
denote the open
interval from
A_{1}
to
A_{2}
.
For a holomorphic function
\varphi(\bm{z})

on the region
\Re(\bm{z})\in\operatorname{Int}(A_{1},A_{2})^{J}
,
we define the
shifted integral

(2.3)

\mathscr{S}(\varphi)(\bm{z})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(\bm{z}+it\operatorname{div}(a))\,dt.

The main term in the asymptotic (
1.3
) will come from
such integrals,
whose general structure we now describe
in terms of the combinatorics of the vector
\operatorname{div}(a)\in\mathbb{Z}^{J}
.

Recall (
1.4
).
Let
J_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{j}\in J:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)>0\}

and
J_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{j}\in J:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)<0\}
,
and let

J_{3}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{j}\in J:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)=0\}\subseteq\{\mathfrak{j}\in J:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b)<0\}.

Clearly
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
,
J_{3}
partition
J
.
For convenience, let
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)
for each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
.

The
\mathcal{X}
-function framework of
[
8
]
*§3 is crucial
for studying (
2.3
).
For each
I\subseteq J
, let

\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}}(\bm{z})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}}e^{-\bm{y}\cdot\bm{z}|_{I}}\,d\bm{y}=\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}z_{\mathfrak{j}}^{-1}\quad\textnormal{for $\Re(\bm{z})\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{J}$},

with meromorphic continuation to all
\bm{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{J}
.
We have
\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{I}
.
Consider the cone

\Lambda_{I}(X)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{I}/(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}).

Let
\mu_{I}
be the quotient measure on
\mathbb{R}^{I}/(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I})

induced by assigning unit length to the vector
\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}\in\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}
.
The dual cone
\Lambda^{\ast}_{I}(X)
of
\Lambda_{I}(X)
is
the set of elements
\varkappa\in(\mathbb{R}^{I})^{\ast}=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{R}^{I},\mathbb{R})

such that
(\varkappa(D_{\mathfrak{j}}))_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}

and
\varkappa(\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I})=0
.
Let

(2.4)

\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}(\bm{z})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\Lambda^{\ast}_{I}(X)}e^{-\varkappa(\bm{z}|_{I})}\,d\varkappa\quad\textnormal{for $\Re(\bm{z})\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{J}+\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)$},

where
d\varkappa
is the Haar measure on
(\mathbb{R}^{I}/(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}))^{\ast}

such that
d\varkappa
is dual to the measure
\mu_{I}
.
The function
\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}
is
\mathbb{C}\operatorname{div}(a)
-invariant
,
i.e. it is invariant under translation by
\mathbb{C}\operatorname{div}(a)
.

Remark 2.9
.

If
\gcd_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}(\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})=1
,
then
d\varkappa
is the unique Haar measure on
(\mathbb{R}^{I}/(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}))^{\ast}

such that the dual lattice
(\mathbb{Z}^{I}/(\mathbb{Z}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}))^{\ast}
has covolume
1
.
In particular, this applies if
I=J
,
because
\gcd_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}(\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})=1

(since
\operatorname{Pic}(X)
is free
and
\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\mathbb{Z}^{J}/\mathbb{Z}\operatorname{div}(a)
).

Remark 2.10
.

In some degenerate cases,
\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}
vanishes identically.
For example,
if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 2

and
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}\cap(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I})\neq 0
,
then all the nonzero coordinates of
\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I}
are of the same sign
(and the number of nonzero coordinates is
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 2
),
so

\dim(\Lambda^{\ast}_{I}(X))\leq\lvert I\rvert-2<\lvert I\rvert-1=\dim(\mathbb{R}^{I}/(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I})),

whence
\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}=0
.

Let
\lVert\bm{z}\rVert\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\max_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}{\lvert z_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{J}(A_{1},A_{2})
be the ring of holomorphic functions
\varphi(\bm{z})
on
\Re(\bm{z})\in\operatorname{Int}(A_{1},A_{2})^{J}
that are
polynomially bounded in vertical strips
,
meaning that for each compact set
K\subseteq\operatorname{Int}(A_{1},A_{2})
,
there exists a real
A=A_{3}(K)>0
such that

\varphi(\bm{z})\ll_{K}(1+\lVert\bm{z}\rVert)^{A}\quad\textnormal{for $\Re(\bm{z})\in K^{J}$}.

Let
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}(A_{1},A_{2})
be the ring of
\mathbb{C}\operatorname{div}(a)
-invariant functions in
\mathcal{H}_{J}(A_{1},A_{2})
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}(A_{1},A_{2})
be the set of functions
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{J}(A_{1},A_{2})

such that for each compact set
K\subseteq\operatorname{Int}(A_{1},A_{2})
,
there exists a real
A=A_{4}(K)>0
such that

(2.5)

\varphi(\bm{z}+it\operatorname{div}(a))\ll_{K}(1+\lVert\bm{z}\rVert)^{A}(1+t^{2})^{-1}\quad\textnormal{for $(\Re(\bm{z}),t)\in K^{J}\times\mathbb{R}$}.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{J}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{\delta>0}\mathcal{H}_{J}(-\delta,\delta)
,
and similarly define
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}
be the set of functions
\digamma(\bm{z})

(meromorphic on a neighborhood of the line
\Re(\bm{z})=\bm{0}
)
such that

\digamma(\bm{z})\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\frac{z_{\mathfrak{j}}}{1+z_{\mathfrak{j}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}.

Both
\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}

are
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}
-modules.

The following lemma expresses
\mathscr{S}(\digamma)
in terms of
\mathcal{X}
-functions, for any
\digamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}
(in the spirit of
[
11
]
*(3.5.2), which builds on
[
8
]
*Théorème 3.1.14).
For a set
I\subseteq J
,
let

\mathbf{g}_{I}(\bm{z})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}}(\bm{z})=\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}z_{\mathfrak{j}}^{-1}

if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\neq 1
,
and if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert=1
then let

\mathbf{g}_{I}(\bm{z})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}}(\bm{z})}{\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}(1+z_{\mathfrak{j}})}=\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\frac{1}{z_{\mathfrak{j}}(1+z_{\mathfrak{j}})}.

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
P\subseteq J
be a set.
Suppose
\digamma\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}

and

\digamma(\bm{z})\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in P}\frac{z_{\mathfrak{j}}}{1+z_{\mathfrak{j}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}.

(1)

There exists a vector
(h_{I})_{I\subseteq P}\in\prod_{I\subseteq P}\mathcal{H}_{J}
,
with
h_{I}\in\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}
for
I\subseteq J_{3}

and
h_{I}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}

for all other
I
,
such that we have an identity of meromorphic functions

(2.6)

\digamma=\sum_{I\subseteq P}h_{I}\mathbf{g}_{I}

(on a neighborhood of the line
\Re(\bm{z})=\bm{0}
).

(2)

We have
h_{P}(\bm{0})=(\digamma\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in P}z_{\mathfrak{j}})(\bm{0})

and

\mathscr{S}(\digamma)=\sum_{I\subseteq P:\,\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 1}h_{I}\mathscr{S}(\mathbf{g}_{I})+\sum_{I\subseteq P\cap J_{3}}\mathbf{g}_{I}\mathscr{S}(h_{I}).

(3)

We have
\mathscr{S}(\mathbf{g}_{I})=0

if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert=1
,
and
\mathscr{S}(\mathbf{g}_{I})=\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}

if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 2
.

(4)

We have

\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}(A_{1},A_{2}))\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}(A_{1},A_{2}).

That is, for any function
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}(A_{1},A_{2})
,
we have
\mathscr{S}(\varphi)\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}(A_{1},A_{2})
.

Proof.

(1):
Let
2^{P}
denote the set of all subsets
I\subseteq P
.
We construct
(h_{I})_{I\subseteq P}
by the following recursive algorithm:

(1)

Let
h_{P}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\digamma/\mathbf{g}_{P}\in\mathcal{H}_{J}
, and
h_{I}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0\in\mathcal{H}_{J}
for all
I\in 2^{P}\setminus\{P\}
;
and let
\mathscr{I}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}2^{P}
.
Note that
h_{I}\mathbf{g}_{I}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}
for all
I\in 2^{P}
; we will maintain this property at each step.

(2)

If
\mathscr{I}=\emptyset
, terminate the algorithm.
Otherwise, choose an element
I_{\star}\in\mathscr{I}
of maximal cardinality.

(3)

Suppose first that
\lvert I_{\star}\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 1
.
Choose an element
i_{\star}\in I_{\star}\setminus J_{3}
,
and replace the functions
h_{I_{\star}},h_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}\in\mathcal{H}_{J}
with, respectively,

h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}}((z_{\mathfrak{j}})_{\mathfrak{j}\in J})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}h_{I_{\star}}((z_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}z_{i_{\star}}/\mathsf{u}_{i_{\star}})_{\mathfrak{j}\in J})\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}

and
h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}h_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}+(h_{I_{\star}}-h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}})\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}/\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}\in\mathcal{H}_{J}
.
2
2

2

One can use Cauchy’s integral formula to prove
(h_{I_{\star}}-h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}})/z_{i_{\star}}\in\mathcal{H}_{J}
,
which implies
h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}\in\mathcal{H}_{J}
.

Note that
\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}
, so

h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}}\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J},\quad h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}=h_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}\setminus\{i_{\star}\}}+h_{I_{\star}}\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}-h^{\prime}_{I_{\star}}\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}.

Now replace
\mathscr{I}
with
\mathscr{I}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{I}\setminus\{I_{\star}\}
, and go back to step (b).

(4)

Suppose instead that
I_{\star}\subseteq J_{3}
.
Then
1/\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}
, which together with
h_{I_{\star}}\mathbf{g}_{I_{\star}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}
implies
h_{I_{\star}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}\cap\mathcal{H}_{J}=\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}
.
3
3

3

One can use Cauchy’s integral formula to show that
\mathcal{M}_{\dagger,J}\cap\mathcal{H}_{J}=\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}
.

Replace
\mathscr{I}
with
\mathscr{I}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{I}\setminus\{I_{\star}\}
, and repeat (b).

The algorithm terminates after
1+\lvert 2^{P}\rvert
occurrences of (b).
For the final output
(h_{I})_{I\subseteq P}
,
the conditions of (1) hold by inspection of (c) and (d).

(2):
For the first part, multiply (
2.6
) by
\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in P}z_{\mathfrak{j}}
and plug in
\bm{z}=\bm{0}
.
For the second part, use the
\mathbb{C}\operatorname{div}(a)
-invariance of
h_{I}
when
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 1
, and of
\mathbf{g}_{I}
when
I\subseteq J_{3}
.

(3):
This follows from
[
8
]
*Proposition 3.1.9
if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert\geq 2
and
\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}\neq 0

(in which case
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{I}\cap(\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)|_{I})=0
,
by Remark
2.10
),
and from the identity

(2.7)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{dt}{\prod_{1\leq k\leq r}(z^{\prime}_{k}+it)}=0\quad\textnormal{for $r\geq 2$ and $\Re(z^{\prime}_{k})>0$}

if
\lvert I\setminus J_{3}\rvert=1
or
\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}=0
.
(To prove (
2.7
),
write
s=it
to view the integral over
\mathbb{R}

as a contour integral over a vertical line
\Re(s)=0
in
\mathbb{C}
,
and shift the contour to
\Re(s)\to\infty
.)

(4):
This follows from (
2.3
)
and (
2.5
),
since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+t^{2})^{-1}\,dt\ll 1
.
∎

2.4.
Peyre’s constant

Cf.
[
43
,
45
]
.
First,

\alpha_{\textnormal{Pey}}(X)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{Pic}(X)}(K_{X}^{-1})=\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{J}(X)}(K_{X}^{-1});

by Remark
2.9
, the measure
d\varkappa
in
\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{J}(X)}
is correctly normalized in terms of
\operatorname{Pic}(X)
.
Here
\alpha_{\textnormal{Pey}}(X)>0
by Proposition
2.2
.
Second,
\beta_{\textnormal{Pey}}(X)=1
since
X
is rational; see e.g.
[
53
]
*two lines before Theorem 48.
Let

\lvert\omega\rvert_{v}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lvert a_{v}\rvert_{v}^{-1}\,da_{v}\,db_{v}

be the measure on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
associated to
the top form
\omega=a^{-1}\,da\,db
.
Then

\int_{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\lvert\omega\rvert_{p}=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\lvert a_{p}\rvert_{p}^{-1}\,da_{p}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}db_{p}=\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}da_{p}=1-p^{-1}

for any prime
p
,
where the
\mathscr{G}
is the obvious integral model of
G
over
\mathbb{Z}
.
(As a scheme,
\mathscr{G}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.)
Finally,
define the local Tamagawa measure

(2.8)

d\tau_{v}=d\tau_{X,\mathsf{H},v}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}H_{\operatorname{div}(\omega),v}\cdot\lvert\omega\rvert_{v}

on
X(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
;
initially the formula (
2.8
) only makes sense on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, but it extends to
X(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
by Definition
2.6
.
Let
L(s,\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))
be the Artin
L
-function associated to the representation
\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})\otimes\mathbb{C}
of
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
.
It is known that
L(s,\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))
converges absolutely on
\Re(s)>1
, is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}
, and has a pole of order
\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))\geq 1
at
s=1
;
let

L^{\ast}(1,\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lim_{s\to 1}{\frac{L(s,\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))}{(s-1)^{\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))}}}.

Define on
X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
the measure

d\tau\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L^{\ast}(1,\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))\,d\tau_{\infty}\prod_{p}\frac{d\tau_{p}}{L_{p}(1,\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}))}.

It is known that
d\tau

(unlike the local measures
d\tau_{v}
)
depends only on
X
and
H_{K_{X}^{-1}}=\mathsf{H}
,
and not on the top form
\omega
or the local heights
H_{\operatorname{div}(\omega),v}
.
Let

\tau(X,\mathsf{H})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{X(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}d\tau,

which (by the Weil conjectures) factors as an absolutely convergent Euler product;
see e.g.
[
10
]
*Corollary 2.4 and Theorem 2.5.
Peyre’s constant is

(2.9)

\mathcal{A}_{X,\mathsf{H}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{\alpha_{\textnormal{Pey}}(X)\tau(X,\mathsf{H})}{(\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))-1)!}>0.

2.5.
Spectral expansion

For
\bm{s}=\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}s_{\mathfrak{j}}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\in\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)\otimes\mathbb{C}

and
g=(g_{v})_{v}\in G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, let

H_{v}(\bm{s},g_{v})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},v}(g_{v})^{s_{\mathfrak{j}}},\quad H(\bm{s},g)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{v}H_{v}(\bm{s},g_{v}),\quad\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q})}H(\bm{s},g^{\prime}g)^{-1}.

For each prime
p
, choose a maximal open subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{p}
of
\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
such that
H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}
is right
\mathbf{K}_{p}
-invariant for all
\mathfrak{j}\in J
;
then
\mathbf{K}_{p}=\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for all but finitely many
p
.
Let
\mathbf{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{p}\mathbf{K}_{p}
.
Following
[
52
]
*§5, we will decompose
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)

using the automorphic machinery of
[
52
]
*§3.
Since we work more generally than
[
52
]
*§5, it seems appropriate to provide some details;
but the key formula (
2.31
) is not new (only our subsequent analysis of it is).

Let
dg=\prod_{v}dg_{v}
be the measure on
G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
given by

dg_{\infty}=\lvert\omega\rvert_{\infty}

and
dg_{p}=\lvert\omega\rvert_{p}/(1-p^{-1})
.
Let
d^{\times}{a}=\prod_{v}d^{\times}{a_{v}}

be the Haar measure on
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}

with
d^{\times}{a_{\infty}}=a_{\infty}/\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}

and
\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}d^{\times}{a_{p}}=1
.
Then
dg=d^{\times}{a}\,db

and
\int_{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}dg_{p}=1
.
Also, for
p
sufficiently large,
Definition
2.6
implies

(2.10)

H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})=1\quad\textnormal{for all $g_{p}\in\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})$},

whence
\int_{\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})^{-1}\,dg_{p}=1
.

For the rest of §
2
, let

A_{5}>0
be a large real constant
(depending on
\operatorname{div}(a)
and
\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(b)
).
If
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}

and
g\in G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, then

(2.11)

\begin{split}\lvert H(\bm{s},g)\rvert&\gg H(\operatorname{div}_{0}(a)+\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(a)+\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(b),g)^{A_{5}^{1/2}}\\
&\gg\prod_{v}\max(1,\lvert a(g_{v})\rvert_{v}^{-1},\lvert a(g_{v})\rvert_{v},\lvert b(g_{v})\rvert_{v})^{A_{5}^{1/2}},\end{split}

by Definition
2.6
;
cf.
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.2.
For unitary characters
\chi
of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
, let

H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}H(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\overline{\chi}(\det(g))\,dg,\quad H^{\ast}_{v}(\bm{s},\chi)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}H_{v}(\bm{s},g_{v})^{-1}\overline{\chi}_{v}(\det(g_{v}))\,dg_{v}.

If
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}
, then by (
2.11
), we have
H^{\ast}_{v}(\bm{s},\chi)\ll 1
for all
v
, and
H^{\ast}_{p}(\bm{s},\chi)=1+O(p^{-2})
for all
p
large enough in terms of the conductor of
\chi
;
so
H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)=\prod_{v}H^{\ast}_{v}(\bm{s},\chi)\ll_{\chi}1
.

For the rest of §
2
, assume
X
is split.
(Splitness simplifies many formulas.)

Lemma 2.12
.

Let
\delta>0
be small.
Then each of the following implies the next:

(1)

The divisor
D
has strict normal crossings (see §
1.1
).

(2)

Let
\Re(\bm{s}+\operatorname{div}(\omega))\in[-\delta,\delta^{-1}]^{J}
.
Then for each
v
, the integral
H^{\ast}_{v}(\bm{s},1)
converges absolutely uniformly over
\bm{s}
.
Moreover,
H^{\ast}_{p}(\bm{s},1)/\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\zeta_{p}(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)=1+O(p^{-1-\delta})
.

(3)

We have
\lim_{\bm{s}\to-\operatorname{div}(\omega)}{H^{\ast}(\bm{s},1)\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}=\tau(X,\mathsf{H})
.

Proof.

(1)
\Rightarrow
(2):
See
[
10
]
*§4.3.2 (or Lemma 4.1) for the convergence of individual integrals,
and
[
10
]
*§4.3.3 with
\mathscr{B}=\emptyset
(based on a formula of Denef) for the asymptotic.

(2)
\Rightarrow
(3):
Write
H^{\ast}=\prod_{v}H^{\ast}_{v}
;
switch
\lim
,
\prod
using (2).
Then express
\tau(X,\mathsf{H})
in terms of
H^{\ast}_{v}
via (
2.8
).
Cf.
[
9
]
*proof of Proposition 6.2 or
[
10
]
*proof of Proposition 4.10.
∎

For the rest of §
2
, assume
D
has strict normal crossings.
(This condition makes some technical calculations more uniform.)
To decompose
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)
requires not just
H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)
but other integral transforms as well, which we now define alongside other relevant notions.

First, let
\mathsf{S}\subseteq\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
be a finite set such that the following hold:

(1)

If
p\notin\mathsf{S}
and
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, then
H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}
satisfies Definition
2.6
with
\Delta_{p}=1
.

(2)

If
p\notin\mathsf{S}
and
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, then
H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}
is
\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-invariant (on the right), i.e.
\mathbf{K}_{p}=\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

(3)

If
I\subseteq J
, then
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
is smooth over
\mathbb{Z}[1/\mathsf{S}]
, the ring of
\mathsf{S}
-integers.
Here
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
denotes the closure of
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
in
\mathscr{X}
,
and for
I=\emptyset
we let
\bigcap_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{X}
.

(We can arrange for (3) to hold since
X
is smooth and
D
has strict normal crossings.)

For
p\in\mathsf{S}
, let
\mathsf{r}_{p}(\mathbf{K})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\min{\{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}:(1+p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subseteq\mathbf{K}_{p}\}}
.
Let
\mathsf{N}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{p\in\mathsf{S}}p^{\mathsf{r}_{p}(\mathbf{K})}
, and let
\mathbf{M}
be the set of characters

(2.12)

\lambda\colon(\mathbb{R}^{\times}/(\mathbb{R}^{\times})^{2})\times\prod_{p\in\mathsf{S}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/(1+p^{\mathsf{r}_{p}(\mathbf{K})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}.

For
a^{\prime}=(a^{\prime}_{v})_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,
write
\lambda(a^{\prime})=\prod_{v}\lambda_{v}(a^{\prime}_{v})
,
where
\lambda_{\infty}\colon\mathbb{R}^{\times}/(\mathbb{R}^{\times})^{2}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}

and
\lambda_{p}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
are characters
such that
\lambda_{p}=1
for all
p\notin\mathsf{S}
.
For
a_{p}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
,
let
\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(a_{p})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}a_{p}/p^{v_{p}(a_{p})}
.
Also, for
a=(a_{v})_{v}\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
,
let
\lambda_{\mathsf{S}}(a)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{p}\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(a_{p})
, where

(2.13)

\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(a_{p})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{p^{\prime}\neq p}\lambda_{p^{\prime}}(p^{v_{p}(a_{p})})=\prod_{p^{\prime}\in\mathsf{S}\setminus\{p\}}\lambda_{p^{\prime}}(p^{v_{p}(a_{p})}).

We are now prepared to define the key components
\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(g)

of a non-abelian Fourier transform on
G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.
For
(m,\lambda,t)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\times\mathbf{M}\times\mathbb{R}

and
g=(a,b)\in G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, let

(2.14)

\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(g)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\psi(\alpha b)\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(\alpha a)\,\lvert\alpha a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{it},

where
\psi
is the additive character from §
1.1
,
and where for
a=(a_{v})_{v}\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
we let

(2.15)

\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lambda_{\infty}(a_{\infty})\prod_{p}\lambda_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(a_{p}))\bm{1}_{\mathsf{N}a_{p}/m\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}.

By additive reciprocity (
\psi(\mathbb{Q})=1
),
the function
\theta_{m,\lambda,t}
is left
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant.
Furthermore,
\lvert\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(g)\rvert\leq 2
for all
g
,
since the sum in (
2.14
) is supported on

\alpha=\pm\prod_{p}p^{v_{p}(m/\mathsf{N}a_{p})}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.
Moreover, at
1_{G}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(1,0)
we have

\begin{split}\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(1_{G})&=\sum_{\alpha=\pm m/\mathsf{N}}\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(\alpha)\,\lvert\alpha\rvert_{\infty}^{it}\\
&=\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{\infty}^{it}(1+\lambda(-1))\prod_{p}\lambda_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(m/\mathsf{N}))\\
&=\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{\infty}^{it}(1+\lambda(-1))\prod_{p}\prod_{p^{\prime}\neq p}\lambda_{p}((p^{\prime})^{v_{p^{\prime}}(m/\mathsf{N})})\\
&=\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{\infty}^{it}(1+\lambda(-1))\prod_{p^{\prime}}\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p^{\prime}}(m/\mathsf{N}),\end{split}

by the definition of
\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p^{\prime}}
.
By the definition of
\lambda_{\mathsf{S}}
, it follows that

(2.16)

\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(1_{G})=\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{\infty}^{it}(1+\lambda(-1))\lambda_{\mathsf{S}}(m/\mathsf{N})=\frac{(1+\lambda(-1))\lambda_{\mathsf{S}}(m/\mathsf{N})}{\prod_{p}\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{p}^{it}},

where the last step follows from the product formula

\prod_{v}\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{v}^{it}=1
.
Let

(2.17)

H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}H(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\overline{\theta}_{m,\lambda,t}(g)\,dg.

For
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
,
define
H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)
to be

(2.18)

\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(-\alpha b_{p}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\overline{\lambda}_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(\alpha a_{p}))\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(\alpha a_{p})\lvert a_{p}\rvert_{p}^{-it}\,dg_{p}.

Let
H^{\vee}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{G(\mathbb{R})}H_{\infty}(\bm{s},g_{\infty})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(\alpha b_{\infty})\lambda_{\infty}(\alpha a_{\infty})\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,dg_{\infty}
.
(Note that
\lambda_{\infty}=\overline{\lambda}_{\infty}
.)

Let
H^{\prime}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}H^{\vee}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)
.
For
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, define
H^{\prime}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,m,\alpha)
to be

(2.19)

\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(-\alpha b_{p}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\overline{\lambda}_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(\alpha a_{p}))\,dg_{p}.

Before proceeding, we need some foundational bounds.
Let
\lVert\bm{s}\rVert\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\max_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}{\lvert s_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}
.

Proposition 2.13

(Cf.
[
52
]
*Lemma 5.2)
.

Let
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}
and
g\in G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Then
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)
converges absolutely to a bounded function of
(\bm{s},g)
, holomorphic in
\bm{s}
and smooth in
g
.
Also,
H(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\in L^{1}(G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))
and
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)\in L^{q}(G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))^{\mathbf{K}}
for all
\bm{s}
, for all
q\in[1,\infty]
.

Proof.

Except for smoothness in
g
, this follows from (
2.11
) as in
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.2.
For smoothness in
g
, use
\mathbf{K}
-invariance at finite places,
and at the infinite place use the fact that
for every composition
\mathcal{T}
of operators in
\{a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial a},a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}\}
, we have

(2.20)

\mathcal{T}(H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},\infty}^{-1})\ll_{\mathcal{T}}H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},\infty}^{-1},

thanks to Lemma
5.1
(2)
(or
[
53
]
*proof of Lemma 5.9),
together with left invariance of
\mathcal{T}
,
i.e. the property
\mathcal{T}(f(g^{\prime}g))=(\mathcal{T}{f})(g^{\prime}g)
for
g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{R})
.
∎

Lemma 2.14
.

Suppose
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}
and
\alpha,t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then the following hold:

(1)

For
\varsigma\in\{\pm 1\}
, we have

\int_{G(\mathbb{R}):\,\operatorname{sgn}(a_{\infty})=\varsigma}H_{\infty}(\bm{s},g_{\infty})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(\alpha b_{\infty})\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,dg_{\infty}\ll\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{6}}{(1+\alpha^{4})(1+t^{2})}.

(2)

We have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\times}}\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{\infty}(\bm{s},g_{\infty})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(\alpha b_{\infty})\,db_{\infty}\rvert\,d^{\times}{a_{\infty}}\ll(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{4})/(1+\alpha^{4})
.

Proof.

For (1) and (2), integrate by parts over
b_{\infty}
four times if
\lvert\alpha\rvert\geq 1
.
For (1), further integrate by parts over
\log{\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert}
twice if
\lvert t\rvert\geq 1
.
To bound derivatives, use (
2.20
);
to bound
H^{-1}
itself, use (
2.11
) and the largeness of
A_{5}
.
∎

Lemma 2.15
.

Suppose
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}
and
(m,\alpha)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\times\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.
Then the following hold:

(1)

We have
H^{\prime}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,m,\alpha)=\bm{1}_{v_{p}(m)=v_{p}(\alpha)}+O((p+\lvert m/\alpha\rvert_{p}+\lvert\alpha/m\rvert_{p})^{-4})
.

(2)

We have
\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})\rvert^{-1}\,dg_{p}=\bm{1}_{v_{p}(\alpha)\geq 0}+O((p+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-4})
.

Proof.

Use (
2.11
) and the fact (
2.10
) for large
p
.
∎

Lemma 2.16
.

Suppose
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then the following hold:

(1)

We have

{\textstyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\sum_{m\geq 1}\lvert H^{\prime}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\prod_{p}H^{\prime}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,m,\alpha)\rvert\ll(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{6})/(1+t^{2}).}

(2)

We have

{\textstyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\times}}\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{\infty}(\bm{s},g_{\infty})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(\alpha b_{\infty})\,db_{\infty}\rvert\,d^{\times}{a_{\infty}})\prod_{p}(\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})\rvert^{-1}\,dg_{p})<\infty.}

Proof.

(1):
Let
\alpha^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\alpha/m
.
Now plug in Lemmas
2.14
(1)
and
2.15
(1).
It then suffices to prove
\sum_{m\geq 1}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}=r_{1}/r_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}(1+(m\alpha^{\prime})^{4})^{-1}\cdot\lvert r_{1}r_{2}\rvert^{\epsilon-4}<\infty
, where we write
\alpha^{\prime}=r_{1}/r_{2}
in lowest terms (with
r_{2}\geq 1
).
But
(1+(m\alpha^{\prime})^{4})\cdot(r_{1}r_{2})^{4}\geq(r_{2}^{4}+m^{4})\cdot r_{1}^{4}
, which suffices.

(2):
Use Lemmas
2.14
(2) and
2.15
(2), and the bound
\sum_{\alpha=r_{1}/r_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}(1+\alpha^{4})^{-1}\lvert r_{2}\rvert^{\epsilon-4}<\infty
.
∎

For the rest of §
2
, assume
\Re(\bm{s})\in[A_{5},2A_{5}]^{J}
.
We will decompose
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)
into two pieces,
based on an averaging process over
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
using the short exact sequence of
pointed sets

(noting that
G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
is not actually a group)

(2.21)

1\to\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{b\mapsto(1,b)}G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\xrightarrow{\det\colon(a,b)\mapsto a}\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\to 1,

where the map
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})

is well-defined since
(1,c+b)=(1,c)(1,b)

for all
b\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
c\in\mathbb{Q}
.
In addition, if
\phi\in C(G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))
,
then
\phi(a,b)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-translation invariant in the
b
entry,
thanks to the translation identity
(1,c)(a,b)=(a,c+b)
for
c\in\mathbb{Q}
.
For
g\in G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, following
[
53
]
*§3.2.2
(or
[
52
]
*Lemma 3.3), we now let

(2.22)

\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},g)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\det(g),b^{\prime}))\,db^{\prime},

where
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\det(g),b^{\prime}))
is well-defined
because
\mathsf{Z}
is left
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant.
For any
g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
,
the scaling identity
(\det(g^{\prime}),1)(\det(g),b^{\prime})=(\det(g^{\prime}g),b^{\prime}\det(g^{\prime}))
implies that

\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},g)=\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\det(g^{\prime}g),b^{\prime}\det(g^{\prime})))\,db^{\prime}=\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\det(g^{\prime}g),b^{\prime}))\,db^{\prime}=\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},g^{\prime}g),

since
\mathsf{Z}
is left
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant
and multiplication by
\det(g^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Q}

on the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}

is measure-preserving.
Thus
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
is left
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant.
In fact,
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
is also right
\mathbf{K}
-invariant,
because if
k=(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathbf{K}
,
then the identity
(\det(g),b^{\prime})k=(\det(gk),\det(g)k_{2}+b^{\prime})

and right
\mathbf{K}
-invariance of
\mathsf{Z}
imply that

\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},g)=\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\det(g),b^{\prime})k)\,db^{\prime}=\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\det(gk),b^{\prime}))\,db^{\prime}=\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},gk).

We have
\mathsf{Z}_{0}\in(C^{\infty}\cap L^{\infty})(G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))
,
because
\mathsf{Z}\in(C^{\infty}\cap L^{\infty})(G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))

(by Proposition
2.13
)
and
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is compact.
Also,
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
is left
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
-invariant, and thus descends to a (right)
\det(\mathbf{K})
-invariant function of
\det(g)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
,
where
\det(\mathbf{K})
denotes the image of
\mathbf{K}

under the map
\det\colon G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\to\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
.
Fourier analysis on
C(\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times})^{\det(\mathbf{K})}
,
as in
[
53
]
*§3.2.2,
gives

(2.23)

\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},g)=\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(\det(g),0))=\int H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)\chi(\det(g))\,d\chi,

where
\chi
runs over characters of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
lying in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times})^{\det(\mathbf{K})}
.
Before defining
d\chi
and justifying (
2.23
),
we first explain why
H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)
is a Fourier coefficient of
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
.
By Proposition
2.13
,

H(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\in L^{1}(G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))
,
so Fubini’s theorem allows us to
rewrite the integral
H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)
,
by folding
G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
first over
G(\mathbb{Q})

and then over
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
as follows:

\begin{split}H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)=\int_{G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}H(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\overline{\chi}(\det(g))\,dg&=\int_{G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\sum_{g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q})}H(\bm{s},g^{\prime}g)^{-1}\overline{\chi}(\det(g))\,dg\\
&=\int_{G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)\overline{\chi}(\det(g))\,dg\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\chi}(a)\,db\,d^{\times}{a}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(a,0))\overline{\chi}(a)\,d^{\times}{a},\end{split}

by (
2.22
),
where
\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\chi}(a)\,db
is viewed as a
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
-invariant function of
a\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
,
even though
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))
is not
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
-invariant.
This is what we wanted.

The characters
\chi
in (
2.23
) are of the form
\chi_{0}\,\lvert\cdot\rvert^{it}
with
(\chi_{0},t)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
\mathcal{U}
is a finite group, because we work over
\mathbb{Q}
.
(Over number fields, the story is more complicated
and we refer the interested reader to
[
53
]
*Lemma 47.)
The measure
d\chi
is the product of the counting measure on
\mathcal{U}

with the measure
\frac{dt}{2\pi}
on
\mathbb{R}
.
The Fourier expansion (
2.23
) is justified, since

\mathsf{Z}_{0}\in L^{1}
by Proposition
2.13
,
and
\int\lvert H^{\ast}(\bm{s},\chi)\rvert\,d\chi<\infty
by Lemma
2.14
(1) with
\alpha=0
.

Proposition 2.17
.

Fix
I\subseteq J

with
I\cap J_{1}\neq\emptyset
and
I\cap J_{2}\neq\emptyset
.
Fix a vector

\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{\mathfrak{j}})_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\in-\operatorname{div}(\omega)+\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J\setminus I}\mathbb{R}_{>0}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\subseteq\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)\otimes\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{R}^{J}.

Then
(s-1)^{\lvert I\rvert-1}\mathsf{Z}_{0}(s\bm{\kappa},1_{G})
is holomorphic, with at most polynomial growth in vertical strips, on
\Re(s)\geq 1-\delta
.
Also,

\lim_{s\to 1}{(s-1)^{\lvert I\rvert-1}\mathsf{Z}_{0}(s\bm{\kappa},1_{G})}=\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{I}(X)}(\bm{\kappa})\lim_{\bm{s}^{\prime}\to\bm{\kappa}}{H^{\ast}(\bm{s}^{\prime},1)\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in I}(s^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\kappa_{\mathfrak{j}})}.

Proof.

Use (
2.23
), Lemma
2.12
(1)
\Rightarrow
(2),
and Lemma
2.11
with
P=I

and

\digamma(\bm{z})=H^{\ast}(\bm{z}+\bm{\kappa},\chi_{0}),

where
\chi_{0}\in\mathcal{U}
.
Then specialize to
\bm{z}=(s-1)\bm{\kappa}
and let
s\to 1
.
The main contribution to
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
comes from
\chi_{0}=1
.
Cf.
[
52
]
*proof of Theorem 2.1 or
[
53
]
*proof of Theorem 48.
∎

Proposition
2.17
and Lemma
2.12
give a satisfactory understanding of
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
for us.
It remains to discuss
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{Z}-\mathsf{Z}_{0}
.
We follow
[
52
]
*proofs of Lemma 5.3 and Proposition 5.4.
Clearly
\mathsf{Z}_{1}
is left
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant,
since
\mathsf{Z}
and
\mathsf{Z}_{0}
are.
For
a\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
, let

(2.24)

\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\psi}(b)\,db,

where the quantities
\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,b))

and
\psi(b)
(the character from §
1.1
)
are well-defined.
We have
\mathsf{h}\in(C^{\infty}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times})
, since
\mathsf{Z}_{1}=\mathsf{Z}-\mathsf{Z}_{0}\in(C^{\infty}\cap L^{\infty})(G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))
and
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is compact.
Since
\operatorname{vol}(\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})=1
,
and
\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(a,b))
is independent of
b
,
we have

\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(a,b^{\prime}))\,db^{\prime}=\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(a,b))=\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b^{\prime}))\,db^{\prime}

by (
2.22
),
whence
\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,b))\,db=0
.
By Fourier expansion of
\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,b))
in
b\in\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
as in
[
52
]
*proof of
\Theta I=1

in the final display of the proof of Lemma 3.4,
we find that

\begin{split}\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,b))&=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,b+y))\overline{\psi}(\alpha y)\,dy\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(a,y))\overline{\psi}(\alpha(y-b))\,dy\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\psi(\alpha b)\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},(\alpha a,\alpha y))\overline{\psi}(\alpha y)\,dy,\end{split}

by the scaling identity
(\alpha,0)(a,b)=(\alpha a,\alpha b)
for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.
Multiplication by
\alpha
on
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is measure-preserving,
so by (
2.24
),
we conclude that

(2.25)

\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},g)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\psi(\alpha b)\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},\alpha a).

But
\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\psi}(b)\,db=\mathsf{Z}_{0}(\bm{s},(a,0))\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{\psi}(b)\,db=0
, so (
2.24
) simplifies to

(2.26)

\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)=\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\psi}(b)\,db.

The following lemma is essentially

[
52
]
*first paragraph of the proof of Lemma 5.3:

Lemma 2.18
.

We have
\mathsf{h}\in L^{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times})
.

Proof.

For each
b\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, the definition of
\mathsf{Z}
yields

\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))=\sum_{(\alpha^{-1},y\alpha^{-1})\in G(\mathbb{Q})}H(\bm{s},(\alpha^{-1},y\alpha^{-1})(a,b))^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Q}}H(\bm{s},(\alpha^{-1}a,\alpha^{-1}(b+y)))^{-1}.

Plugging this into (
2.26
)
and unfolding
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
over
\mathbb{Q}

(by Fubini’s theorem), we get

\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}H(\bm{s},(\alpha^{-1}a,\alpha^{-1}b))^{-1}\overline{\psi}(b)\,db.

Using the triangle inequality, and moving
\sum_{\alpha}
to the outside, we get

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\lvert\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)\rvert\,d^{\times}{a}&\leq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\biggl\lvert{\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}H(\bm{s},(\alpha^{-1}a,\alpha^{-1}b))^{-1}\overline{\psi}(b)\,db}\biggr\rvert\,d^{\times}{a}\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\biggl\lvert{\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}H(\bm{s},(a,b))^{-1}\overline{\psi}(\alpha b)\,db}\biggr\rvert\,d^{\times}{a}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\lvert I(a,\alpha)\rvert\,d^{\times}{a},\end{split}

say.
With analogous local notation, we have

I(a,\alpha)=\prod_{v}I_{v}(a_{v},\alpha)
(by Fubini’s theorem).
However, by the right
\mathbf{K}
-invariance of
H
,
we have
I_{p}(a_{p},\alpha)=I_{p}(a_{p},\alpha)\psi_{p}(\alpha a_{p}y_{p})

for all
y_{p}\in\mathsf{N}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Taking
y_{p}=\mathsf{N}
, it follows that
I_{p}(a_{p},\alpha)=0

unless
\alpha a_{p}\mathsf{N}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
By the triangle inequality, we get

\int_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}\lvert I_{p}(a_{p},\alpha)\rvert\,d^{\times}{a_{p}}\leq\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})\rvert^{-1}\,dg_{p}
.
Since

\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\lvert I(a,\alpha)\rvert\,d^{\times}{a}=\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\prod_{v}\lvert I_{v}(a_{v},\alpha)\rvert\,d^{\times}{a}=\prod_{v}\int_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}^{\times}}\lvert I_{v}(a_{v},\alpha)\rvert\,d^{\times}{a_{v}},

it now follows from Lemma
2.16
(2)
that
\mathsf{h}\in L^{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times})
.
∎

By Lemma
2.18
,
we now have
\mathsf{h}\in L^{q}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times})
for all
q\in[1,\infty]

(since
\mathsf{h}\in L^{\infty}
).
This lets us Fourier-expand
\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)
in the variable
a
.
We do this first at finite places
v
, using
L^{2}
theory,
and then at
v=\infty
, using
L^{1}
theory.
(We roughly follow
[
52
]
*proof of Proposition 3.6.)
Let
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f}^{\times}
be the finite part of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
.
Recall the characters
\lambda\in\mathbf{M}
from (
2.12
),
and the functions
\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)
from (
2.15
).
Let
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})^{\lambda_{\infty}}
consist of
even (resp. odd) functions in
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})

if
\lambda_{\infty}
is trivial (resp. nontrivial).
Then
\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a_{f},a_{\infty})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})^{\lambda_{\infty}}

as a function of
a_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
.
Let

(2.27)

\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}(\bm{s},a_{\infty})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\varsigma\in\{\pm 1\}}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f}^{\times}}\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},(a_{f},\varsigma a_{\infty}))\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(a_{f},\varsigma a_{\infty})\,d^{\times}{a_{f}}.

Then
\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})
,
where
\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}\in L^{1}

because
\mathsf{h}\in L^{1}
and
\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}\in L^{\infty}
,
and where
\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}\in L^{\infty}

because
\mathsf{h},\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}\in L^{\infty}
and
\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}
is supported on
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

Lemma 2.19
.

We have

\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\sum_{m\geq 1}\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}(\bm{s},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{Z}
is right
\mathbf{K}
-invariant,
(
2.26
) implies
that
\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},au_{1})=\psi(au_{2})\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)
for all
(u_{1},u_{2})\in\mathbf{K}
.
Taking
u_{1}=1
and
u_{2}\in\mathsf{N}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
we find that
\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)
is supported on

a\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\cap\left(\mathbb{R}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)=\bigcup_{m\geq 1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right).

Taking
u_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\cap(1+\mathsf{N}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
we find that if
a\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,
then
\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)
depends only on

\bm{s}
,
a_{\infty}
, and the class of
\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(a_{p})=a_{p}/p^{v_{p}(a_{p})}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/(1+\mathsf{N}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for each
p\in\mathsf{S}
.
Consider the restriction
\mathsf{h}^{\prime}_{m}(\bm{s},a)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)\cdot\bm{1}_{a\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}

of
\mathsf{h}
to
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
Then
\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)=\sum_{m\geq 1}\mathsf{h}^{\prime}_{m}(\bm{s},a)
.
Additionally, by Fourier analysis on the finite group

(\mathbb{R}^{\times}/(\mathbb{R}^{\times})^{2})\times\prod_{p\in\mathsf{S}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/(1+\mathsf{N}\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
,
we have
\mathsf{h}^{\prime}_{m}(\bm{s},a)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}(\bm{s},a_{\infty})
,
since the Fourier coefficient of
the vector
\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)=\lambda_{\infty}(a_{\infty})\prod_{p}\lambda_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(a_{p}))

in
\mathsf{h}^{\prime}_{m}(\bm{s},a)
is

(2.28)
