\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\varsigma\in\{\pm 1\}}\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f}^{\times}}\mathsf{h}^{\prime}(\bm{s},(a_{f},\varsigma a_{\infty}))\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(a_{f},\varsigma a_{\infty})\,d^{\times}{a_{f}}=\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}(\bm{s},a_{\infty}).

The equality (
2.28
)
holds by (
2.27
),
since
\mathsf{h}^{\prime}(\bm{s},a)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(a)=\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(a)
,
since
\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}
is supported on
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\times\prod_{p}\mathsf{N}^{-1}m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
The lemma now immediately follows from Fubini’s theorem,
since for any
a\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
,
there are only finitely many pairs
(\lambda,m)
with
\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)\neq 0
.
∎

Lemma 2.20
.

For any
(m,\lambda,t)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\times\mathbf{M}\times\mathbb{R}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\times}}\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}(\bm{s},a_{\infty})\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,d^{\times}{a_{\infty}}=\int_{G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)\overline{\theta}_{m,\lambda,t}(g)\,dg.

Proof.

Multiplication by
-1
on
\mathbb{R}^{\times}
is measure-preserving.
So by (
2.27
) and Fubini’s theorem,

I\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\times}}\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}(\bm{s},a_{\infty})\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,d^{\times}{a_{\infty}}=\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(a)\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,d^{\times}{a}.

Plugging in (
2.26
),
and folding
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
over
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
,
we get

\begin{split}I&=\int_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\psi}(b)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(a)\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,db\,d^{\times}{a}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\alpha a,\alpha b))\overline{\psi}(\alpha b)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(\alpha a)\lvert\alpha a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,db\,d^{\times}{a}\end{split}

by Fubini’s theorem, which applies since
\mathsf{Z}\in L^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}))
and
\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(\alpha a)
is supported on
\alpha=\pm\prod_{p}p^{v_{p}(m/\mathsf{N}a_{p})}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
(if
a\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
is fixed).
Thanks to
the invariance
\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(\alpha a,\alpha b))=\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))
for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
, we can move
\sum_{\alpha}
inside to get

\begin{split}I&=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\overline{\psi}(\alpha b)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(\alpha a)\lvert\alpha a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,db\,d^{\times}{a}.\end{split}

By (
2.14
),
we have
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\overline{\psi}(\alpha b)\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{m,\lambda}(\alpha a)\lvert\alpha a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}=\overline{\theta}_{m,\lambda,t}(a,b)
,
for any
a\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
and
b\in\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
So
I=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}\int_{\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},(a,b))\overline{\theta}_{m,\lambda,t}(a,b)\,db\,d^{\times}{a}
,
which suffices by (
2.21
).
∎

Lemma 2.21
.

For any
(m,\lambda,t)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\times\mathbf{M}\times\mathbb{R}
, we have

\int_{G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\mathsf{Z}(\bm{s},g)\overline{\theta}_{m,\lambda,t}(g)\,dg=H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}H^{\prime}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\prod_{p}H^{\prime}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,m,\alpha).

Proof.

Unfolding
G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
over
G(\mathbb{Q})
proves the first equality,
by the definition (
2.17
) of
H^{\ast}
.
Fubini’s theorem here
is justified by Proposition
2.13
.
For the second equality, we first expand definitions,
using (
2.14
)
and (
2.15
),
to write
H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)
as

\int_{G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}H(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}\overline{\psi}(\alpha b)\lvert\alpha a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\overline{\lambda}_{\infty}(\alpha a_{\infty})\prod_{p}\overline{\lambda}_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(\alpha a_{p}))\bm{1}_{\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}/m\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\,dg.

Moving
\sum_{\alpha}
to the outside,
and writing
\int_{G(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}=\prod_{v}\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
,
completes the proof,
by the definition of
H^{\prime}_{\infty}
and
H^{\prime}_{p}
from (
2.19
).
The manipulations are justified by Lemma
2.16
(1).
∎

We have

\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\sum_{m\geq 1}\lvert H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)\rvert<\infty
,
by Lemma
2.16
(1)
and the second equality in Lemma
2.21
.
Therefore, Fourier expansion of
\mathsf{h}_{m,\lambda}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\times})
gives

(2.29)

\mathsf{h}(\bm{s},a)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\sum_{m\geq 1}\mathbf{v}_{m,\lambda}(a)(4\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)\lvert a_{\infty}\rvert_{\infty}^{it}\,dt,

by Lemmas
2.19
,

2.20
,
and
2.21
.
Plugging this into (
2.25
) gives

(2.30)

\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},g)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\sum_{m\geq 1}(4\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(g)\,dt,

upon recalling the definition of
\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(g)
from (
2.14
);
the required use of Fubini’s theorem
is justified by Lemma
2.16
(1).
Using (
2.16
) to evaluate
\theta_{m,\lambda,t}(g)
at
g=1_{G}
, and using the second equality in Lemma
2.21
to rewrite
H^{\ast}(\bm{s},m,\lambda,t)
, we get

\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},1_{G})=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}}\sum_{m\geq 1}(4\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(1+\lambda(-1))\lambda_{\mathsf{S}}(m/\mathsf{N})}{\prod_{p}\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{p}^{it}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}H^{\prime}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\prod_{p}H^{\prime}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,m,\alpha)\,dt.

By (
2.13
),
the factor
\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(a_{p})
of
\lambda_{\mathsf{S}}
depends only on
v_{p}(a_{p})
.
Move
\sum_{\alpha}
outside,
write

\begin{split}&\sum_{m=p^{k}:\,k\geq 0}H^{\prime}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,m,\alpha)\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(m/\mathsf{N})\lvert m/\mathsf{N}\rvert_{p}^{-it}\\
&=\sum_{m=p^{k}:\,k\geq 0}\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in m\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})^{-1}\mathsf{e}(-\alpha b_{p}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\overline{\lambda}_{p}(\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(\alpha a_{p}))\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(\alpha a_{p})\lvert\alpha a_{p}\rvert_{p}^{-it}\,dg_{p}\\
&=\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-it}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\end{split}

using (
2.19
) and (
2.18
),
and recall
H^{\prime}_{\infty}=\lvert\alpha\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}H^{\vee}_{\infty}
,
to get

(2.31)

\mathsf{Z}_{1}(\bm{s},1_{G})=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{M}:\,\lambda(-1)=1}\,\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}}(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\prod_{v}H^{\vee}_{v}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)\,dt

(cf.
[
52
]
*Proposition 5.4).
The required manipulations are justified by Lemma
2.16
(1).

Both additive and multiplicative harmonics appear above; the sum over
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
somehow reflects the non-abelian nature of
G
.
The multiplicative harmonics in (
2.31
) in fact reflect a symmetry in
\bm{s}
.
By (
2.1
) we have
\lvert a(g_{v})\rvert_{v}=H_{v}(\operatorname{div}(a),g_{v})^{-1}
, so by inspection of (
2.18
),

(2.32)

H^{\vee}_{v}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)=H^{\vee}_{v}(\bm{s}-it\operatorname{div}(a),\lambda,0,\alpha).

Thus it will suffice to study
H^{\vee}_{v}
for
t=0
(without the factor
\lvert a\rvert_{v}^{-it}
).

3.
New geometric and parametric observations

In this section, we make crucial observations for later.
Until specified otherwise,
we do not assume
X
is split or that
D
has strict normal crossings.
(This generality will be useful in §
6
.)
In particular, the
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
are irreducible but not necessarily smooth or geometrically irreducible.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{j}\in J
and
c\in\mathbb{Q}
.
Then
\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}\leq 1-\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)
.

Proof.

Let
e_{1}=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)
and
e_{2}=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)
.
Now work over
\mathbb{C}
.
Locally near a
general
point
x\in D_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
choose coordinates
y,z\ll 1

such that

a=y^{e_{1}}f_{3}(y,z),\quad b-c=y^{e_{2}}f_{4}(y,z),

where
f_{3}
,
f_{4}
are analytic with
f_{3}(0,0)f_{4}(0,0)\neq 0
.
(We choose
y
so that
y=0
cuts out
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
near
x
.)
Thus

da\,db=O(y^{e_{1}+e_{2}-1})\,dy\,dz.

So
\omega=db\,da/a=O(y^{e_{2}-1})\,dz\,dy
.
So
-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\omega)\geq e_{2}-1
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.4
.

By Proposition
2.2
,
\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}\geq 1-\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)
.
Now use Proposition
3.1
.
∎

Recall
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
,
J_{3}
from §
2.3
.
Inspired by Proposition
1.4

and the condition (
1.5
), let

(3.1)

\displaystyle J^{c}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)\},

(3.2)

\displaystyle J^{\ast}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b/a)=0\},

(3.3)

\displaystyle I^{c}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathfrak{j}\in J:\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)>0\},

for each
c\in\mathbb{Q}
.
Then in particular,

(3.4)

J^{c}_{1}\subseteq I^{c}\subseteq J_{1},

where the latter containment holds by Proposition
1.4
.

Definition 3.2
.

For each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, let

(3.5)

{\textstyle\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\argmax_{c\in\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)},

where in general for a function
f_{5}\colon V\to\mathbb{R}
,
we let

(3.6)

{\textstyle\argmax_{V}f_{5}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\argmax_{x\in V}f_{5}(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{x\in V:f_{5}(x)\geq f_{5}(y)\;\textnormal{for all $y\in V$}\}}.

Proposition 3.3
.

If
\mathfrak{j}\in I^{c}
for some
c\in\mathbb{Q}
,
then
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})=\{c\}
;
otherwise,

\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})=\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

For
d\in\mathbb{Q}
, let
f_{6}(d)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-d)
.
If
\mathfrak{j}\in I^{c}
, then for all
d\neq c
, we have

f_{6}(d)=0<f_{6}(c),

so
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})=\{c\}
.
If
\mathfrak{j}\notin\bigcup_{c\in\mathbb{Q}}I^{c}

and
f_{6}(0)\geq 0
, then
f_{6}(c)=0
for all
c\in\mathbb{Q}
,
so
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})=\mathbb{Q}
.
If
f_{6}(0)<0
, then
f_{6}(c)=f_{6}(0)
for all
c\in\mathbb{Q}
,
so
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})=\mathbb{Q}
.
∎

Thus
I^{c}\cap I^{c^{\prime}}=\emptyset
if
c\neq c^{\prime}
.
In addition, for
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, we find that

D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is special (Definition
1.5
)
if and only if

\mathfrak{j}\in J^{\ast}_{2}
or
\mathfrak{j}\in\bigcup_{c\in\mathbb{Q}}J^{c}_{1}
.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
c\in\mathbb{Q}
.
Then
\lvert J^{c}_{1}\cup J^{\ast}_{2}\rvert\leq\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))
.

Proof.

Since
X
admits no nonconstant morphism to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we have
\operatorname{div}_{\infty}((b-c)/a)\neq 0
(by the algebraic Hartogs lemma).
But
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}((b-c)/a)\geq 0
for all
\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c}_{1}\cup J^{\ast}_{2}
.
Since
\operatorname{div}_{\infty}((b-c)/a)
is supported on
D
,
it follows that
\lvert J^{c}_{1}\cup J^{\ast}_{2}\rvert\leq\lvert J\rvert-1=\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))
,
where the final equality holds by Proposition
2.1
.
(This holds even if
X
is non-split, because
J
labels irreducible divisors over
\mathbb{Q}
rather than
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
.)
∎

Precisely understanding the
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
seems tricky in general, but the following will suffice:

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{j}\in J
and

c\in\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})
.
Let
e_{1}=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)
,
e_{2}=\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)
,
and
F_{7}=(b-c)^{e_{1}}/a^{e_{2}}
.
Then
F_{7}\in\Gamma(U_{1},\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\times}
for some dense open set
U_{1}\subseteq X
with
U_{1}\cap D_{\mathfrak{j}}\neq\emptyset
.
The rational map
f_{8}\colon D_{\mathfrak{j}}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}}
extending
F_{7}|_{U_{1}\cap D_{\mathfrak{j}}}
is nonconstant.

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(F_{7})=0
, the first statement is clear.
In particular,
f_{8}^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\neq\emptyset
.

Case 1:
e_{2}=0
.

If
f_{8}
were constant, then we would have
b|_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}=c^{\prime}

for some constant
c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Q}
,
and thus
1\leq\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c^{\prime})\leq\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c)=e_{2}=0
,
where the second inequality holds because
c\in\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})
.
That is absurd;
so instead,
f_{8}
is nonconstant.

Case 2:
e_{1}=e_{2}\neq 0
.

The formula (
1.1
) is “homogeneous” in
a
,
b
,
so it induces a right
G
-action

y\cdot(u_{1},u_{2})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(u_{2}+y)/u_{1}

on
y=b/a\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
which extends to a
G
-action on
y\in\mathbb{P}^{1}

(with a fixed point at
\infty
).
Moreover, for this
G
-action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
the rational map
b/a
from
X
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is
G
-equivariant.
Similarly,
the rational map
(b-c)/a
from
X
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is
G
-equivariant;
one can check this using (
1.1
),
or by factoring
(b-c)/a
through the
G
-equivariant map

(a,b)\mapsto(1,-c)(a,b)=(a,b-c)
on
G
.
Thus
\frac{b-c}{a}|_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}

is a
G
-equivariant rational map
D_{\mathfrak{j}}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Since
f_{8}^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\neq\emptyset
,
and
G
acts transitively on
\mathbb{A}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
,
it follows that
f_{8}
is nonconstant.

Case 3:
e_{2}\neq 0
and
e_{1}\neq e_{2}
.

Then
e_{1}>e_{2}

by Proposition
1.4
,
so
a/(b-c)
vanishes on a dense open set
V_{1}\subseteq D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.
But (
1.1
) yields,
in
\operatorname{Rat}(X\times G)
,
the identity

F_{7}(x\cdot(u_{1},u_{2}))=(au_{2}+b-c)^{e_{1}}/(au_{1})^{e_{2}}=(au_{2}/(b-c)+1)^{e_{1}}\cdot F_{7}(x)/u_{1}^{e_{2}}

(for
x\in X
and
(u_{1},u_{2})\in G
).
Since
F_{7}(x)
,

F_{7}(x\cdot(u_{1},u_{2}))
,
and
(au_{2}/(b-c)+1)^{e_{1}}

are regular on
X\times G
near a
general
point
(x,g)\in D_{\mathfrak{j}}\times G
,
restriction to
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
gives

f_{8}(x\cdot(u_{1},u_{2}))=f_{8}(x)/u_{1}^{e_{2}}

in
\operatorname{Rat}(D_{\mathfrak{j}}\times G)
.
Thus
f_{8}
is
G
-equivariant for
a nontrivial multiplicative action of
G
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}

(where
G
fixes
0
and
\infty
, and acts transitively on
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
).
Since
f_{8}^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
is nonempty, it follows that
f_{8}
is nonconstant.
∎

From now on, assume
X
is split.
Thus, each
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is
geometrically
irreducible.
In particular, a nonconstant function on
D_{\mathfrak{j}}

will remain nonconstant on any nonempty open subset of
D_{\mathfrak{j}}(E)
,
for any local field
E
of characteristic
0
.
This observation, applied to the function
F_{7}
from Proposition
3.5
, leads to new insight on local parameterizations of
X
near
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{j}
,
c
,
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, and
F_{7}

be as in Proposition
3.5
.
Let
U_{2}\subseteq X
be the largest open set such that

D_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap U_{2}
is smooth and

a,b-c\in\Gamma(U_{2}\setminus D_{\mathfrak{j}},\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\times}
.
Let
x\in(D_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap U_{2})(\mathbb{R})
.

(1)

Assume
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{2}
.
Then locally near
x
,
there are real-analytic coordinates
y,z\ll 1

with
a=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{1}}
and
(b-c)/y^{e_{2}}=\mathsf{w}_{2}+z
,
where
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
.

(2)

Assume
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{3}
.
Then locally near
x
,
there are real-analytic coordinates
y,z\ll 1

with
b-c=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{2}}
and
a=\mathsf{w}_{2}+z
,
where
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
.

(3)

The functions
(\mathcal{T}{y})/y

and
\mathcal{T}{z}
are regular near
x
,
for any
\mathcal{T}\in\{(b-c)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b},a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial a},a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}\}
.

Proof.

(1):
There exists
\mathsf{t}\in\operatorname{Rat}(X)
(defining
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
locally)
such that
a/\mathsf{t}^{e_{1}},(b-c)/\mathsf{t}^{e_{2}}\in\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{\times}
.
Let
\upsilon\in\operatorname{Rat}(X)
be a regular local coordinate complementary to
\mathsf{t}
;
then analytically we have

a/\mathsf{t}^{e_{1}}=\mathsf{w}_{1}+f_{9}(\mathsf{t},\upsilon),\quad(b-c)/\mathsf{t}^{e_{2}}=\mathsf{w}_{2}+f_{10}(\mathsf{t},\upsilon),

say,
where
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
and
f_{9}(0,0)=f_{10}(0,0)=0
.
Since
e_{1}\neq 0

(we have
e_{1}>0
if
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
, and
e_{1}<0
if
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
),
we may change variables from
\mathsf{t}

to
y\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{t}(1+f_{9}(\mathsf{t},\upsilon)/\mathsf{w}_{1})^{1/e_{1}}
.
More precisely,
we are changing variables from
(\mathsf{t},\upsilon)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
to
(y,\upsilon)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
using the inverse function theorem for analytic functions.
Then

a=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{1}},\quad(b-c)/y^{e_{2}}=\mathsf{w}_{2}+f_{11}(y,\upsilon),\quad F_{7}(a,b)=(\mathsf{w}_{2}+f_{11})^{e_{1}}/\mathsf{w}_{1}^{e_{2}}=\mathsf{w}_{3}+f_{12}(y,\upsilon),

say,
where
\mathsf{w}_{3}=\mathsf{w}_{2}^{e_{1}}/\mathsf{w}_{1}^{e_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
.

Differentiating
a=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{1}}
in
b

gives
\partial{y}/\partial{b}=0
,
since
e_{1}\neq 0
and
\partial{a}/\partial{b}=0
.
Differentiating
F_{7}=\mathsf{w}_{3}+f_{12}
in
b
gives

e_{1}F_{7}=(b-c)\partial{F_{7}(a,b)}/\partial{b}=(b-c)\partial{f_{12}(y,\upsilon)}/\partial{b}=(b-c)(\partial{\upsilon}/\partial{b})(\partial{f_{12}}/\partial{\upsilon}),

since
\partial{y}/\partial{b}=0
.
Yet the function
\partial{\upsilon}/\partial{b}
near
x

is regular away from
D_{\mathfrak{j}}

(because
\upsilon\in\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
,
and
(a,b)
is a pair of regular
coordinates
away from
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
).
Therefore,
(b-c)\partial{\upsilon}/\partial{b}
is either regular near
x
, or polar along
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.
Since
e_{1}F_{7}\in\Gamma(U_{2},\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\times}
,
we conclude that
the
regular
function
\partial{f_{12}}/\partial{\upsilon}
near
x

is either invertible, or zero along
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
.
But by Proposition
3.5
,
the map
F_{7}=\mathsf{w}_{3}+f_{12}
is nonconstant along
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
so the latter case is impossible
(since
f_{12}(y,\upsilon)|_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}=f_{12}(0,\upsilon)
locally).
Instead,
\partial{f_{12}}/\partial{\upsilon}
must be invertible.
So by the analytic inverse function theorem,
we may change variables from
\upsilon
to
\upsilon^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f_{12}(y,\upsilon)
.
Yet

(1+f_{11}(y,\upsilon)/\mathsf{w}_{2})^{e_{1}}=1+f_{12}(y,\upsilon)/\mathsf{w}_{3}=1+\upsilon^{\prime}/\mathsf{w}_{3}.

Taking
e_{1}
th roots lets us change variables
from
\upsilon^{\prime}
to
z\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f_{11}(y,\upsilon)
.
Thus
a=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{1}}
and
(b-c)/y^{e_{2}}=\mathsf{w}_{2}+z
,
as desired.

(2):
This is similar.
First, note that
e_{1}=0
(since
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{3}
) and write

(b-c)/\mathsf{t}^{e_{2}}=\mathsf{w}_{1}+f_{13}(\mathsf{t},\upsilon),\quad a=\mathsf{w}_{2}+f_{14}(\mathsf{t},\upsilon),

say, where
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is locally cut out by
\mathsf{t}=0
.
Next, change variables from
\mathsf{t}
to a suitable
y

so that
b-c=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{2}}
and
a=\mathsf{w}_{2}+f_{15}(y,\upsilon)
, say.
Here
\partial{y}/\partial{a}=0
, since
e_{2}\neq 0
and
\partial{b}/\partial{a}=0
.
Now analyze the equation

1=\partial{a}/\partial{a}=\partial{f_{15}(y,\upsilon)}/\partial{a}=(\partial{\upsilon}/\partial{a})(\partial{f_{15}}/\partial{\upsilon})

using the nonconstancy of
a|_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}
(guaranteed by Proposition
3.5
)
and the local identity
f_{15}(y,\upsilon)|_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}=f_{15}(0,\upsilon)
,
to change variables from
\upsilon
to
z\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f_{15}(y,\upsilon)
.

(3):
Given (1) and (2),
this is routine.
For
\mathcal{T}\in\{(b-c)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b},a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial a}\}
,
use the chain rule and the fact that

(\mathcal{T}a)/a\in\mathbb{Z}
,
(\mathcal{T}b)/(b-c)\in\mathbb{Z}

are constant.
For
\mathcal{T}=a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}
,
use
a/(b-c)\in\Gamma(U_{2},\mathcal{O}_{X})
(given by Proposition
1.4
)
to reduce to the
\mathcal{T}=(b-c)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}
case.
(We note here that (3) is only new when
\mathcal{T}=(b-c)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}
;
the other cases already appeared in
[
9
]
.)
∎

Remark 3.7
.

The lemma generalizes to any
local field
E
of characteristic
0
.
This gives a map
(y,z)\mapsto(a,b)
that locally parameterizes
G(E)
.
Being a parameterization, this map must be injective away from
D
.
Taking
E=\mathbb{C}
, we find that injectivity forces
\gcd(e_{1},e_{2})=1
.

For the rest of §
3
, assume
D
has strict normal crossings.
Using the inverse function theorem for formal power series with integral coefficients,
4
4

4

The particular statement we need is that if
\varphi_{1}\in R[[x,y]]
,
\varphi_{1}(0,0)=0
, and
\partial{\varphi_{1}}/\partial{y}\in R[[x,y]]^{\times}
, where
R
is a ring, then there exists a
\varphi_{2}\in R[[x,y]]
such that
\varphi_{2}(0,0)=0
and
\varphi_{1}(x,\varphi_{2}(x,y))=\varphi_{2}(x,\varphi_{1}(x,y))=y
.
The proof is standard:
we first construct a right inverse
\varphi_{2}
of
\varphi_{1}(x,\cdot)
,
degree by degree, by Hensel’s lemma in
R[[x,y]]
;
iterating, we get a right inverse
\varphi_{3}
of
\varphi_{2}(x,\cdot)
;
finally, a composition trick shows that
\varphi_{3}=\varphi_{1}
.

instead of the inverse function theorem for analytic functions,
we can refine Lemma
3.6
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
The result is Lemma
3.8
below.
Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(in an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
),
and let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}
.
Explicitly,
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}
(resp.
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}
)
is generated over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(resp.
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
)
by the set of all roots of unity in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
of order coprime to
p
.
Moreover, the residue field
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}/p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}

is an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
of
the residue field
\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

Let
\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\colon\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{F}_{p})

and
\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}\colon\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}})\to\mathscr{X}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})

be reduction modulo
p
.
If
\mathscr{V}
is a scheme and
R
is a ring,
then
\mathscr{V}\times R
, or
\mathscr{V}_{R}
,
denotes the
base change

\mathscr{V}\times\operatorname{Spec}(R)
.
If moreover
R\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}\}
,
then we may identify
\mathscr{V}(R/pR)

with the set of
R/pR
-valued closed points of
the special fiber
\mathscr{V}_{R/pR}\subseteq\mathscr{V}_{R}
.
Finally, elements
f\in\Gamma(\mathscr{V},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{V}})

will often be written as
f(x)
,
in order to emphasize that
f
induces a map
\mathscr{V}(R)\to R,\;x\mapsto f(x)
.
This will all be convenient below,
for describing and studying certain local rings of schemes.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{j}
,
c
,
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
,
and
U_{2}
be as in Lemma
3.6
.
Let
\mathscr{U}_{2}
be the complement of the closure of
X\setminus U_{2}
in
\mathscr{X}
.
Let
p
be a large prime.
Let
R\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}\}

and
x_{0}\in\mathscr{U}_{2}(R/pR)
.
Then there exist an analytic isomorphism

\rho_{R}^{-1}(x_{0})\to pR^{2},\quad x\mapsto(y,z),

and units
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}\in R^{\times}
depending on
x_{0}
,
such that for all
x\in\rho_{R}^{-1}(x_{0})
the following hold:

(1)

if
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(R/pR)

and
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{2}
,
then
a(x)=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{1}}
and
b(x)-c=y^{e_{2}}(\mathsf{w}_{2}+z)
;

(2)

if
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(R/pR)

and
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{3}
,
then
b(x)-c=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{e_{2}}
and
a(x)=\mathsf{w}_{2}+z
;
and

(3)

if
x_{0}\in\mathscr{G}(R/pR)
,
then
a(x)=\mathsf{w}_{1}+y
and
b(x)-c=\mathsf{w}_{2}+z
.

Proof.

Uniformity over
p
and
x_{0}
takes some care.
We use the quasi-compactness of
U_{2}
.
Let
V_{2}\subseteq\mathscr{U}_{2}
be a small affine open neighborhood of a point of
U_{2}
.
Then there exist two elements
\mathsf{t}(x),\upsilon(x)\in\Gamma(V_{2},\mathcal{O}_{V_{2}})

such that
for every prime
p\gg 1

and point
x^{\prime}\in V_{2}(R)
,
the maximal ideal of the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{V_{2}\times R,\rho_{R}(x^{\prime})}

is generated by
p
,
\mathsf{t}(x)-\mathsf{t}(x^{\prime})
, and
\upsilon(x)-\upsilon(x^{\prime})
.
5
5

5

Thus

\mathsf{t}(x)-\mathsf{t}(x^{\prime})
and
\upsilon(x)-\upsilon(x^{\prime})

are regular local coordinates for
(V_{2})_{R/pR}
at
\rho_{R}(x^{\prime})\in V_{2}(R/pR)
.
This condition, when cast in terms of differentials,
is equivalent to the nonvanishing of some determinant modulo
p
,
and thus can be guaranteed by removing from
V_{2}
the zero locus of the determinant if necessary.

Moreover, since
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is smooth, we may choose
\mathsf{t}
and
\upsilon

so that
\operatorname{div}(\mathsf{t}|_{V_{2}})=\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}|_{V_{2}}

(if
V_{2}
is small enough).

In general,
given a point
x_{0}\in\mathscr{U}_{2}(R/pR)
,
we choose
V_{2}
and
x^{\prime}\in V_{2}(R)

such that
\rho_{R}(x^{\prime})=x_{0}
.
This is possible by Hensel’s lemma,
provided that
p
is large enough.

Now
a/\mathsf{t}^{e_{1}}
and
(b-c)/\mathsf{t}^{e_{2}}

are invertible regular functions over
\mathbb{Q}

(i.e. morphisms
(V_{2})_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
);
so are
e_{1}F_{7}
and
(b-c)\partial{\upsilon}/\partial{b}

(resp.
a
and
\partial{\upsilon}/\partial{a}
)
if
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{2}

(resp.
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{3}
).
So they are invertible regular
over some
\mathbb{Z}[1/A_{6}]
with
A_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
.
By the inverse function theorem in

R[[\mathsf{t}(x)-\mathsf{t}(x^{\prime}),\upsilon(x)-\upsilon(x^{\prime})]],

if
p\nmid A_{6}
,
the method of Lemma
3.6

thus yields (1) and (2)
for each
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap V_{2})(R/pR)
.

If
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{G}\cap V_{2})(R/pR)

and
p\nmid A_{6}
,
then
a(x^{\prime}),b(x^{\prime})-c\in R^{\times}
,
so for (3) we can simply take

\mathsf{w}_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}a(x^{\prime})
,

\mathsf{w}_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}b(x^{\prime})-c
,

y\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}a(x)-a(x^{\prime})
,
and
z\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}b(x)-b(x^{\prime})
.
∎

We end with some local constancy analysis useful for Lemmas
4.8
and
5.1
.
Roughly speaking, we will cover
X
by open sets on which all divisors
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
behave compatibly.
On each such open set, we will prove two smoothness results for local height functions:
Lemma
3.11
for large primes
p
,
and Lemma
3.14
for small primes
p
.

Definition 3.9
.

For any set
U\subseteq X
, not necessarily open,
let

\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(U)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcap_{\mathfrak{j}\in J:\,D_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap U\neq\emptyset}\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})\subseteq\mathbb{Q},

where
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})
is the set defined in (
3.5
).

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{(2)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\textnormal{open }U\subseteq X:\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(U)\neq\emptyset\}
.
Then
\bigcup_{U\in\mathcal{C}^{(2)}}U=X
.

Proof.

If
x\in X
, then
\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(U)=\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(\{x\})

for any small enough open set
U\ni x
.
To conclude, it suffices to show that

\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(\{x\})\neq\emptyset
for all
x\in X
.
Suppose for contradiction that
\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(\{x\})=\emptyset

for some
x\in X
.
Then by Proposition
3.3
,
we must have
x\in D_{\mathfrak{i}}\cap D_{\mathfrak{j}}

for some
\mathfrak{i}\in I^{c}
and
\mathfrak{j}\in I^{c^{\prime}}
with
c\neq c^{\prime}
.
Now
\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{i}}}(b-c),\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c^{\prime})>0
,
by (
3.3
).
But
D
has strict normal crossings,
and
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}
(since
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{i})=\{c\}\neq\{c^{\prime}\}=\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})
),
so
D_{\mathfrak{i}}\cap D_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(b)=\emptyset
.
Thus
x\notin\operatorname{div}_{\infty}(b)
,
whence
b\in\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
.
So
c=b(x)=c^{\prime}
, a contradiction.
∎

It turns out that
sets
U\in\mathcal{C}^{(2)}
possess extra analytic symmetries.
To facilitate proofs, let
\Gamma_{p}(\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{R})
be the set of
\mathfrak{R}
-valued analytic functions on a
p
-adic analytic manifold
\mathfrak{M}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
U\in\mathcal{C}^{(2)}
and
c\in\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(U)
.
Let
\mathscr{U}
be the complement of the closure of
X\setminus U
in
\mathscr{X}
.
Then for all sufficiently large primes
p
, the following hold for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
:

(1)

The map
\phi\colon(a,b)\mapsto(ax_{1},c+(b-c)x_{2})
on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

maps
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap\mathscr{U}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
to itself.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, then
H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}(g)=H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}(\phi(g))
for all
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap\mathscr{U}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proof.

Let
B
be the closure in
\mathscr{X}
of the set of pairwise intersections of irreducible components of
D\cup\operatorname{div}_{0}(b-c)
.
Importantly for Hartogs-style arguments,
B_{\mathbb{Q}}
has dimension
0
.

Let
p
be large.
Let
V\subseteq\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be an affine open set
such that for each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, there exist
\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}},\upsilon_{\mathfrak{j}}\in\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})
with
\operatorname{div}(\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}|_{V})=\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}|_{V}
such that
\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
\upsilon_{\mathfrak{j}}
are regular coordinates for
V_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
(as in the proof of Lemma
3.8
; note that
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is smooth).
By calculation in terms of the analytic local coordinates of Lemma
3.8
(applicable by Definition
3.9
),
we find that for all
f_{16}\in\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})

and
x^{\prime}\in(V\setminus B)(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}})
,
the rational functions

(\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}})/(p\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}})
,

(f_{16}-\phi^{\ast}{f_{16}})/p

on
V
lie in

(3.7)

\operatorname{Rat}(V)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}[[\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}(x)-\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}(x^{\prime}),\upsilon_{\mathfrak{j}}(x)-\upsilon_{\mathfrak{j}}(x^{\prime})]]=\operatorname{Rat}(V)\cap\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{V\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}},\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}(x^{\prime})}=\mathcal{O}_{V,\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}(x^{\prime})},

where
\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}
denotes completion.
(The first equality in (
3.7
) comes from
the maximal ideal
(p,\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}(x)-\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}(x^{\prime}),\upsilon_{\mathfrak{j}}(x)-\upsilon_{\mathfrak{j}}(x^{\prime}))

of
\mathcal{O}_{V\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}},\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}(x^{\prime})}
.
For the second,
note that
\operatorname{Rat}(V)=\operatorname{Frac}(\mathcal{O}_{V,\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}(x^{\prime})})
,
and
\mathcal{O}_{V,\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}(x^{\prime})}\to\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{V\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}},\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}}}(x^{\prime})}

is a faithfully flat injection of regular local rings.)
Thus

\frac{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}}}{p\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}},\frac{f_{16}-\phi^{\ast}{f_{16}}}{p}\in\bigcap_{x^{\prime}\in(V\setminus B)_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{V,x^{\prime}},

since
(V\setminus B)(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{ur}})
maps onto
(V\setminus B)(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
.
Yet
\dim(B_{\mathbb{F}_{p}})=0=\dim(V_{\mathbb{F}_{p}})-2
,
so every prime divisor
\mathfrak{P}\subseteq V
that intersects
B_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}

must pass through some point of
(V\setminus B)_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.
Upon writing each
\mathcal{O}_{V,x^{\prime}}
as the intersection of
its localizations at height
1
primes,
we conclude that

\frac{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}}}{p\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}},\frac{f_{16}-\phi^{\ast}{f_{16}}}{p}\in\bigcap_{x^{\prime}\in V_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{V,x^{\prime}}.

Crucially, each function in
\bigcap_{x^{\prime}\in V_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{V,x^{\prime}}

induces on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-points a function in
\Gamma_{p}(V(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
By converting between
the local analytic coordinates in (
3.7
)
and the global algebraic coordinates on
V
given by
\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})
,
it follows that for every
x^{\prime}\in V(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, the analytic map

\phi|_{\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{-1}(x^{\prime})\cap\phi^{-1}(V(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))}\colon\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{-1}(x^{\prime})\cap\phi^{-1}(V(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap V(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

(where
\phi^{-1}(V(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\phi(g)\in V(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\}
)
extends uniquely to an analytic map

\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{-1}(x^{\prime})\to\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{-1}(x^{\prime}),

since
\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{-1}(x^{\prime})\cap\phi^{-1}(V(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
is dense in
\rho_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{-1}(x^{\prime})
,
in the
p
-adic topology.
(Density follows from
the fact that
X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\setminus\phi^{-1}(V(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))

is a proper algebraic subset of
X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.)

Thus
\phi
extends (uniquely) to a map
V(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to V(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
Covering
\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
by
V
yields (1).
6
6

6

A referee
showed us that for
p\gg 1
,
the map in (1) in fact induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme morphism

\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.
The main additional observation needed is that

(\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}})/(p\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}),(f_{16}-\phi^{\ast}{f_{16}})/p\in\Gamma(\mathscr{G}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cap V,\mathcal{O}_{V})
.

Also,
(\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}})/\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}\in 1+p\Gamma_{p}(V(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})

implies

\lvert\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\phi(x))\rvert_{p}=\lvert\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}(x)\rvert_{p}

for all
x\in V(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
whence (2) holds by Definition
2.6
.
∎

Lemma
3.11
leaves out small primes
p
, which we address by
a
p
-adic analytic Hartogs.
Let
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(f_{17})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\cup\{\infty\}

be the degree of the leading homogeneous term of a power series
f_{17}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

Fix a finite extension
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and an integer
n\geq 2
.
Fix
P,Q\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]=\mathcal{O}_{K}[[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]]
.
Suppose
\gcd(P,Q)=1
and

\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q)=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q\bmod{\pi_{K}})\geq 1
,
where
\pi_{K}
is a uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Then for some finite extension
L/K
,
the set

\{\bm{x}\in p\mathcal{O}_{L}^{n}:Q(\bm{x})=0,P(\bm{x})\neq 0\}

has a limit point lying in

(p\mathcal{O}_{L}^{2}\setminus 0)\times\{0\}^{n-2}
,
where
p\mathcal{O}_{L}^{2}\setminus 0\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p\mathcal{O}_{L}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
.

Proof.

If
Q=0
, then
P\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]^{\times}
, so
P(\bm{x})\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
for all
\bm{x}\in p\mathcal{O}_{K}^{n}
, and the result is obvious.
Now suppose
Q\neq 0
.
Since
Q\notin\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]^{\times}
, we must have
P\neq 0
.
Also,
Q
is primitive (i.e.
\pi_{K}\nmid Q
), since
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q\bmod{\pi_{K}})=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q)<\infty
.
By removing factors of
\pi_{K}
in
P
, we may assume
P
is primitive too.
Let
P_{0},Q_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[\bm{x}]
be the lowest-degree
primitive
homogeneous terms in
P
,
Q
, respectively.
Then in particular,

\deg(Q_{0})=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q\bmod{\pi_{K}})=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q).

After replacing
K
with a finite extension if necessary,
there exists a
\bm{k}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{n-1}\times\{0\}
such that
P_{0}(k_{1},\dots,k_{n-1},1)Q_{0}(k_{1},\dots,k_{n-1},1)\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.
Via the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-linear automorphism
\bm{x}\mapsto\bm{x}+x_{n}\bm{k}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]
,
we may then assume

P_{0}(0,\dots,0,1)Q_{0}(0,\dots,0,1)\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}.

Thus the power series
P,Q\bmod{(\pi_{K},x_{1},\dots,x_{n-1})}\in(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\pi_{K})[[x_{n}]]

have orders
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(\cdot)
equal to
\deg{P_{0}}
,
\deg{Q_{0}}
, respectively.
So by the Weierstrass preparation theorem
in
R[[x_{n}]]
,
where
R\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{O}_{K}[[x_{1},\dots,x_{n-1}]]
,
there exist unique
monic
polynomials
P_{1},Q_{1}\in R[x_{n}]

of degrees
\deg{P_{0}}
,
\deg{Q_{0}}
, respectively,
with

P/P_{1},Q/Q_{1}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]^{\times}.

Let
P_{1,j},Q_{1,j}\in R
be the
x_{n}^{j}
coefficients of
P_{1}
,
Q_{1}
, respectively.
Reduction modulo
(\pi_{K},x_{1},\dots,x_{n-1})
forces
\pi_{K}\mid P_{1,j}(\bm{0})
if
j<\deg{P_{1}}
, and
\pi_{K}\mid Q_{1,j}(\bm{0})
if
j<\deg{Q_{1}}
.
Therefore, any divisor of
P_{1}Q_{1}
in
R[x_{n}]
either lies in
R^{\times}
or the ideal
(\pi_{K},x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
.
From
\gcd(P,Q)=1
in
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]
,
we thus get
\gcd(P_{1},Q_{1})=1
in
R[x_{n}]
.
So by Gauss’ lemma,
we have
\gcd(P_{1},Q_{1})=1
in
\operatorname{Frac}(R)[x_{n}]
,
a Euclidean domain, whence

AP_{1}+BQ_{1}=C

for some
A,B,C\in R
with
C\neq 0
.
On the other hand,

Q/Q_{1}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[\bm{x}]]^{\times}
implies

\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q_{1})=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q)=\deg(Q_{0})=\deg(Q_{1})

(if we define
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q_{1})
viewing
Q_{1}
as a power series in
\bm{x}
),
whence

\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q_{1,j})\geq\deg(Q_{1})-j\geq 1

(so
Q_{1,j}(\bm{0})=0
)
for all
j<\deg{Q_{1}}
.
Let

f_{18,j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}Q_{1,j}|_{x_{3}=\dots=x_{n-1}=0}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[x_{1},x_{2}]].

The idea now is to choose points
\bm{x}
at which
Q_{1}=0
and
C\neq 0
.
Let
L(d)
be the compositum of all degree
\leq d
extensions of
K
in
\overline{K}
;
then
L(d)/K
is finite (by Krasner’s lemma).

Case 1:
n\geq 3
, and
f_{18,j}=0
for all
j<\deg{Q_{1}}
.

For each large integer
r\geq 1
,
choose

x_{1},x_{2}\in p+p^{r}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times},\quad x_{3},\dots,x_{n-1}\in p^{r}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}

with
C\neq 0
; then choose
x_{n}\in\overline{K}
with
Q_{1}=0
.
(Roughly,
v_{p}(x_{3}),\dots,v_{p}(x_{n-1})
ensure
Q_{1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
resembles a monomial in
x_{n}
of degree
\deg{Q_{1}}
.)
Letting
r\to\infty
produces an infinite sequence of
\bm{x}\in p\mathcal{O}_{L(\deg{Q_{1}})}^{n}
tending to
(p,p,0,\dots,0)
.

Case 2:
n\geq 3
, and
f_{18,j}\neq 0
for some
j<\deg{Q_{1}}
.

Take
j<\deg{Q_{1}}
minimal with
f_{18,j}\neq 0
.
Via a linear automorphism
(x_{1},x_{2})\mapsto(x_{1},x_{2}+kx_{1})
with
k\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
, assume
f_{18,j}|_{x_{2}=0}\neq 0
.
Choose
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

f_{18,j}(p^{c}y,0)\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[y]]
is divisible by its own leading term.
Given large integers
r,d\geq 1
,
choose

x_{2}\in p^{d}+p^{r}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times},\quad x_{3},\dots,x_{n-1}\in p^{dr}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}

with
C\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[[x_{1}]]\setminus 0
.
Then
C
has only finitely many zeros
x_{1}\in\overline{K}
with
v_{p}(x_{1})>0
,
and for each such
x_{1}
the set
\{x_{n}\in\overline{K}:Q_{1}=0\}
is finite.
Thus there exists
x_{n}\in p^{r}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
such that

\{x_{1}\in\overline{K}:v_{p}(x_{1})>0,\;Q_{1}=C=0\}=\emptyset.

Next, if
r
and
d
are large enough, then by Weierstrass preparation in
\mathcal{O}_{K}[[y]]
(applied to
Q_{1}(p^{c}y,x_{2},\dots,x_{n})/x_{n}^{j}
) and the fact
Q_{1,j}(\bm{0})=0
, there exists
x_{1}\in p^{c+1}\mathcal{O}_{L(d)}
with
Q_{1}=0
.
(Roughly,
v_{p}(x_{2}),\dots,v_{p}(x_{n})
and the minimality of
j
ensure

Q_{1}/x_{n}^{j}
resembles a monomial in
y
of degree
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(f_{18,j}|_{x_{2}=0})
.)
Taking
r\to\infty
produces
\bm{x}\in p\mathcal{O}_{L(d)}^{n}
with
1\ll\max(\lvert x_{1}\rvert_{p},\lvert x_{2}\rvert_{p})\ll 1
and
x_{3},\dots,x_{n}\to 0
.
Now pass to a convergent subsequence (by compactness).

Case 3:
n=2
.

Here
\#\{x_{1}\in\overline{K}:v_{p}(x_{1})>0,\;C=0\}<\infty
.
So there are infinitely many
x_{1}\in p^{\deg{Q_{1}}}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
with
C\neq 0
.
For each such
x_{1}
, there exists
x_{2}\in p\mathcal{O}_{L(\deg{Q_{1}})}
with
Q_{1}=0
.
(It is important here that
Q_{1,j}(0)=0
for all
j<\deg{Q_{1}}
.)
By compactness, this suffices.
∎

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
f_{19}\in\operatorname{Frac}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N},y,z]])
,
where
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
Suppose that for every
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and finite extension
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
there exists
m=m(l,E)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that

(3.8)

f_{19}\in\Gamma_{p}(p^{m}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{N}\times(p^{l}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{2}\setminus p^{l+1}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{2}),\mathcal{O}_{E}).

Then there exists
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that

f_{19}\in\Gamma_{p}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{N+2},\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Proof.

Write
f_{19}=\varsigma P/Q
with
P,Q\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\xi_{1},\dots,z]]
,
Q\neq 0
, and
\varsigma\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
.
By a suitable scaling
(\xi_{1},\dots,z)\mapsto(p^{d}\xi_{1},\dots,p^{d}z)
, we may assume

\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q)=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q\bmod{p})
.
After dividing out
\gcd(P,Q)
, and applying Gauss’ lemma to the leading homogeneous terms of
Q
,
\gcd(P,Q)
,
Q/\gcd(P,Q)
, we may assume that
\gcd(P,Q)=1
and
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q)=\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q\bmod{p})
.

Case 1:
Q(\bm{0})\neq 0
.

Then
Q(\bm{0})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,
so
f_{19}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\xi_{1},\dots,z]]\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
Hence
f_{19}\in f_{19}(\bm{0})+\Gamma_{p}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{N+2},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

if
l
is large enough.
Yet
f_{19}(0,\dots,0,p^{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
l\geq 1
,
by (
3.8
).
We conclude that
f_{19}(\bm{0})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
and thus
f_{19}\in\Gamma_{p}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{N+2},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for any sufficiently large
l
.

Case 2:
Q(\bm{0})=0
.

Then
\operatorname{ord}_{0}(Q)\geq 1
.
So by the
(K,n)=(\mathbb{Q}_{p},N+2)
case of Lemma
3.12
,
there exist a finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
an integer
l\geq 1
,
and a sequence of points

\bm{x}^{(m)}\in p^{m}\mathcal{O}_{L}^{N}\times(p^{l}\mathcal{O}_{L}^{2}\setminus p^{l+1}\mathcal{O}_{L}^{2})

indexed by integers
m\geq 1
,
such that
Q(\bm{x}^{(m)})=0
and
P(\bm{x}^{(m)})\neq 0

for all
m\geq 1
.
This contradicts the
m=m(l,L)
case of (
3.8
).
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
U\in\mathcal{C}^{(2)}

and
c\in\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(U)
.
Fix a prime
p

and a compact open set
\mathfrak{o}_{p}\subseteq U(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Then there exists an integer
A_{7}=A_{7}(U,c,p,\mathfrak{o}_{p})\geq 1

such that the following hold for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in 1+p^{A_{7}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
:

(1)

The map
\phi\colon(a,b)\mapsto(ax_{1},c+(b-c)x_{2})
on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

maps
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap\mathfrak{o}_{p}
to itself.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{j}\in J
, then
H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}(g)=H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}(\phi(g))
for all
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap\mathfrak{o}_{p}
.

Proof.

Given a point
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}_{p}
,
let
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}\cong p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}

denote a small compact open neighborhood of
x^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{o}_{p}
,
where
x^{\prime}
is identified with the origin
(0,0)\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}
.
Let
V\subseteq U_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
be an affine open set such that
for each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
there exists
\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}\in\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})

with
\operatorname{div}(\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}|_{V})=D_{\mathfrak{j}}|_{V}
.
Let
f_{20,1},\dots,f_{20,k}\in\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})
generate
\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})
as a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra.
Assume
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}\subseteq V(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
If
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}\cong p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}
is sufficiently small,
then for each finite extension
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
we may view
p\mathcal{O}_{E}^{2}
as an open neighborhood of
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}
in
V(E)
.
Let
B\subseteq\mathscr{X}

be as in the proof of Lemma
3.11
;
then
B(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is a finite set.
By shrinking
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}\cong p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}
if necessary,
we may assume that

p\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{2}\cap B(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\subseteq\{x^{\prime}\}.

That is, we assume that
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}

(and its neighborhood
p\mathcal{O}_{E}^{2}
for every
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
)
is disjoint from
B(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
,
except in the case where
x^{\prime}\in B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
to begin with.

Algebraically, view
\phi
now as a rational map
\mathbb{A}^{2}\times X\dashrightarrow X

defined by the formula

(x_{1},x_{2},a,b)\mapsto(ax_{1},c+(b-c)x_{2})

on
(a,b)\in G
.
A
p
-adic Lemma
3.6
(see Remark
3.7
)
shows that for each
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
the rational functions

(\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}})/(p\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}})

and
(f_{20,i}-\phi^{\ast}{f_{20,i}})/p
on
\mathbb{A}^{2}\times V

lie in
\Gamma_{p}((1+p^{m}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{2}\times(p^{l}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{2}\setminus p^{l+1}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{2}),\mathcal{O}_{E})
.
By the
N=2
case of Proposition
3.13
,
we get

(\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\phi^{\ast}{\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}})/(p\mathsf{t}_{\mathfrak{j}}),(f_{20,i}-\phi^{\ast}{f_{20,i}})/p\in\Gamma_{p}((1+p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{2}\times p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

for some
l=l(x^{\prime})\geq 1
.
In particular, if
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p}\cong p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\subseteq p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}\cong\mathfrak{o}^{\prime}_{p}
,
then
\phi
extends uniquely to an analytic map

\Phi\colon(1+p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{2}\times\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p}\to\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p}
,
since
((1+p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{2}\times\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p})\cap\phi^{-1}(V(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))

is dense in
(1+p^{l}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{2}\times\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p}
.
In addition,
by Definition
2.6
,
we have

H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}=\Phi^{\ast}{H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},p}}
on
(1+p^{l^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{2}\times\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p}

for some integer
l^{\prime}=l^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\geq l
.
So (1) and (2) hold with
A_{7}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\max_{x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}^{\ast}_{p}}l^{\prime}(x^{\prime})
,
where
\mathfrak{o}^{\ast}_{p}\subseteq\mathfrak{o}_{p}
is a finite set
of points
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{o}_{p}
whose associated neighborhoods
\mathfrak{o}^{\prime\prime}_{p}\ni x^{\prime}

cover
\mathfrak{o}_{p}
.
∎

4.
Non-archimedean local calculations

Recall the definition
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(a)

from §
2.3
.
Throughout §
4
,
assume
X
is split and
D
has strict normal crossings,
fix
\delta\in\operatorname{Int}(0,1)
and
A_{8}\in\operatorname{Int}(1,\infty)
,
and let
\bm{s}\in\mathbb{C}^{J}
be such that

(4.1)

-\delta\leq\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}-2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})\leq A_{8}\qquad\textnormal{for all $\mathfrak{j}\in J$}.

In particular,

\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)>0
for
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}
,
but not always for
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
.
This asymmetry turns out to be natural,
due to the integrality condition
\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in (
2.18
).
Ultimately, the translate
2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
in (
4.1
)
will ensure satisfactory bounds in
Lemmas
4.4
,

4.6
,
and
4.7
.

For any
\delta>0
, the region (
4.1
) goes beyond the region
\Omega^{\prime}_{\epsilon}
considered in
[
52
]
*Lemma 5.11.
Our eventual success thus relies on precise calculations, revealing leading-order structure like in
[
56
]
, that we carry out using
a new source of cancellation in the local integral (
2.18
): the nonconstancy identified in Proposition
3.5
, fed into
[
3
]
.

Let
(\lambda,\alpha)\in\mathbf{M}\times\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.
Over (
4.1
), it turns out that the most important factors
H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,t,\alpha)
in (
2.31
) are those with
p
large and
v_{p}(\alpha)
small but nonzero.
But we also need reasonable control on other factors.
To get our bearings,
we first prove a useful bound
(applicable to (
2.18
) after taking absolute values)
valid for arbitrary
p
and
\alpha
,
which does not require any subtle cancellations:

Lemma 4.1
.

Then
\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g_{p})\rvert^{-1}\,dg_{p}

is (for all
\epsilon>0
)

\ll_{p,\epsilon}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\epsilon}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{\epsilon}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}>1}+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\epsilon}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}<1}.

Proof.

Since
\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g)\rvert=H_{p}(\Re(\bm{s}),g)
, we may assume
\bm{s}
is real.
Let
U\subseteq X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be small open sets
\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}
forming a cover
\mathscr{C}_{p}
of
X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Writing
U\cap D\subseteq D_{\mathfrak{k}}\cup D_{\mathfrak{l}}

for some
\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l}\in J
depending on
U

(possible since
D
has strict normal crossings),
we get
7
7

7

In §
4
,
we let
dg\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}dg_{p}

and generally omit subscripts of
p
from variables and measures.

\int_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}):\,\mathsf{N}\alpha a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{dg}{\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g)\rvert}\ll_{p}\sum_{U\in\mathscr{C}_{p}}\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{(s_{\mathfrak{k}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{k}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{(s_{\mathfrak{l}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{l}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,dy\,dz,

where
\varsigma_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bm{1}_{U\cap D_{\mathfrak{j}}\neq\emptyset}
for
\mathfrak{j}\in\{\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l}\}
,
where
y
(resp.
z
) is a local parameter for
D_{\mathfrak{k}}
(resp.
D_{\mathfrak{l}}
) if
\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}=1
(resp.
\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}=1
),
and where
\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\ll_{p}\mathsf{N}
is a nonzero multiple of
\mathsf{N}
such that
the local analytic function
\mathsf{N}^{\prime}y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}/(\mathsf{N}a)
is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-valued.
Given
U
,
\mathfrak{k}
,
\mathfrak{l}
, call the integral on the right
I(U,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l})
.
The contribution to
I(U,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l})
from
\lvert y\rvert_{p}=Y
and
\lvert z\rvert_{p}=Z
,
where
Y,Z\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
is

(1-p^{-1})^{2}\cdot f_{21}(Y,Z)\cdot\bm{1}_{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha)},

where
f_{21}(Y,Z)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}Y^{(s_{\mathfrak{k}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{k}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}+1}Z^{(s_{\mathfrak{l}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{l}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}+1}

and for any set
R\subseteq\mathbb{R}
we let

(4.2)

\mathfrak{S}_{R}(\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{(Y,Z)\in(0,1]^{2}:\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\,Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\leq 1,\;(\log_{p}{Y},\log_{p}{Z})\in R^{2}\}.

We now apply the
(d,e)=(2,3)
case of Lemma
4.2
below.
(In particular,
V=\mathbb{R}^{2}
.)
Take half-planes
\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},\hbar_{3}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
corresponding to
the half-regions
\{Y\leq 1\}
,
\{Z\leq 1\}
, and
\{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\,Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\leq 1\}
in
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{2}
.
Then
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{R}=\mathfrak{S}_{R}(\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha)
.
Using (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) if
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
, or the trivial fact that
I(U,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l})=0
if
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}=\emptyset
, we find that

I(U,\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l})\ll_{\epsilon}(\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-1/\epsilon}+(\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p})^{\epsilon}\cdot(M_{1}+M_{2}+M_{3}),

where
M_{1}=f_{22}(1,1)
,

M_{2}=f_{22}(\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}},1)\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}\neq 0}
,
and
M_{3}=f_{22}(1,\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}})\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}\neq 0}
,
where
f_{22}(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f_{21}(x)\bm{1}_{x\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha)}
.
Here
M_{1}=\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}
.
Also,
M_{2}
(resp.
M_{3}
) equals

\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\geq 1}+\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1})\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{j}}\neq 0}

for
\mathfrak{j}=\mathfrak{k}
(resp.
\mathfrak{j}=\mathfrak{l}
).
This suffices, since
\lvert\mathscr{C}_{p}\rvert,\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\ll_{p}1
(and
\bm{s}\ll 1
) and

\begin{split}(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-1/\epsilon}&\leq\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}+\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{i}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}>1},\\
\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}&\leq\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}+\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{i}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}>1},\\
\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\geq 1}&\leq\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}=1}+\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{i}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}>1},\\
\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{i}}\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{N}^{\prime}\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}&\ll_{p}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1}+\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{i}}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}>1}+\sum_{i\in J_{2}}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}\bm{1}_{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}<1}.\end{split}

(These final displayed inequalities follow from (
4.1
) and the fact
J_{1}\neq\emptyset
.)
∎

The goal of the following lemma
is to bound a certain class of
p
-adic integrals
via linear programming over a suitable region in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume
\bm{s}
is real.
Let
\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l}\in J
.
Let
\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}},\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}\in\{0,1\}
.
Let
\mathfrak{S}_{R}(\alpha)
be as in (
4.2
).
Let
d,e\geq 1
be integers.
Let
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
be an affine space of dimension
d
.
Let
\hbar_{1},\dots,\hbar_{e}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
be closed half-planes,
whose boundaries are the lines
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{e}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
, respectively.
Let

\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{(Y,Z)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{2}:(\log_{p}{Y},\log_{p}{Z})\in R^{2}\cap V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e}\}.

Assume
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}(\alpha)

and
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
.
Then
\max_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}}{f_{21}}
exists,
and for all
\epsilon>0
we have

(4.3)

\int_{(\lvert y\rvert_{p},\lvert z\rvert_{p})\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{(s_{\mathfrak{k}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{k}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{(s_{\mathfrak{l}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{l}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\,dy\,dz\ll_{\epsilon}(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-1/\epsilon}+(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{\epsilon}\max_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}}{f_{21}}.

For any set
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\{1,\dots,e\}
,
let
\ell_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcap_{e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}}\ell_{e^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Call
\mathcal{E}

extreme
if

\begin{split}(V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e})\cap\ell_{\mathcal{E}}&\neq\emptyset,\\
(V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e})\cap\ell_{\mathcal{E}\cup\{e^{\prime\prime}\}}&=\emptyset,\end{split}

for all
e^{\prime\prime}\in\{1,\dots,e\}\setminus\mathcal{E}
.
Then

(4.4)

\max_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}}{f_{21}}\leq\sum_{\textnormal{extreme }\mathcal{E}}f_{21}(p^{x_{\mathcal{E}}}),

for some choice of points

x_{\mathcal{E}}\in V\cap\ell_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{S}_{R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{S}_{R}(\alpha)
for convenience.
We first prove that if
L\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}

with
L\leq f_{21}\leq 2L
, then

(4.5)

\log_{p}{Y},\log_{p}{Z}\ll_{\epsilon}(1+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+L^{-1})^{\epsilon}.

Order
\mathfrak{k}
,
\mathfrak{l}
so that
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}\geq\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}
.
We prove (
4.5
) by casework.

Case 1:
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}<0
.

Then
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}=\emptyset
unless
\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1
.
Also,
\log_{p}{Y},\log_{p}{Z}\ll\lvert v_{p}(\alpha)\rvert
for
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
The claim (
4.5
) follows,
since if
\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\leq 1
,
then

\lvert v_{p}(\alpha)\rvert=\log_{p}(1/\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})\ll\log(1/\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})\ll_{\epsilon}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\epsilon}.

Case 2:
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}\geq 0
.

Then
(s_{\mathfrak{k}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{k}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}\geq-\delta
,
since either
\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}=0
or
\mathfrak{k}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}
.
Now suppose
f_{21}\asymp L
for some
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}
;
then
Y\asymp L^{e_{1}}/Z^{e_{2}}

and
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\asymp L^{e_{3}}/Z^{e_{4}}
,
where

e_{1}=((s_{\mathfrak{k}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{k}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}+1)^{-1},\;e_{2}=((s_{\mathfrak{l}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{l}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}+1)e_{1},\;e_{3}=\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}e_{1},\;e_{4}=\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}e_{2}-\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}.

If
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}\geq 0
,
then
(s_{\mathfrak{l}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{l}})\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}\geq-\delta
,
so the constraint
Y\leq 1
in
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}

implies
Z\gg L^{e_{1}/e_{2}}
,
where
0<\frac{e_{1}}{e_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}
.
Meanwhile, if
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}<0
, then
\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}=1
and (using (
4.1
) for
\mathfrak{j}\in\{\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l}\}
)

\begin{split}\tfrac{e_{4}}{e_{1}}=\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}\tfrac{e_{2}}{e_{1}}+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\rvert e_{1}^{-1}&\geq\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}+1-\delta)+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\rvert((2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}-\delta)\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}+1)\\
&=\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}(1-\delta)+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\rvert(-\delta\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}+1)\\
&\geq\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}(1-\delta)+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\rvert(1-\delta)=(\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\rvert)(1-\delta),\end{split}

so the constraint
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\leq\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}

implies
Z\gg\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{1/e_{4}}L^{e_{3}/e_{4}}
,
where
0<\frac{1}{e_{4}}\ll\frac{1}{e_{1}}\ll A_{8}

and
0\leq\frac{e_{3}}{e_{4}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}
.
Either way, we now have a lower bound on
Z
,
which together with the upper bound
Z\leq 1

and the estimate
Y\asymp L^{e_{1}}/Z^{e_{2}}

gives (
4.5
).

Having established (
4.5
) (in both Case 1 and Case 2),
we now use our assumptions on
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}

to prove the existence of
\max_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}}{f_{21}}
,
and the inequality (
4.4
).
The function
\log{f_{21}}
is linear (hence convex)
in
(\log_{p}{Y},\log_{p}{Z})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
However, the closed set
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
might not be bounded.
To address this, let
M\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

and note that by the
L\geq M
case of (
4.5
),
we have

\{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}:f_{21}\geq M\}\subseteq\{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}:\log_{p}{Y},\log_{p}{Z}\ll_{\epsilon}(1+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+M^{-1})^{\epsilon}\}.

Since
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
it follows that for every
M\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, the closed set

\{x\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}:f_{21}(x)\geq M\}

is bounded, and therefore compact.
Choose a point
x_{0}\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}

(possible since
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
),
and let
M=f_{21}(x_{0})/2>0
.
Then
f_{21}
achieves a maximum
M_{\star}\geq f_{21}(x_{0})=2M>M

on the set
\{x\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}:f_{21}(x)\geq M\}
,
which must then be a global maximum over the set
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover, recall the definition of
\argmax(\cdot)
from (
3.6
).
By convexity of
\log{f_{21}}
, the set
\argmax_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}}{f_{21}}

contains a vertex
x^{\prime}
of
\{x\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}:f_{21}\geq M\}
,
with
f_{21}(x^{\prime})=M_{\star}\neq M
.
(Here we think of

\{x\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}:f_{21}\geq M\}

as the image under
\exp\colon\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{2}

of a compact polytope.)

We have
\log_{p}(x^{\prime})\in\log_{p}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}})=(V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e})\cap\ell_{\emptyset}
, so there exists a set
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\{1,\dots,e\}
such that
\log_{p}(x^{\prime})\in(V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e})\cap\ell_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Take
\mathcal{E}
to have maximal cardinality among such sets.
Then
\log_{p}(x^{\prime})\in\hbar_{e^{\prime}}\setminus\ell_{e^{\prime}}

for all
e^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,e\}\setminus\mathcal{E}
.
Suppose for contradiction that for some
e^{\prime\prime}\in\{1,\dots,e\}\setminus\mathcal{E}
,
the set
(V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e})\cap\ell_{\mathcal{E}\cup\{e^{\prime\prime}\}}

contains a point, say
\log_{p}(x^{\prime\prime})
.
Then for all
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
sufficiently small (not necessarily positive),
the point

\log_{p}(x^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(1-\varepsilon)\log_{p}(x^{\prime})+\varepsilon\log_{p}(x^{\prime\prime})

lies in
(V\cap\hbar_{1}\cap\dots\cap\hbar_{e})\cap\ell_{\mathcal{E}}

and satisfies
f_{21}(x^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon})>M
,
so
x^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\in\{x\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}:f_{21}\geq M\}
.
Since
x^{\prime}
is a vertex,
this can only happen if
x^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}=x^{\prime}
,
i.e.
\log_{p}(x^{\prime\prime})=\log_{p}(x^{\prime})
.
But
\mathcal{E}
is maximal, so
x^{\prime\prime}\neq x^{\prime}
.
Thus our assumption was wrong,
so in fact
\mathcal{E}
is extreme,
giving (
4.4
).

Finally, we turn to (
4.3
).
Call the integral
I
.
Then

I=(1-p^{-1})^{2}\sum_{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}}f_{21}(Y,Z)=(1-p^{-1})^{2}\sum_{\log_{2}{L}\in\mathbb{Z}}L\cdot\#\{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}:L\leq f_{21}<2L\}

by summing over level sets of
f_{21}
, a la Lebesgue.
By (
4.5
),
since
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
we have

\#\{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}:L\leq f_{21}<2L\}\ll_{\epsilon}(1+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+L^{-1})^{\epsilon}\ll_{\epsilon}1+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\epsilon}+L^{-\epsilon},

where the last step follows from the estimate

1+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+L^{-1}\asymp\max(1,\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},L^{-1})
.
So

I\ll_{\epsilon}\sum_{\log_{2}{L}\in\mathbb{Z}}(L+L\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\epsilon}+L^{1-\epsilon})\ll_{\epsilon}M_{\star}+M_{\star}\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-\epsilon}+M_{\star}^{1-\epsilon},

by summing geometric series over
L\ll M_{\star}
.
Finally, note that
\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})\geq 1
.
If
M_{\star}\geq\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-1/\epsilon^{1/2}}
,
then
M_{\star}^{1-\epsilon}\leq\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{\epsilon^{1/2}}M_{\star}
,
so
I\ll_{\epsilon}\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{\epsilon^{1/2}}M_{\star}
.
If
M_{\star}\leq\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-1/\epsilon^{1/2}}
,
then
I\ll_{\epsilon}\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-0.9/\epsilon^{1/2}}
.
Either way, we have

I\ll_{\epsilon}\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-0.9/\epsilon^{1/2}}+\max(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1},\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{\epsilon^{1/2}}M_{\star}.

Replacing
\epsilon
with
\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{2}
, we conclude that (
4.3
) holds.
∎

For subsequent calculations,
we choose
c(\mathfrak{j})\in\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})

and let
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{ord}_{D_{\mathfrak{j}}}(b-c(\mathfrak{j}))

for
\mathfrak{j}\in J
.
Also, we make the following definition for convenience in §
5
(when we apply Lemma
4.4
).

Definition 4.3
.

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}
be a finite set containing
the set
\mathsf{S}
defined after Lemma
2.12
,
such that

\{q\in\mathbb{Q}:I^{q}\neq\emptyset\}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[1/\overline{\mathsf{S}}]

and
\{q_{1}-q_{2}:q_{1},q_{2}\in\mathbb{Q},\;I^{q_{1}},I^{q_{2}}\neq\emptyset\}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[1/\overline{\mathsf{S}}]^{\times}\cup\{0\}
,
where
I^{q}
is the set defined in (
3.3
).

Lemma 4.4

(Denominator bias)
.

Suppose
v_{p}(\alpha)=-k<0
.
Let

\mathfrak{D}=\mathfrak{D}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)-\bm{1}_{p\notin\overline{\mathsf{S}}}\sum_{c\in\mathbb{Q}}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c}_{1}}\mathsf{e}(-c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})p^{-k(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}.

Then
\mathfrak{D}\ll_{\epsilon}p^{(\epsilon-2)k}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}(p^{\delta-1}+p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+\bm{1}_{k>\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}})p^{(\delta-1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}
.

Proof.

Here
\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}=p^{k}
, so by Lemma
4.1

(and the bound
p^{-k\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\leq p^{-2k}p^{(\delta-1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}
for
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
,
using
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}>0
),
we may assume
p
is large.
Following
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.6, let
\rho
be the reduction map
X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
;
then
H^{\vee}_{p}=\sum_{x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})
, where

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\rho^{-1}(x_{0}):\,\alpha a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}H_{p}(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\mathsf{e}(-\alpha b\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(\alpha a)\,dg.

Let
\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})
be the contribution to
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})
from
\alpha a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, and let
\mathcal{I}^{1}_{p}(x_{0})
be the contribution from
\alpha a\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
; then
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})+\mathcal{I}^{1}_{p}(x_{0})
, and we have

\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})=\int_{\rho^{-1}(x_{0}):\,\alpha a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\alpha b\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\,dg}{H_{p}(\bm{s},g)},\quad\lvert\mathcal{I}^{1}_{p}(x_{0})\rvert\leq\int_{\rho^{-1}(x_{0}):\,\alpha a\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\frac{dg}{\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g)\rvert}.

For each
\mathfrak{j}\in J
,
let
U_{\mathfrak{j}}\subseteq X
be the largest open set on which
a,b-c(\mathfrak{j})\in\Gamma(U_{\mathfrak{j}}\setminus D_{\mathfrak{j}},\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\times}
.
Let
\mathscr{Z}_{\mathfrak{j}}
be the closure of
X\setminus U_{\mathfrak{j}}
in
\mathscr{X}
.
Then let
\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\setminus\mathscr{Z}_{\mathfrak{j}}
.
We claim the following:

(1)

If
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
, then

\sum_{x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=(\mathsf{e}(-c(\mathfrak{j})\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c(\mathfrak{j})}_{1}}+O(p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k})+O(p^{-1}))\cdot p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}.

(2)

If
x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll_{\epsilon}p^{(\epsilon-2)k}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}:\,x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}(p^{\delta-1}+\bm{1}_{k>\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}})p^{(\delta-1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}.

These two claims would imply the lemma,
since on the one hand

\biggl\lvert{H^{\vee}-\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\sum_{x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})}\biggr\rvert\leq\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\sum_{x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\setminus\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}})(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\lvert\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\rvert\ll\max_{x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\lvert\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\rvert,

which may be bounded using (2);
and on the other hand for each
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
, by (1),

\begin{split}\sum_{x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})&=\mathsf{e}(-c(\mathfrak{j})\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c(\mathfrak{j})}_{1}}p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}+O(p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+p^{-1})p^{-2k}p^{(\delta-1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\\
&=\sum_{c\in\mathbb{Q}}\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c}_{1}}\mathsf{e}(-c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})p^{-k(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+O(p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+p^{-1})p^{-2k}p^{(\delta-1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}},\end{split}

where the last equality follows from Proposition
3.3
.
Indeed, if
\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c}_{1}
for some
c\in\mathbb{Q}
,
then
\mathcal{C}^{(0)}(\mathfrak{j})=\{c\}

(by Proposition
3.3

and the containment
J^{c}_{1}\subseteq I^{c}
from (
3.4
)),
so
c(\mathfrak{j})=c
.

We prove (1) first.
Suppose
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
and
c(\mathfrak{j})=c
, and let
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
By Lemma
3.8
,
the set
\rho^{-1}(x_{0})\subseteq X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

has analytic coordinates
y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}

such that
a=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}

and
b-c=y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\mathsf{w}_{2}+z)
,
where
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
Upon writing

dg=\lvert\omega\rvert_{p}/(1-p^{-1})
in terms of
d\tau_{p}

using (
2.8
),
as in
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.6, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)=k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\mathsf{e}(-c\alpha-y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\mathsf{w}_{2}+z)\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\,\frac{dy\,dz}{1-p^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{e}(-c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\int_{y\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)=k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1}\mathsf{e}(-y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\mathsf{w}_{2}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\frac{\bm{1}_{y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}{p}\,\frac{dy/\lvert y\rvert_{p}}{1-p^{-1}}.

Thus
p\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})=p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\mathsf{e}(-c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}
if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\geq\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
;
and if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}<\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
, then summing over
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
,
y\in p^{k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
(integrating over
y
first if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\neq 0
, and
x_{0}
first if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}=0
) gives

\sum_{x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}p\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})\ll p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}\cdot p^{1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}=k-1}

(by the bound
[
3
]
*Theorem 6 on exponential sums over curves;
this applies because
\operatorname{sgn}_{p}(y)^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\bmod{p}
is nonconstant if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\neq 0
, and if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}=0
then
\mathsf{w}_{2}\equiv b-c\bmod{p}
is nonconstant by Proposition
3.5
).
Furthermore, if
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\geq\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
, then (by Proposition
1.4
)
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}=\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
and
\mathfrak{j}\in J^{c}_{1}
.
On the other hand, for
\mathcal{I}^{1}_{p}(x_{0})
, summing a geometric series over
v_{p}(y)\in\mathbb{Z}
yields

\mathcal{I}^{1}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)\geq(k+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\,dy\,dz\leq\frac{p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)(k+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}}{1-p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)}}\cdot\frac{1}{p}\ll\frac{p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}}{p^{3}},

since
\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)\geq 2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
.
Finally, summing
\mathcal{I}^{0}_{p}(x_{0})
,
\mathcal{I}^{1}_{p}(x_{0})

over
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
using the Lang–Weil estimate
delivers (1), since
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
is geometrically irreducible.

We now prove claim (2).
If
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\setminus\bigcup_{i\in J\setminus\{\mathfrak{j}\}}\mathscr{D}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
, then
