\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)\geq k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\,dy\,dz\ll p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{-1}\leq p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{-1},

which suffices.
Similarly, if
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
and
i\in J_{3}
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)\geq k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i})}\,dy\,dz\ll p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{-\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)},

which suffices since
\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)\geq 2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1
and
\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)\geq 1-\delta
.

The remaining cases are messy to do explicitly,
so we now apply Lemma
4.2
.
Initially, we take

(\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{l},\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}},\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}},d,e)=(\mathfrak{j},i,1,1,2,3)
,
with half-planes
\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},\hbar_{3}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
corresponding to
the half-regions
\{Y\leq p^{-1}\}
,
\{Z\leq p^{-1}\}
, and
\{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}\,Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{k}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{k}}}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{l}}\varsigma_{\mathfrak{l}}}\leq 1\}
in
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{2}
,
so that
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{R}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{R}(\alpha)
.
If
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some distinct
\mathfrak{j},i\in J_{1}
, then (
4.3
) gives
8
8

8

The pesky term
(\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}^{-1}+\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p})^{-1/\epsilon}\leq p^{-k/\epsilon}
in (
4.3
) is negligible, so we do not mention it further.

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\\
y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\in p^{k}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i})}\,dy\,dz\ll_{\epsilon}p^{k\epsilon}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\leq p^{-k}\end{subarray}}{Y^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)}Z^{\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)}},

which by (
4.4
) is
\leq p^{k\epsilon}p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)\max(1,(k-\mathsf{u}_{i})/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})}p^{-\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)}
(assuming
\argmax({\cdots})\cap\{Z=p^{-1}\}\neq\emptyset
; otherwise, switch
\mathfrak{j}
,
i
),
which is in turn (using
\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)\geq(1-\delta)+2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}
)

(4.6)

\leq p^{k\epsilon}p^{-(1-\delta)\max(1,(k-\mathsf{u}_{i})/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})(k-\mathsf{u}_{i})/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1)}=p^{k\epsilon}p^{-(1-\delta)\max(2,(k+\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{u}_{i})/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})}p^{-2k};

this suffices, since
\max(2,\frac{k+\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{u}_{i}}{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}})\geq\bm{1}_{k\leq\max(\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}},\mathsf{u}_{i})}+\frac{k}{\max(\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}},\mathsf{u}_{i})}
by Lemma
4.5
below.
If
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
and
i\in J_{2}
, then (
4.3
) gives

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\\
y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\in p^{k}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i})}\,dy\,dz\ll_{\epsilon}p^{k\epsilon}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\leq p^{-k}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}\end{subarray}}{Y^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)}Z^{\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)}};

but the conditions
Z\leq p^{-1}
and
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\leq p^{-k}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}
together imply
Y<1
, say,
so if we extend the half-region
\{Y\leq p^{-1}\}
to
\{Y\leq 1\}

before applying (
4.4
), then we find that

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\leq p^{-k}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}\end{subarray}}({\cdots})\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\leq 1,\;Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\leq p^{-k}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}\end{subarray}}({\cdots})=({\cdots})|_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z=p^{-1},\\
Y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}=p^{-k}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}\end{subarray}}=p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)(k+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{-\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i}+1)},

which is
\leq p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)(k+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1)}=p^{-2k}p^{(\delta-1)(k+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{\delta-1}\leq p^{-2k}p^{(\delta-1)k/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p^{\delta-1}
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
k,l,m\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
Then
\max(1,\frac{k+m-l}{m})\geq\min(\frac{k}{l},\frac{k}{m})
.

Proof.

If
l\leq m
, then

\frac{k+m-l}{m}\geq\frac{k}{m}
.
If
k\leq l
, then

1\geq\frac{k}{l}
.
If
k>l>m
,
then
\frac{k+m-l}{m}>\frac{k}{l}
.
∎

Lemma 4.6

(Numerator bias)
.

Suppose
v_{p}(\alpha)=k>0
.
Let

\mathfrak{N}=\mathfrak{N}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)-\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J^{\ast}_{2}}p^{-k(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}.

Then
\mathfrak{N}\ll_{\epsilon}p^{(2+\epsilon)k}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}(p^{\delta-1}+p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+\bm{1}_{k>\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert})p^{(\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}
.

Proof.

Here
\lvert\alpha\rvert_{p}=p^{-k}
, so by Lemma
4.1
and the bound
1+p^{-k\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\leq 2p^{2k}p^{(\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}
(and the fact
J_{2}\neq\emptyset
), we may assume
p
is large.
Define
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})
and
\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}
as before.
Then
H^{\vee}_{p}=\sum_{x_{0}\in\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})
.
As noted in
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.8, we have

\sum_{x_{0}\notin\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\lvert\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\rvert\leq\sum_{x_{0}\notin\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\int_{\rho^{-1}(x_{0})}\lvert H_{p}(\bm{s},g)\rvert^{-1}\,dg\ll 1;

this is because
\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)\geq 1-\delta>0
for
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}
.
This
O(1)
bound is satisfactory, since
J_{2}\neq\emptyset
and
1\leq p^{2k}p^{\delta-1}p^{(\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}
.
Thus it remains to study
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})
for
x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
We claim the following, of which the lemma is a direct consequence:

(1)

If
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
and
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then

p\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}\bm{1}_{\mathfrak{j}\in J^{\ast}_{2}}+p^{(2\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert+\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}O(p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+p^{-1}).

(2)

If
x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll_{\epsilon}p^{(2+\epsilon)k}\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}:\,x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}(p^{\delta-1}+\bm{1}_{k>\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert})p^{(\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}.

For (1),
suppose
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
and
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
Then by Lemma
3.8
,
the set
\rho^{-1}(x_{0})
has analytic coordinates
y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}

with
a=\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}

and
b-c(\mathfrak{j})=y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\mathsf{w}_{2}+z)
,
where
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
Since
c(\mathfrak{j})\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, it follows that

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\mathsf{e}(-y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}(\mathsf{w}_{2}+z)\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(\alpha y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}})\,\frac{dy\,dz}{1-p^{-1}}.

For any given
y
, the integral over
z
vanishes unless
y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}p\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, i.e.
v_{p}(y)\leq(k+1)/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert
;
here
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\leq\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}<0
by Proposition
1.4
, since
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
.
Integrating over the ranges
v_{p}(y)=(k+1)/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert

(i.e.
y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\alpha\in p^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
)
and
v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert
(i.e.
y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
) separately, we then get

\displaystyle p\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=p\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,(k+1)/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert=v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}({\cdots})+p\int_{y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\,v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}({\cdots})

\displaystyle=p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)(k+1)/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}O(p^{-1/2})\bm{1}_{\mathbb{Z}\ni(k+1)/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}+\sum_{1\leq r\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)r}\lambda_{\mathsf{S},p}(p^{k-r\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}});

the
O(p^{-1/2})
factor comes from cancellation over
y
,
occurring since
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\neq 0
and
\mathsf{w}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
Since
-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)\leq 2\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert+\delta-1
, and any integer
<k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert
is
\leq(k-1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert
, we get

p\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}=\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}+p^{(2\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert+\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}O(p^{-1/2}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\mid k}+p^{-(2\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert+\delta-1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}).

This implies (1), since
2\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert+\delta-1\geq\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert
.

We now turn to (2).
If
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
and
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\setminus\bigcup_{i\in J\setminus\{\mathfrak{j}\}}\mathscr{D}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}:\,v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\,dy\,dz\leq\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}:\,v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta}\,dy\,dz\ll\frac{p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}}{p},

which suffices.
Similarly, if
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
and
i\in J_{3}
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}:\,v_{p}(y)\leq k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta}\,dy\,dz\ll p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}p^{-(1-\delta)}\quad(\textnormal{since $\mathsf{u}_{i}=0$}).

We treat the remaining cases by Lemma
4.2
,
with
(d,e)=(2,3)
initially.
If
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some distinct
\mathfrak{j},i\in J_{2}
,
then
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=0
unless
k\geq\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert
, in which case

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\\
p^{k}y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta}\,dy\,dz\ll_{\epsilon}p^{k\epsilon}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}\geq p^{-k}\end{subarray}}{Y^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1}Z^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1}}

by (
4.3
),
where
\argmax({\cdots})\subseteq\{Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}=p^{-k}\}

by (
4.4
)
(since if
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}Z^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}>p^{-k}
,
then
Y^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1}Z^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1}

can be increased by decreasing
Y
or
Z
)
and thus, after switching
\mathfrak{j}
,
i
if necessary so that
\argmax({\cdots})\cap\{Z=p^{-1}\}\neq\emptyset
, we have

\max({\cdots})=p^{(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)(\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert-k)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1)}=p^{2k}p^{(\delta-1)(k+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert-\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}

(cf. (
4.6
));
here
\frac{k+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert-\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}=\max(2,\frac{k+\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert-\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert}{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert})\geq\bm{1}_{k\leq\max(\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert,\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert)}+\frac{k}{\max(\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert,\lvert\mathsf{u}_{i}\rvert)}
by Lemma
4.5
.
If
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
and
i\in J_{1}
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}:\\
p^{k}y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta}\,dy\,dz\ll_{\epsilon}p^{k\epsilon}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\geq p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\end{subarray}}{Y^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1}Z^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1}}

by (
4.3
),
where (after decreasing
Y
if necessary,
which will only increase
Y^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1}
)

\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}\geq p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\end{subarray}}({\cdots})=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y,Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}=p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\end{subarray}}({\cdots})\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\leq 1,\;Z\leq p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}=p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\end{subarray}}({\cdots})=({\cdots})|_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z=p^{-1}:\\
Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}=p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\end{subarray}}

(by the
(d,e)=(1,2)
case of (
4.4
),
9
9

9

We take the affine line
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
corresponding to the curve
\{Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}=p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\}
in
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{2}
,
and we take
\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
corresponding to
\{Y\leq 1\}
and
\{Z\leq p^{-1}\}
in
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{2}
.
Note that
\emptyset\neq\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}_{R}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{R}(\alpha)
.

since
\{Z\leq p^{-1}\}\cap\{Y^{\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}=p^{-k}Z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\}\subseteq\{Y<1\}
),
which is

=p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)(k+\mathsf{u}_{i})/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta+1)}=p^{2k}p^{(\delta-1)(k+\mathsf{u}_{i})/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}p^{\delta-1}\leq p^{2k}p^{(\delta-1)k/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}p^{\delta-1}.

This completes the proof of (2).
∎

We now handle the “generic” case of
p
coprime to the numerator and denominator of
\alpha
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose
v_{p}(\alpha)=0
.
Let

\mathfrak{G}=\mathfrak{G}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)-1.

Then
\mathfrak{G}\ll p^{2(\delta-1)}
.

Proof.

For small
p
, use Lemma
4.1
(which gives the satisfactory bound
\mathfrak{G}\ll_{p}1
).
For large
p
, we roughly follow
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.10.
Define
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})
and
\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}
as before.
Since
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=p^{-2}/(1-p^{-1})=1/\lvert\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\rvert
for all
x_{0}\notin\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, we have

H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)-1=\sum_{x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=\sum_{x_{0}\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0}),

since
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})=0
for all
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\setminus\bigcup_{i\in J_{1}}\mathscr{D}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
if
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{2}
.
We claim the following, which readily imply the lemma (by summing over
\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}
first, and then
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\setminus\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}
separately):

(1)

If
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}
and
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll p^{\delta-3}
.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}
and
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, then
\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll p^{2\delta-2}
.

For (2),
we integrate absolutely.
If
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\setminus\bigcup_{i\in J\setminus\{\mathfrak{j}\}}\mathscr{D}_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}\cup J_{3}
, then

(4.7)

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\,dy\,dz\leq\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta}\,dy\,dz\ll p^{-(2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta+1)}p^{-1}=p^{\delta-2-2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}},

which is
\leq p^{\delta-2}
since
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\geq 0
.
If
x_{0}\in(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}\cap\mathscr{D}_{i})(\mathbb{F}_{p})
for some distinct
\mathfrak{j},i\in J
, then

\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})\ll\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}:\,y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{\Re(s_{i}-\mathsf{d}_{i})}\,dy\,dz\leq\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}:\,y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\delta}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{2\mathsf{u}_{i}-\delta}\,dy\,dz,

which (since
\lvert y^{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}z^{\mathsf{u}_{i}}\rvert_{p}\leq 1
) is

\leq\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{-\delta}\lvert z\rvert_{p}^{-\delta}\,dy\,dz\ll p^{2\delta-2}
.
Thus (2) holds.

Yet (1) requires cancellation in some cases.
Suppose
x_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{j}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
Claim (1) is clear by (
4.7
) if
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}
, because then
\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}\geq 1
.
On the other hand, if
\mathfrak{j}\in J_{3}
, then
Lemma
3.8
gives

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{p}(x_{0})&=\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\,\frac{dy\,dz}{1-p^{-1}}\\
&=\int_{p\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\lvert y\rvert_{p}^{s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1}\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{w}_{1}y^{\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\,\frac{dy/\lvert y\rvert_{p}}{p-1}=\frac{p^{-(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)}}{p-1}\cdot\frac{-\bm{1}_{\lvert\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert=1}}{p}\ll p^{-\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}+3)}\end{split}

(since
c(\mathfrak{j})\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
\mathsf{v}_{\mathfrak{j}}<0
),
which suffices for (1) since
\Re(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})\geq-\delta
.
∎

Aside from the
estimates
above, it is essential for us to have a local
constancy
result:

Lemma 4.8
.

There exists
A_{9}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, independent of
p
,
such that if

\alpha^{\prime}/\alpha\equiv 1\bmod{p^{\max(1,-v_{p}(\alpha))}A_{9}\mathbb{Z}_{p}},

then
\mathfrak{F}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)=\mathfrak{F}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha^{\prime})
,
where
\mathfrak{F}=\mathfrak{D}\cdot\bm{1}_{v_{p}(\alpha)<0}+\mathfrak{N}\cdot\bm{1}_{v_{p}(\alpha)>0}+\mathfrak{G}\cdot\bm{1}_{v_{p}(\alpha)=0}
.

Proof.

Let
A_{9}\geq 1
be a highly divisible integer.
By Lemmas
3.11
and
3.14
and Proposition
3.10
, we may partition
X(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
into compact open sets
\mathfrak{o}_{c,p}
(indexed by a finite set of
c\in\mathbb{Q}
) such that
(1)
\mathfrak{o}_{c,p}
is invariant under an action of
x_{1},x_{2}\in 1+pA_{9}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
that is defined on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by scaling
a
,
b-c
by
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, respectively;
and (2)
H_{p}(\bm{s},g)
is invariant under this group action.
So by (
2.18
), we may decompose
H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)
(for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
) as a sum of the form
\sum_{c}\mathsf{e}(c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})f_{23,c,p}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)
, where
f_{23,c,p}
is invariant under scaling of
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
by
1+pA_{9}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Since
\mathsf{e}(c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
depends only on
c\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, the desired lemma follows.

(Previously, a similar but more explicit non-archimedean symmetry argument has played an essential role in the circle method over function fields; see
[
28
]
*Lemma 3.6.)
∎

5.
Archimedean endgame

In this section, assume
X
is split and
D
has strict normal crossings.
For convenience, let

\mathbf{u}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{div}(a)=\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\in\mathbb{R}^{J},\quad\mathbf{d}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-\operatorname{div}(\omega)=\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}}D_{\mathfrak{j}}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}.

Let
C^{\infty}(X(\mathbb{R}))
be the set of smooth functions
X(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{C}

on the (possibly non-orientable) closed manifold
X(\mathbb{R})
.
The
left-invariant
differential operators
a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial a}
and
a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}
play an important role in
[
52
]
*proof of Lemma 5.9, but the (non-invariant) operators
(b-c)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b}
help us too.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
f_{24}\in C^{\infty}(X(\mathbb{R}))
.
Suppose
f_{24}
is supported on a compact set
K\subseteq U(\mathbb{R})

for some
U\in\mathcal{C}^{(2)}

(see Proposition
3.10
),
and choose
c\in\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(U)
.

(1)

The function
((b-c)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b})^{k_{1}}(a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial a})^{k_{2}}(a\,\frac{\partial}{\partial b})^{k_{3}}f_{24}|_{G(\mathbb{R})}

extends to a function in
C^{\infty}(X(\mathbb{R}))
.

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{j}\in J
.
The function

H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},\infty}(a,b)\cdot((b-c)\,\tfrac{\partial}{\partial b})^{k_{1}}(a\,\tfrac{\partial}{\partial a})^{k_{2}}(a\,\tfrac{\partial}{\partial b})^{k_{3}}[f_{24}(a,b)H_{D_{\mathfrak{j}},\infty}(a,b)^{-1}]

on
G(\mathbb{R})

extends to a function in
C^{\infty}(X(\mathbb{R}))
.

Proof.

(1):
Let
x\in K
.
By the chain rule, it suffices to prove that the derivatives of
local coordinates
\mathsf{t},\upsilon\in\operatorname{Rat}(X)
near
x

are regular.
By algebraic Hartogs, it suffices to prove regularity
away from a codimension
2
locus
(as in
[
9
]
*proof of Proposition 2.2).
Via analytic coordinates,
regularity then follows from Lemma
3.6

(applicable by Definition
3.9
).

(2):
By an induction with (1), we may assume
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=1
.
By the product rule,
together with (1) and Definition
2.6
,
it suffices to show that the derivative of
\mathsf{t}\in\operatorname{Rat}(X)
,
where
\mathsf{t}=0
is a local equation for
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
,
is divisible by
\mathsf{t}
.
It suffices to do this away from a codimension
2
locus.
Divisibility then follows from Lemma
3.6
.
∎

Via Proposition
3.10
,
cover

Then we have the decomposition

(5.1)

H^{\vee}_{\infty}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)=\sum_{\Omega}\mathcal{I}_{\Omega}=\sum_{\Omega}\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{0}\alpha)(\mathcal{J}_{\Omega,0}+\mathcal{J}_{\Omega,\infty}).

Lemma 5.2
.

For all
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
,

,

(\alpha\,\tfrac{\partial}{\partial\alpha})^{k_{2}}{\mathcal{J}_{\Omega}(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)}\ll_{k_{1},k_{2},A_{8},\delta}\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{2+k_{1}+2k_{2}}}{\alpha^{2}(1+\lvert t\rvert^{k_{1}})}.

The same estimate holds if we replace

with

or

.
(Here
\lVert\bm{s}\rVert\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\max_{\mathfrak{j}}{\lvert s_{\mathfrak{j}}\rvert}

Proof.

(The case

is essentially
[

]
*Lemma 5.9.
But differentiability and uniform control for

.
Nonetheless,

Writing

, etc.,
and applying (
2.1
)

\mathcal{I}_{\Omega}(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)=\int_{G(\mathbb{R})}w_{\Omega}(g)H_{\infty}(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\mathsf{e}(\alpha b)\lambda_{\infty}(\alpha a)\lvert a\rvert_{\infty}^{-it}\,dg.

Integrating by parts twice in
b
,
then
k_{1}+k_{2}
times in

,
gives (by Lemma

\mathcal{I}_{\Omega}(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)=\alpha^{-2}(2+it)^{-k_{1}-k_{2}}\int_{G(\mathbb{R})}w_{\Omega,1}(\bm{s},g)H_{\infty}(\bm{s},g)^{-1}\mathsf{e}(\alpha b)\lambda_{\infty}(\alpha a)\lvert a\rvert_{\infty}^{-2-it}\,dg

[

Letting

\mathcal{J}_{\Omega}(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)=\frac{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{\infty}^{it}}{(2+it)^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\int_{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in G(\mathbb{R})}\frac{w_{\Omega,1}(\bm{s},(a,b))}{H_{\infty}(\bm{s},(a,b))}\frac{\mathsf{e}(b^{\prime})\lambda_{\infty}(a^{\prime})}{\lvert a^{\prime}\rvert_{\infty}^{2+it}}\,\frac{db^{\prime}}{\lvert\alpha\rvert_{\infty}}\,\frac{da^{\prime}}{\lvert a^{\prime}\rvert_{\infty}},

where

is viewed as a function of
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime},\alpha)
.
Applying
\alpha\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}

k_{2}

and the chain rule identity

\alpha\,\frac{\partial\varphi(a,b)}{\partial\alpha}=\alpha\,\frac{\partial{a}}{\partial\alpha}\frac{\partial\varphi(a,b)}{\partial{a}}+\alpha\,\frac{\partial{b}}{\partial\alpha}\frac{\partial\varphi(a,b)}{\partial{b}}=-a\,\frac{\partial\varphi(a,b)}{\partial{a}}-(b-\mathsf{c}_{0})\,\frac{\partial\varphi(a,b)}{\partial{b}},

and then changing variables back from

(and applying Lemma

\Rightarrow

We now rewrite (
2.31
).
Plugging (

For each

For convenience,

\mathfrak{m}\colon\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{K}\}\to J^{\ast}_{2},\quad\mathfrak{n}\colon\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}\}\to\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}J^{q}_{1}

\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}q

and let

for all
\mathfrak{F}\in\{\mathfrak{N},\mathfrak{D},\mathfrak{G}\}
.
Write

,

Multiplying (

\prod_{p:\,v_{p}(\alpha)>0}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}}\geq 1:\\

m_{0}m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}=\alpha_{1}\end{subarray}}\,\frac{\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}},

m_{k}^{-\beta_{k}}

comes from the term

p^{-v_{p}(m_{k}^{\mathfrak{u}_{k}})(s_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}+1)/\lvert\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}\rvert}\bm{1}_{\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}\mid v_{p}(m_{k}^{\mathfrak{u}_{k}})}=p^{-v_{p}(m_{k})\beta_{k}}

in (
5.3
) for
p\mid m_{k}
.
Similarly, multiplying (
5.4
) over
p\mid\alpha_{2}
, we find that

(5.6)

\prod_{p:\,v_{p}(\alpha)<0}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1:\\
\textnormal{pairwise coprime},\\
p\mid n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}\Rightarrow p\notin\overline{\mathsf{S}},\\
n_{0}n_{1}^{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\mathfrak{L}}}=\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}\,\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}}.

Finally,

(5.7)

\prod_{p:\,v_{p}(\alpha)=0}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\geq 1:\\
\gcd(r,\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2})=1,\\
\textnormal{$r$ square-free}\end{subarray}}\,\mathfrak{G}_{r}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha).

Multiplying (
5.5
), (
5.6
), and (
5.7
)
together, we find that

\prod_{p}H^{\vee}_{p}(\bm{s},\lambda,0,\alpha)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1:\\
\textnormal{pairwise coprime},\\
\textnormal{$r$ square-free},\\
p\mid n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}\Rightarrow p\notin\overline{\mathsf{S}},\\
(m_{0}m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}})/(n_{0}n_{1}^{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\mathfrak{L}}})=\lvert\alpha\rvert\end{subarray}}\,\frac{(\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r})(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}}.

Plugging this into (
5.2
),
we conclude that for
\Re(\bm{s})
large,
the contribution to (
2.31
) from
\alpha>0

(the case
\alpha<0
being completely analogous)
equals

(5.8)

\sum_{f_{25},\lambda}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1:\\
\textnormal{pairwise coprime},\\
\textnormal{$r$ square-free},\\
p\mid n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}\Rightarrow p\notin\overline{\mathsf{S}}\end{subarray}}\,\mathscr{S}{\left(\frac{(\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}f_{25})(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{0}\alpha)}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}}\right)},

where for any given
m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1
we let

(5.9)

\alpha=\alpha(m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{m_{0}m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}{n_{0}n_{1}^{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\mathfrak{L}}}}.

(Cf. (
1.6
).)
Let
\mathsf{P}(\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}
,
for convenience.

Before proceeding, we decompose (
5.8
) into three pieces.
By reordering if necessary, assume

\{\mathsf{c}_{\ell}:1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}\}=\{\mathsf{c}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\},

where
\mathsf{c}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}
are pairwise distinct
and
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{L}
.
For each
\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}
, let

n[\ell^{\prime}]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\ell}}.

Let
\xi>0
be small.
Let
\Xi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\xi^{1/2}
.
We are now prepared to define some important functions

\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{i}(r,m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}),

where
i\in\{1,2,3\}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{3}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}1-\mathcal{P}_{1}-\mathcal{P}_{2}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w_{0}{\left(\frac{rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}}\right)}w_{0}{\left(\frac{rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha^{-1}}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}}\right)}\sum_{\mathfrak{V}\subseteq\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}:\,\lvert\mathfrak{V}\rvert\leq 1}\,\prod_{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{V}}w_{\infty}{\left(\frac{n[\ell^{\prime}]}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}}\right)}\prod_{\ell^{\prime}\notin\mathfrak{V}}w_{0}{\left(\frac{n[\ell^{\prime}]}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w_{0}{\left(\frac{rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}}\right)}w_{0}{\left(\frac{rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha^{-1}}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}}\right)}\sum_{\mathfrak{V}\subseteq\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}:\,\lvert\mathfrak{V}\rvert\geq 2}\,\prod_{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{V}}w_{\infty}{\left(\frac{n[\ell^{\prime}]}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}}\right)}\prod_{\ell^{\prime}\notin\mathfrak{V}}w_{0}{\left(\frac{n[\ell^{\prime}]}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}}\right)}.

Using smooth, as opposed to sharp, cutoffs, helps in
\mathcal{P}_{1}
(see the proof of Lemma
5.10
).

Definition 5.3
.

The
piece of (
5.8
) defined by
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is
the quantity

\sum_{f_{25},\lambda}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1:\\
\textnormal{pairwise coprime},\\
\textnormal{$r$ square-free},\\
p\mid n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}\Rightarrow p\notin\overline{\mathsf{S}}\end{subarray}}\,\mathcal{P}_{i}\cdot\mathscr{S}{\left(\frac{(\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}f_{25})(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{0}\alpha)}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}}\right)},

where
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{i}(r,m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}})
.

In other words, Definition
5.3
introduces a weight of
\mathcal{P}_{i}
into the original sum (
5.8
).
Roughly speaking,

\mathcal{P}_{3}
is supported on very degenerate ranges of variables,

\mathcal{P}_{2}
is supported on ranges where two or more of the quantities
n[\ell^{\prime}]
are large,
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is supported on ranges where only one of the quantities
n[\ell^{\prime}]
is large.
We formalize these support conditions in the next paragraph.
For now, we mention that the weight
\mathcal{P}_{3}
can be handled quite crudely, the weight
\mathcal{P}_{2}
allows for significant cancellation via a global reciprocity argument, and the weight
\mathcal{P}_{1}
allows for cancellation via local averaging modulo all but the largest quantity
n[\ell^{\prime}]
.

Since
\operatorname{Supp}{w_{0}}\subseteq\operatorname{Int}(-2,2)

and
\operatorname{Supp}{w_{\infty}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]
,
we see that if
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
then

w_{0}(n/\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi})\neq 0\Rightarrow n<2\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi},\quad w_{\infty}(n/\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi})\neq 0\Rightarrow n>\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}.

Therefore, the function
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is supported on

(5.10)

rm_{0}n_{0}\max(\alpha,\alpha^{-1})<2\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi},\quad\#\{\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}:n[\ell^{\prime}]<2\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}\}\geq\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}-1

(i.e. every point in the support of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
satisfies (
5.10
)),
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
is supported on

(5.11)

rm_{0}n_{0}\max(\alpha,\alpha^{-1})<2\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi},\quad\#\{\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}:n[\ell^{\prime}]>\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}\}\geq 2.

Also, since
w_{0}|_{[-1,1]}=1
and
(w_{0}+w_{\infty})(\frac{n[\ell^{\prime}]}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}})=1
, we see that if
rm_{0}n_{0}\max(\alpha,\alpha^{-1})\leq\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}
, then
\mathcal{P}_{1}+\mathcal{P}_{2}=1
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{P}_{3}
is supported on

(5.12)

rm_{0}n_{0}\max(\alpha,\alpha^{-1})>\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}.

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{J}(-\delta,\infty)

from §
2.3
.
Let

(5.13)

\mathcal{H}_{\star}(\delta)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{h(\bm{s}):h(\bm{z}+\mathbf{d}+2\mathbf{u})\in\mathcal{H}_{J}(-\delta,\infty)\},

where
h(\bm{z}+\mathbf{d}+2\mathbf{u})
is viewed as a function of
\bm{z}
.
The point of this definition is that
if
\bm{s}
lies in the region (
4.1
),
then
\bm{z}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bm{s}-\mathbf{d}-2\mathbf{u}

satisfies
\Re(z_{\mathfrak{j}})\geq-\delta
for all
\mathfrak{j}\in J
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\xi>0
be small.
Then the piece of (
5.8
) defined by
\mathcal{P}_{3}

extends to an element of
\mathcal{H}_{\star}(\delta)
, provided
\delta
is sufficiently small (in terms of
\xi
).

Proof.

Assume
\delta\leq\xi
.
We would like to apply Lemma
2.11
(4), but will need to treat small and large
\alpha
separately, using two different
\mathbb{R}\operatorname{div}(a)
-translates of the variable
\bm{s}
.
Let

F_{26,-1}(\bm{s})=\sum_{f_{25},\lambda}\sum_{r,m_{0},\dots}\bm{1}_{\alpha<1}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{3}\cdot\frac{(\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}f_{25})(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{0}\alpha)}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}},

and let
F_{26,1}(\bm{s})
be the corresponding sum
with
\bm{1}_{\alpha\geq 1}
in place of
\bm{1}_{\alpha<1}
.
In
F_{26,\varsigma}
, where
\varsigma\in\{\pm 1\}
,
the weight
\mathcal{P}_{3}(r,m_{0},n_{0},\dots)
implies,
via (
5.12
),
that

rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha^{\varsigma}>\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}
.
Therefore, upon taking absolute values in
F_{26,\varsigma}
, and plugging in Lemma
5.2
and the inequality
1\leq(rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha^{\varsigma})^{\xi}/\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi^{2}}
,
we get that
F_{26,\varsigma}(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u}-it\mathbf{u})
is
\ll_{\xi}(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{4})/(1+t^{2})
times

\sum_{r,m_{0},\dots}\frac{\lvert\mathfrak{G}_{r}\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)\rvert\,(n_{0}/m_{0})^{2}(\alpha n_{0}/m_{0})^{-\varsigma\xi}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{1-\delta}}\cdot\frac{(rm_{0}n_{0}\alpha^{\varsigma})^{\xi}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{\xi^{2}}},

for all
\bm{s}
in (
4.1
) and
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
in view of the inequality

\begin{split}\frac{\alpha^{-2}}{\prod_{1\leq k\leq\mathfrak{K}}\lvert m_{k}^{\beta_{k}-\varsigma\xi\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}}\rvert}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{1}{\lvert n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}-\varsigma\xi\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{n}(\ell)}}\rvert}&\leq\frac{\alpha^{-2}}{\prod_{1\leq k\leq\mathfrak{K}}m_{k}^{(2-\varsigma\xi)\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}+1-\delta}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{1}{n_{\ell}^{(2-\varsigma\xi)\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{n}(\ell)}+1-\delta}}\\
&=\frac{\alpha^{-2}(\alpha n_{0}/m_{0})^{2-\varsigma\xi}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{1-\delta}}=\frac{(n_{0}/m_{0})^{2}(\alpha n_{0}/m_{0})^{-\varsigma\xi}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{1-\delta}},\end{split}

where
\alpha^{-2}
comes from Lemma
5.2
.
Observe that the powers of
\alpha^{\varsigma}
above cancel out.

When all summation variables but
n_{0}
are fixed,
Lemma
4.4

implies

\sum_{n_{0}\geq 1}n_{0}^{2-\varsigma\xi}n_{0}^{\xi}\lvert\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\rvert\leq\prod_{p}\left(1+O_{\xi}(p^{O(\xi)})\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in J_{1}}\left(p^{-(1+1/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}})}+p^{-1/2}p^{-1}+p^{-(\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}+1)/\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{j}}}\right)\right)\ll_{\xi}1,

provided
\bm{s}
lies in (
4.1
) and
\xi
is sufficiently small.
If we then similarly use Lemma
4.6
to sum over
m_{0}
, and Lemma
4.7
to sum over
r
, we get
(suppressing coprimality restrictions)

(5.14)

\sum_{r,m_{0},n_{0}\geq 1}(n_{0}/m_{0})^{2-\varsigma\xi}(rm_{0}n_{0})^{\xi}\lvert\mathfrak{G}_{r}\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)\rvert\ll_{\xi}1.

So if
\delta<\xi^{2}
, the sum
F_{26,\varsigma}(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u}-it\mathbf{u})
(and its analog with absolute values everywhere) is

\ll_{\xi}\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)}}{1+t^{2}}\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},n_{1},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1}\frac{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{\delta-1}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{\xi^{2}}}\ll_{\delta,\xi}\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)}}{1+t^{2}}.

Applying Lemma
2.11
(4) to
\mathscr{S}(F_{26,\varsigma})
now gives Lemma
5.4
,
since the piece of (
5.8
) defined by
\mathcal{P}_{3}

is precisely
\sum_{\varsigma\in\{\pm 1\}}\mathscr{S}(F_{26,\varsigma})(\bm{s})=\sum_{\varsigma\in\{\pm 1\}}\mathscr{S}(F_{26,\varsigma})(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u})
(when
\Re(\bm{s})
is large).
∎

For
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, we use a Weyl-type inequality for monomials in several variables.
There are many ways to establish such inequalities (see e.g.
[
42
,
41
,
4
]
and references within).
However, handling technical issues such as lopsidedness and coprimality requires care.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
M_{1},\dots,M_{\mathfrak{K}},y,q\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
with
\gcd(y,q)=1
.
Let
P_{1},\dots,P_{\mathfrak{K}}
be arithmetic progressions contained in
[M_{1},2M_{1}),\dots,[M_{\mathfrak{K}},2M_{\mathfrak{K}})
, respectively, all with modulus
\leq R
.
Let
K(I)=\sum_{i\in I}(\mathfrak{u}_{i}-1)
.
Then for any nonempty set
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{K}\}
, we have

\sum_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{R^{O(1)}(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q\prod_{i\notin I}M_{i}^{-\mathfrak{u}_{i}},\min_{i\in I}(M_{i}),q^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})^{1/2^{K(I)}}}.

Proof.

We first prove a simpler case, and then the general case.

Case 1:
M_{i}=1
and
P_{i}=\{1\}
for all
i\notin I
.

For each
i\in I
, let
P_{i}-P_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{x-x^{\prime}:x,x^{\prime}\in P_{i}\}
.
We use Weyl differencing
K=K(I)
times (to “linearize” the monomial
\prod_{i\in I}m_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}
) to get

{\textstyle\lvert\mathbb{E}_{\bm{m}\in\prod_{i\in I}P_{i}}\,\mathsf{e}(y(\prod_{i\in I}m_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})/q)\rvert^{2^{K}}}\ll\mathbb{E}_{\bm{h}\in\prod_{i\in I}(P_{i}-P_{i})^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}-1}}\,\lvert\mathbb{E}_{\bm{m}\in\prod_{i\in I}P_{i}}\,\mathsf{e}(yM(\bm{h},\bm{m})/q)\bm{1}_{m_{i}\in Q_{i}(\bm{h})}\rvert,

where
\mathbb{E}_{x\in A}
denotes an average over
x\in A
,
where
Q_{i}(\bm{h})
is a real interval defined in terms of
P_{i}
and
\bm{h}
,
and where
M(\bm{h},\bm{m})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{i\in I}(\mathfrak{u}_{i}!\,m_{i}\prod_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{u}_{i}-1}h_{i,j})
.
Fix an
i_{\star}\in I
, sum over
m_{i_{\star}}\in P_{i_{\star}}\cap Q_{i_{\star}}(\bm{h})
, and use the divisor bound as in
[
42
]
*proof of Lemma 2.2, to get

{\textstyle\lvert\mathbb{E}_{\bm{m}\in\prod_{i\in I}P_{i}}\,\mathsf{e}(y(\prod_{i\in I}m_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})/q)\rvert^{2^{K}}}\ll_{\epsilon}\sum_{i\in I}\frac{1}{\lvert P_{i}\rvert}+\frac{(M^{\prime})^{\epsilon}}{\prod_{i\in I}\lvert P_{i}\rvert^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}}\sum_{1\leq h\leq M^{\prime}}\min(\lvert P_{i_{\star}}\rvert,\lVert yhr/q\rVert_{\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}^{-1})

for some
r\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,R\prod_{i\in I}\mathfrak{u}_{i}!]
,
where
M^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{i_{\star}}^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}(2M_{i})^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}

and
\lVert\vartheta\rVert_{\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\min_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\lvert\vartheta-n\rvert}
.
By
[
55
]
*Lemma 2.2, the sum over
h
is

\ll_{\epsilon}M^{\prime}\lvert P_{i_{\star}}\rvert\left(\frac{r}{q}+\frac{1}{\lvert P_{i_{\star}}\rvert}+\frac{q}{M^{\prime}\lvert P_{i_{\star}}\rvert}\right)(M^{\prime}q)^{\epsilon}.

Multiplying by
\prod_{i\in I}\lvert P_{i}\rvert^{2^{K}}
, we now find that
\lvert\sum_{\bm{m}\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\rvert^{2^{K}}
is

\ll_{\epsilon}\sum_{i\in I}\frac{(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{2^{K}}}{M_{i}}+(M^{\prime}q)^{\epsilon}\left(\frac{M^{\prime}M_{i_{\star}}R}{q}+M^{\prime}+q\right)\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{2^{K}-\mathfrak{u}_{i}},

since
2^{K}\geq\mathfrak{u}_{i}
.
This implies the result up to
q^{\epsilon}
,
which suffices,
since we may assume
\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}\geq q
.
(The result is trivial if
\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}\leq q
.)

Case 2: The general case.

Suppose we freeze
(m_{j})_{j\notin I}

and sum over the remaining variables.
By Case 1,
with
y^{\prime}/q^{\prime}=y(\prod_{j\notin I}m_{j}^{\mathfrak{u}_{j}})/q
in place of
y/q
,
we find that

\sum_{(m_{i})_{i\in I}\in\prod_{i\in I}P_{i}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{R^{O(1)}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q^{\prime},\min_{i\in I}(M_{i}),(q^{\prime})^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})^{1/2^{K(I)}}}

However, since
q\geq q^{\prime}\gg q\prod_{j\notin I}M_{j}^{-\mathfrak{u}_{j}}
, we have

{\textstyle\min(q^{\prime},\min_{i\in I}(M_{i}),(q^{\prime})^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})\gg\min(q\prod_{j\notin I}M_{j}^{-\mathfrak{u}_{j}},\min_{i\in I}(M_{i}),q^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})}.

Combining the last two displays
and summing over
(m_{j})_{j\notin I}
, we conclude that

\sum_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{\lvert\prod_{j\notin I}P_{j}\rvert\,R^{O(1)}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q\prod_{j\notin I}M_{j}^{-\mathfrak{u}_{j}},\min_{i\in I}(M_{i}),q^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})}.

This suffices, since
\lvert P_{j}\rvert\ll M_{j}
for all
j\notin I
.
∎

Corollary 5.6
.

In the setting of Proposition
5.5
, we have (for some constant
\eta>0
)

\sum_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{R^{O(1)}(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q,M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)^{\eta}}.

Proof.

If
\prod_{1\leq i\leq\mathfrak{K}}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}\leq q
, the desired bound is trivial.
So suppose
\prod_{1\leq i\leq\mathfrak{K}}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}\geq q
.
Let
\mathfrak{Y}=\min(q,M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\geq 1
;
then
M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}\geq\mathfrak{Y}^{2}
.
Let

I=\{1\leq i\leq\mathfrak{K}:M_{i}\geq\mathfrak{Y}^{\varrho}\}

for some
\varrho>0

small enough in terms of
\mathfrak{u}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}
.
Then
I\neq\emptyset
,
and
\min_{i\in I}(M_{i})\geq\mathfrak{Y}^{\varrho}
.
Also,

{\textstyle\min(q\prod_{i\notin I}M_{i}^{-\mathfrak{u}_{i}},q^{-1}\prod_{i\in I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}})\geq\mathfrak{Y}/\prod_{i\notin I}M_{i}^{\mathfrak{u}_{i}}}\geq\mathfrak{Y}^{\varrho},

say.
Consequently, by Proposition
5.5
, the exponent
\eta=\varrho/2^{K(\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{K}\})}
is admissible.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

In the setting of Proposition
5.5
, we have (for some constant
\eta>0
)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}:\\
\gcd(m_{i},m_{j})=\gcd(m_{j},Q)=1\end{subarray}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{Q^{\epsilon}R^{O(1)}(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q,M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)^{\eta}}\quad\textnormal{for all $Q\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}$}.

Proof.

We sieve, i.e. use the Möbius inversion formula

\bm{1}_{\gcd(m,n)=1}=\sum_{h\mid m,n}\mu(h)
,
to rewrite the
\gcd
conditions.
We separately analyze small and large values of the divisor
h
.
Let
B\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
If
h_{ij},g_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,B]
, then Corollary
5.6
implies

\sum_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}}\bm{1}_{h_{ij}\mid m_{i},m_{j}}\bm{1}_{g_{j}\mid m_{j},Q}\,\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{(BR)^{O(1)}(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q,M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)^{\eta}}.

Multiplying by
\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq\mathfrak{K}}\mu(h_{ij})
and
\prod_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}}\mu(g_{j})
, and summing over
h_{ij},g_{j}\geq 1
, we get

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in P_{1}\times\dots\times P_{\mathfrak{K}}:\\
\gcd(m_{i},m_{j})=\gcd(m_{j},Q)=1\end{subarray}}\mathsf{e}(ym_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{(BR)^{O(1)}(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{1+\epsilon}}{\min(q,M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)^{\eta}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}}\geq 1:\\
M_{j}\leq m_{j}<2M_{j}\end{subarray}}T_{B}(\bm{m}),

where
T_{B}(\bm{m})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq\mathfrak{K}}\sum_{h>B}\bm{1}_{h\mid m_{i},m_{j}}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}}\sum_{g>B:\,g\mid Q}\bm{1}_{g\mid m_{j}})\cdot(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}})^{\epsilon}
.
Here

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}}\geq 1:\\
M_{j}\leq m_{j}<2M_{j}\end{subarray}}T_{B}(\bm{m})\ll_{\epsilon}(M_{1}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}})^{1+\epsilon}(1+Q^{\epsilon})/B,

since
Q
has
O_{\epsilon}(Q^{\epsilon})
divisors.
Now take
B
to be a small power of
\min(q,M_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots M_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)
.
∎

We can now handle
\mathcal{P}_{2}
,
using reciprocity (
\psi(\mathbb{Q})=1
)
and partial summation.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\xi>0
be small.
Then the piece of (

) defined by

extends to an element of

, provided

).

Proof.

If
\mathfrak{K}\mathfrak{L}=0
,

,

\mathcal{Q}_{3}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{r,m_{0},\dots}\nu\cdot\mathcal{P}_{2}\cdot\mathscr{S}{\left(\frac{(\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}f_{25})(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{C}_{0}\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{2}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{c^{\prime}_{2}n_{0}}})}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{C}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}}\right)};

We now want to apply Corollary

10
10

10

\mathcal{Q}_{4}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{r,m_{0},\ldots:\,\eqref{COND:define-challenging-Kloosterman-oscillation-Z_1-range}}\,R^{\mathfrak{K}}\frac{\lvert\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}\rvert\,(n_{0}/m_{0})^{2}(1+\lvert\alpha\rvert)^{\mathfrak{K}}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{1-\delta}}\frac{O_{\epsilon}(Q^{\epsilon}R^{O(1)}(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}})^{\epsilon})}{\min(q,m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}/q)^{\eta}},

,

.

,

, and

)

(if we take

.
∎

Finally, we rewrite the
\mathcal{P}_{1}
piece in a form convenient for §
6
,
via an equidistribution estimate:

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
y,q,Q,M,A\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
Y\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
Let
w\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be supported on
[1,10]
,
and suppose
\sup{\lvert w^{(B)}\rvert}\ll_{B}A^{B}Y
for all
B\geq 0
,
where
w^{(B)}
is the
B
th derivative of
w
.
Let
\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}:\,\gcd(n,Q)=1}[\ast]

be the average of
\ast
over the set
\{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}:\gcd(n,Q)=1\}
.
Then

(5.17)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}:\,\gcd(m,Q)=1}w(\tfrac{m}{M})\,(\bm{1}_{m\equiv y\bmod{q}}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}:\,\gcd(n,Q)=1}[\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}])\ll_{\epsilon}(QM)^{\epsilon}AYq.

Proof.

By replacing
w
with
w/Y
, assume
Y=1
.
Let
f_{27}(x^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w(x^{\prime}/M)

and let

\hat{f}_{27}(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{27}(x^{\prime})\mathsf{e}(-xx^{\prime})\,dx^{\prime}\ll_{k}M(1+\lvert xM/A\rvert)^{-k},

where the bound on the right-hand side follows from
repeated integration by parts over
x^{\prime}
if
\lvert xM/A\rvert\geq 1
,
or from the triangle inequality if
\lvert xM/A\rvert\leq 1
.
Given
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
,
let

f_{28}(m)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bm{1}_{h\mid m}\bm{1}_{m\equiv y\bmod{q}}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{hq}}[\bm{1}_{h\mid n}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]

and let
\hat{f}_{28}(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{E}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{hq}}[f_{28}(m)\mathsf{e}(xm\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{hq}})]
,
where
\mathbb{E}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{hq}}[\ast]
is the average of
\ast
over
\mathbb{Z}_{hq}
.
By the Poisson summation formula in
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{Z}/hq\mathbb{Z})
,
11
11

11

By this,
we mean the adelic Poisson formula

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Q}}\phi(m)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Q}}\hat{\phi}(x)
,
with a test function
\phi\colon\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathbb{C}

supported on
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathbb{R}\times\prod_{p}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
such that
\phi|_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
factors through the reduction map

\mathbf{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{Z}/hq\mathbb{Z})
.

or by splitting
\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}
into residue classes modulo
hq

and applying
[
37
]
*(4.24), with
v=hq
in each class,
we have

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{27}(m)f_{28}(m)=\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{f}_{27}(\tfrac{m^{\prime}}{hq})\hat{f}_{28}(\tfrac{m^{\prime}}{hq}).

Clearly
\hat{f}_{28}(0)=0

and
\sup\lvert\hat{f}_{28}\rvert\leq\sup\lvert f_{28}\rvert\leq 1
.
If
h\leq\frac{M^{1-\epsilon}}{Aq}
,
then
\hat{f}_{27}(\frac{m^{\prime}}{hq})\ll_{k}\frac{M}{\lvert m^{\prime}M^{\epsilon}\rvert^{k}}
, so

(5.18)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w(\tfrac{m}{M})\,(\bm{1}_{h\mid m}\bm{1}_{m\equiv y\bmod{q}}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{hq}}[\bm{1}_{h\mid n}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}])=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{27}(m)f_{28}(m)\ll_{\epsilon,k}M^{-k}.

We have
\bm{1}_{\gcd(m,Q)=1}=\sum_{h\mid m,Q}\mu(h)=\sum_{h\mid Q}\mu(h)\bm{1}_{h\mid m}
;
cf. the sieving in Corollary
5.7
.
Multiplying (
5.18
) by
\mu(h)
,
summing over
h\mid Q
,
and writing

\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{hq}}[\bm{1}_{h\mid n}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]=\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Qq}}[\bm{1}_{h\mid n}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]

for each
h\mid Q
,
we find that

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w(\tfrac{m}{M})\,(\bm{1}_{\gcd(m,Q)=1}\bm{1}_{m\equiv y\bmod{q}}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Qq}}[\bm{1}_{\gcd(n,Q)=1}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}])\ll_{\epsilon,k}\mathcal{Q}_{5}+\sum_{h\leq\frac{M^{1-\epsilon}}{Aq}}M^{-k},

where

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}_{5}&\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w(\tfrac{m}{M})\sum_{h>\frac{M^{1-\epsilon}}{Aq}:\,h\mid Q}\lvert\bm{1}_{h\mid m}\bm{1}_{m\equiv y\bmod{q}}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Qq}}[\bm{1}_{h\mid n}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]\rvert\\
&\ll\sum_{M\leq m\leq 10M}\sum_{h>\frac{M^{1-\epsilon}}{Aq}:\,h\mid Q}(\bm{1}_{h\mid m}+\tfrac{1}{h})\ll\sum_{h>\frac{M^{1-\epsilon}}{Aq}:\,h\mid Q}\tfrac{M}{h}\ll_{\epsilon}Q^{\epsilon}\tfrac{M}{M^{1-\epsilon}/(Aq)}.\end{split}

Thus

(5.19)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w(\tfrac{m}{M})\,(\bm{1}_{\gcd(m,Q)=1}\bm{1}_{m\equiv y\bmod{q}}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Qq}}[\bm{1}_{\gcd(n,Q)=1}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}])\ll_{\epsilon}(QM)^{\epsilon}Aq.

On the other hand, the
q=1
case of (
5.19
) gives

(5.20)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w(\tfrac{m}{M})\,(\bm{1}_{\gcd(m,Q)=1}-\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Q}}[\bm{1}_{\gcd(n,Q)=1}])\ll_{\epsilon}(QM)^{\epsilon}A.

Multiplying (
5.20
)
by
\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}:\,\gcd(n,Q)=1}[\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]
,
then subtracting (
5.19
),
gives (
5.17
), since

\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Qq}}[\bm{1}_{\gcd(n,Q)=1}\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]=\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{Q}}[\bm{1}_{\gcd(n,Q)=1}]\cdot\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}:\,\gcd(n,Q)=1}[\bm{1}_{n\equiv y\bmod{q}}]
.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\xi>0
be small.
For each
\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}
, let

(5.21)

\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}{\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\ell}}}.

If
\delta

,

) defined by
\mathcal{P}_{1}

for some functions
h_{0},\dots,h_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}
such that

, we have

and
h_{0},h_{\ell^{\prime}}\ll_{K}(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1)}
in vertical strips
\Re(\bm{s})\in K
(for compact

).

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.8
, we may assume
\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{L}\geq 1
.
Fix
\Omega
,

,
\lambda
.
Assume
n[1]\ll n[2]\ll\dots\ll n[\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}]

m_{1}\ll m_{2}\ll\dots\ll m_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
by introducing a suitable factor

\nu=\nu(n[1],\dots,n[\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}],m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}})\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}+\mathfrak{K}}/\mathbb{R}_{>0})
,

) from before).
Let
\mathsf{C}_{0}=\mathsf{c}_{0}-\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}

\mathsf{C}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell}-\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}
.
For any

satisfying (
5.10
),
