n[\ell^{\prime}]\ll\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\Xi}
for all
1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
.

Let
c^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
be the denominator of
\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}
.
The piece of (
5.8
) of interest is

\mathscr{S}(F_{29})(\bm{s})
, where

F_{29}(\bm{s})=\sum_{r,m_{0},\dots}\nu\cdot\mathcal{P}_{1}\cdot\frac{(\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}f_{25})(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{C}_{0}\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{c^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n_{0}}})}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}}\frac{\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{C}_{\ell}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n_{\ell}}})}{n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}}.

Write
\bm{m}^{\prime}=(m_{0},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}-1})
and
\bm{n}=(n_{0},\dots,n_{\mathfrak{L}})
.
Let

\begin{split}f_{30}(\bm{s},\lambda,r,\bm{m}^{\prime},\bm{n})&\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{c^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n_{0}}})\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}],\\
f_{31,\ell^{\prime}}(r,\bm{m}^{\prime},\bm{n})&\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n[\ell^{\prime}]}})],\end{split}

where for a locally constant function
\ast
of
\alpha
on
\mathbb{Q}_{q}

we let
\mathbb{E}[\ast]
denote the average of
\ast

over the set
\{m_{\mathfrak{K}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}:\gcd(m_{\mathfrak{K}},rm_{0}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}-1}n_{0}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})=1\}
.
Let

F_{32}(\bm{s})=\sum_{r,m_{0},\dots}\nu\cdot\mathcal{P}_{1}\cdot\frac{f_{25}(\bm{s},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{C}_{0}\alpha)f_{30}(\bm{s},\lambda,r,\bm{m}^{\prime},\bm{n})}{m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\frac{\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}f_{31,\ell^{\prime}}(r,\bm{m}^{\prime},\bm{n})}{n_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}^{\gamma_{\mathfrak{L}}}}.

Let
\bm{z}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bm{s}-\mathbf{d}-2\mathbf{u}
, and assume
\bm{s}
lies in (
4.1
) with
\delta=\xi/2
.

For
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
we now aim to bound the difference
(F_{29}-F_{32})(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u})

by summing over
m_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
using Proposition
5.9

with suitable parameters
M
,
A
,
Y
,
q
,
Q
.
First we show that
m_{\mathfrak{K}}
is large.
The weight
\nu
in
F_{29}(\bm{s})
forces

m_{\mathfrak{K}}\gg m_{1},m_{2},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
and the weight
\mathcal{P}_{1}
forces
m_{0},n_{0},\alpha^{-1}\ll\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}

by (
5.10
).
Yet by (
5.9
)
and the inequality
\mathfrak{v}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{v}_{\mathfrak{L}}\geq 1
, we have

\alpha n_{0}/m_{0}=\frac{m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}{n_{1}^{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\mathfrak{L}}}}\leq\frac{m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}{n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}}=\frac{m_{1}^{1+\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{1+\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}{\mathsf{P}(\alpha)}.

Letting
A_{10}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(1+\mathfrak{u}_{1})+\dots+(1+\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}})\geq 2\mathfrak{K}>0
, it follows that

(5.22)

m_{\mathfrak{K}}\gg(m_{1}^{1+\mathfrak{u}_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{1+\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}})^{1/A_{10}}=(\mathsf{P}(\alpha)\alpha n_{0}/m_{0})^{1/A_{10}}\gg\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{(1-2\xi)/A_{10}},

since
n_{0}\geq 1
and
\alpha,m_{0}^{-1}\gg\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{-\xi}
.
Second, we show that
\sum_{m_{\mathfrak{K}}}
is smoothly weighted.
By the chain rule,
the
k_{1}=2
case of Lemma
5.2
,
the corollary
m_{\mathfrak{K}}\,\tfrac{\partial\alpha}{\partial{m_{\mathfrak{K}}}}=\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}\alpha
to (
5.9
),
and the corollary
(\alpha\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha})^{k_{2}}\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{C}_{0}\alpha)\ll_{k_{2}}(1+\alpha)^{k_{2}}\ll_{k_{2}}\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi k_{2}}
to (
5.10
),
we have

(5.23)

(m_{\mathfrak{K}}\,\tfrac{\partial}{\partial{m_{\mathfrak{K}}}})^{k_{2}}\,\frac{\nu\cdot\mathcal{P}_{1}\cdot f_{25}(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,\alpha)\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{C}_{0}\alpha)}{m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}+it\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}}\ll_{k_{2}}\frac{(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1+k_{2})}\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi k_{2}}}{\alpha^{2}(1+t^{2})}.

Third, Lemma
4.8
lets us break
\sum_{m_{k}}

into
q
residue classes
\mathcal{R}
modulo
q

on which the quantities

\mathsf{e}(\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{c^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n_{0}}})
,

\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}
,
\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}
,
\mathfrak{G}_{r}
,

\mathsf{e}(-\mathsf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}\alpha\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{n[\ell^{\prime}]}})

are constant, where

q\ll rm_{0}c^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n_{0}\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}]\ll\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{3\xi+\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\Xi}

by (
5.10
).
Fourth, we have a coprimality condition
\gcd(m_{k},Q)=1
, where

Q=rm_{0}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}-1}n_{0}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}}\leq rm_{0}n_{0}\mathsf{P}(\alpha)\ll\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{1+3\xi}

by (
5.10
).
Finally, if in
F_{29}(\bm{s})
,
we make a smooth dyadic partition of unity on the variable
m_{\mathfrak{K}}
,
then Proposition
5.9

with
M\gg\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{(1-2\xi)/A_{10}}
(in view of (
5.22
)),
with
A\asymp(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1)}\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{\xi}

and
Y\asymp(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1)}\alpha^{-2}(1+t^{2})^{-1}

(in view of (
5.23
)),
and with
q
and
Q
as displayed above,
reveals that

(F_{29}-F_{32})(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u})\ll_{\epsilon}\frac{(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1)}}{1+t^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,m_{0},\ldots:\,\eqref{COND:define-dominant-Z_1-range},\\
m_{\mathfrak{K}}\gg\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{(1-2\xi)/A_{10}}\end{subarray}}\,\frac{\lvert\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}\rvert\,(n_{0}/m_{0})^{2}}{(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{1-\delta}}\frac{q^{2}(Qm_{\mathfrak{K}})^{\epsilon}A}{m_{\mathfrak{K}}}.

Key here is the rightmost factor of
m_{\mathfrak{K}}
in the denominator,
coming from cancellation in dyadic ranges
m_{\mathfrak{K}}\asymp M
.
At this point,
writing
m_{\mathfrak{K}}\gg\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{(1-2\xi)/A_{10}}=(m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}n_{1}\cdots n_{\mathfrak{L}})^{(1-2\xi)/A_{10}}
in the denominator,
and using the crude bound
\mathfrak{N}_{m_{0}}\mathfrak{D}_{n_{0}}\mathfrak{G}_{r}\ll(m_{0}n_{0}r)^{O(1)}\ll\mathsf{P}(\alpha)^{O(\xi)}

as we did for Lemma
5.8
after (
5.16
),
we find that if
\xi
is small enough in terms of
A_{10}
, then

(F_{29}-F_{32})(\bm{s}-it\mathbf{u})\ll\frac{(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1)}}{1+t^{2}}\sum_{m,n\geq 1}\frac{1}{(mn)^{1+1/(2A_{10})}}\ll\frac{(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert)^{O(1)}}{1+t^{2}}.

Thus, by definition (
2.5
),

(5.24)

(F_{29}-F_{32})(\bm{s})=(F_{29}-F_{32})(\bm{z}+\mathbf{d}+2\mathbf{u})\in\mathcal{H}_{\dagger,J}(-\delta,\infty).

Therefore,
\mathscr{S}(F_{29}-F_{32})(\bm{s})\in\mathcal{H}_{\star}(\delta)

by Lemma
2.11
(4)
and the definition (
5.13
) of
\mathcal{H}_{\star}(\delta)
.

It remains to analyze
\mathscr{S}(F_{32})
.
Thanks to the factorization (
5.9
) of
\alpha
,
the average over
m_{\mathfrak{K}}
in
f_{31,\ell^{\prime}}

exhibits cancellation
for each
1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
,
since
\mathsf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}\neq 0
.
By the

(u,N,C)=\left(\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}},n[\ell^{\prime}],\frac{-\mathsf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}m_{0}\prod_{1\leq k<\mathfrak{K}}m_{k}^{\mathfrak{u}_{k}}}{n_{0}\prod_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}:\,\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}n[\ell]}\right)

case of Lemma
5.11
below, we have

(5.25)

f_{31,\ell^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{e}(m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{K}}}CN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})]\ll_{\epsilon}n[\ell^{\prime}]^{\epsilon-1/2},

whenever
1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
.
On the other hand, we will satisfactorily bound
f_{30}
by
simply plugging in Lemmas
4.4
,
4.6
, and
4.7
pointwise
(just as in the proof of (
5.14
)
for Lemma
5.4
),
without attempting to obtain cancellation over
m_{\mathfrak{K}}
.

Let
\varsigma=-1
if
f_{25}=\mathcal{J}_{\Omega,0}
,
and
\varsigma=1
if
f_{25}=\mathcal{J}_{\Omega,\infty}
.
For
\Re(\bm{s})
large, we have
\mathscr{S}(F_{32})(\bm{s})=\mathscr{S}(F_{32})(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u})
(cf. the proof of Lemma
5.4
).
But for
\bm{s}
in (
4.1
),
the Dirichlet coefficient

\mathfrak{C}=\mathfrak{C}(\bm{s},t,m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},(n_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}})

of
m_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\beta_{\mathfrak{K}}}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}

in the series
F_{32}(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u}-it\mathbf{u})

is of the form

\sum_{r,m_{0},n_{0}\geq 1}\sum_{n_{\ell}\geq 1:\,\ell\geq 1,\;\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}\frac{O(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)})\bm{1}_{\alpha^{\varsigma}\gg 1}}{\alpha^{2}(1+t^{2})}\frac{f_{30}(\bm{s}-\varsigma\xi\mathbf{u}-it\mathbf{u},\lambda,r,\bm{m}^{\prime},\bm{n})}{(\alpha n_{0}/m_{0})^{\varsigma\xi}}\frac{O(\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}]^{\xi-1/2})}{\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}},

by Lemma
5.2

and (
5.25
).
We proceed to bound this via the strategy of Lemma
5.4
.

By (
5.9
) and (
5.21
),

\alpha/\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}=m_{0}/(n_{0}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\ell}})
.
Thus

rm_{0}n_{0}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\ell}}\geq\max(1,(\alpha/\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}})^{\varsigma})\gg 1+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\varsigma},

since
\alpha^{\varsigma}\gg 1
.
Plugging
1\ll(rm_{0}n_{0}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\mathfrak{v}_{\ell}})^{2\xi}/(1+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\varsigma})^{2\xi}

and the identities
\alpha^{2}=(m_{0}/n_{0})^{2}(\alpha n_{0}/m_{0})^{2}

and
\alpha n_{0}/m_{0}=\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}/\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}]
into our bound for
\mathfrak{C}
above,
and summing over
r
,
m_{0}
,
n_{0}
as in the proof of (
5.14
), we get

\mathfrak{C}\ll_{\xi}\sum_{n_{\ell}\geq 1:\,\ell\geq 1,\;\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}\frac{O(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)})}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{2+\varsigma\xi}(1+t^{2})}\frac{O(\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}]^{2+\varsigma\xi+\xi-1/2+2\xi})}{\lvert\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}\rvert}\frac{1}{(1+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\varsigma})^{2\xi}}.

But
\lvert\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\gamma_{\ell}}\rvert\geq\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{2\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{n}(\ell)}+1-\delta}=(\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}])^{2}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{1-\delta}
, so

\mathfrak{C}\ll_{\xi}\sum_{n_{\ell}\geq 1:\,\ell\geq 1,\;\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}\frac{O(1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)})}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{2+\varsigma\xi}(1+t^{2})}\frac{O(\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}]^{\varsigma\xi+\xi-1/2+2\xi})}{\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{1-\delta}}\frac{1}{(1+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\varsigma})^{2\xi}}.

Since
\prod_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}n[\ell^{\prime}]\geq\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}\neq\mathsf{c}_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}
,
we find that if
\xi
is small enough that
4\xi+\delta<1/2
, then

\mathfrak{C}\ll_{\xi}\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{2+\varsigma\xi}(1+t^{2})}\frac{1}{(1+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\varsigma})^{2\xi}}\asymp\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{2}(1+t^{2})\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{\varsigma\xi}}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-2\varsigma\xi}}=\frac{1+\lVert\bm{s}\rVert^{O(1)}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{2}(1+t^{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{\xi}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\xi}}.

Integrating
\mathfrak{C}
over
t\in\mathbb{R}

(and summing over
f_{25}
,
\lambda
,
\nu
)
gives the lemma,
with

h_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{2}(\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{\xi}+\alpha_{\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}^{-\xi})\sum_{f_{25},\lambda,\nu}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathfrak{C}(\bm{s},t,m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},(n_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}})\,dt\in\mathcal{H}_{\star}(\delta),

where we restrict
\sum_{\nu}
to weights
\nu
associated to the index
\ell^{\prime}=\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
.
(In the partition of unity at the beginning of the proof, each weight
\nu
is associated to some index
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
, corresponding approximately to which of the quantities
n[1],\dots,n[\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}]
is largest.)
∎

The following standard lemma on Gauss sums
is similar to
[
52
]
*Lemma 5.7:

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
u,N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
C\in\mathbb{Z}[1/N]^{\times}
.
Then

\frac{1}{\lvert(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\rvert}\sum_{x\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\mathsf{e}(x^{u}CN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})\ll_{u,\epsilon}N^{\epsilon-1/2},

where
\mathsf{e}(x^{u}CN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{e}(\tilde{x}^{u}CN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})

for any lift
\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{Z}
of
x
modulo
N
.

Proof.

By the Chinese remainder theorem and the divisor bound,
it suffices to treat the case where
N
is a prime power.
Suppose
N=p^{k}
with
k\geq 1
.
By the structure theory of finite abelian groups,
there exists a finite group
\mathfrak{H}

of characters
\chi\colon(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}

such that

(5.26)

\#\{x\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}:\bm{1}_{x^{u}=y}\}=\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{H}}\chi(y)

for all
y\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
,
with
\lvert\mathfrak{H}\rvert=\#\{x\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}:\bm{1}_{x^{u}=1}\}\ll_{u}1
.
By (
5.26
),

\mathcal{Q}_{6}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{x\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\mathsf{e}(x^{u}CN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})=\sum_{y\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\mathsf{e}(yCN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{H}}\chi(y).

But by
[
37
]
*Lemma 3.1,
we have
\lvert\sum_{y\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\chi(y)\mathsf{e}(yCN^{-1}\bmod{\mathbb{Z}_{N}})\rvert\leq\sqrt{N}

(with equality if and only if
\chi
is primitive
in the sense of
[
37
]
*§3.3).
Therefore,

\lvert\mathcal{Q}_{6}\rvert\leq\lvert\mathfrak{H}\rvert\sqrt{N}\ll_{u}\sqrt{N}
.
This suffices, since
\lvert(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\rvert=(1-p^{-1})N\geq N/2
.
∎

6.
Final reductions

In this section, assume
X
is strictly split but not that
D
has strict normal crossings.
We proceed using the blowup strategy of
[
9
]
*§6 (see also
[
52
]
*paragraph after Theorem 5.1).
Let
\pi\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
be an equivariant morphism of the form specified in Definition
2.3
.
Then in particular,
\widetilde{X}
is split and its boundary
\widetilde{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\widetilde{X}\setminus G
has strict normal crossings.

Index the irreducible components of
\widetilde{D}
by
\widetilde{J}\supseteq J

so that
\widetilde{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
is the strict transform of
D_{\mathfrak{j}}
for
\mathfrak{j}\in J
,
and
\widetilde{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
is an exceptional divisor (
\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, since
\widetilde{X}
is split) for
\mathfrak{j}\in\widetilde{J}\setminus J
.
Then

\operatorname{Pic}(\widetilde{X})=\mathbb{Z}^{\widetilde{J}\setminus J}\oplus\pi^{\ast}{\operatorname{Pic}(X)},\quad\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(\widetilde{X})=\mathbb{Z}^{\widetilde{J}}=\mathbb{Z}^{\widetilde{J}\setminus J}\oplus\pi^{\ast}{\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(X)},\quad\operatorname{div}(a|_{\widetilde{X}})=\pi^{\ast}{\operatorname{div}(a)}.

Thus we may choose
\widetilde{H}
(satisfying Proposition
2.8
for
\widetilde{X}
) so that
\widetilde{H}(\pi^{\ast}\bm{s},g)=H(\bm{s},g)
.

For numerical comparison of
X
and
\widetilde{X}
,
the following standard lemma is essential:

Lemma 6.1
.

In
\operatorname{Pic}^{G}(\widetilde{X})
, we have
-\pi^{\ast}{\operatorname{div}(\omega)}\in-\operatorname{div}(\pi^{\ast}{\omega})+\sum_{\mathfrak{j}\in\widetilde{J}\setminus J}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\widetilde{D}_{\mathfrak{j}}
.

We can now study
\mathsf{Z}(sK_{X}^{-1},1_{G})=\widetilde{\mathsf{Z}}(s\pi^{\ast}{K_{X}^{-1}},1_{G})
.
Let
w\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then the Mellin transform
w^{\vee}(s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{0}^{\infty}w(x)x^{s-1}\,dx
is holomorphic on
\Re(s)>0
, with rapid decay (i.e.
w^{\vee}(s)\ll_{L}(1+\lvert s\rvert)^{-L}
for all
L\geq 0
) in vertical strips.
Let
\sigma,B\geq 2
be large.
By Mellin inversion,

(6.1)

\sum_{x\in G(\mathbb{Q})}w{\left(\frac{H(K_{X}^{-1},x)}{B}\right)}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Re(s)=\sigma}w^{\vee}(s)B^{s}\widetilde{\mathsf{Z}}(s\pi^{\ast}{K_{X}^{-1}},1_{G})\,dt,\quad\textnormal{where $t=\Im(s)$}.

Contour shifting, via Proposition
2.17
(for
\widetilde{X}
, with
\bm{\kappa}=\pi^{\ast}{\mathbf{d}}=-\pi^{\ast}{\operatorname{div}(\omega)}
), gives

(6.2)

\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Re(s)=\sigma}w^{\vee}(s)B^{s}\widetilde{\mathsf{Z}}_{0}(s\pi^{\ast}{\mathbf{d}},1_{G})\,dt=\left(\tilde{c}_{X,H}w^{\vee}(1)+O{\left(\frac{1}{\log{B}}\right)}\right)\frac{B(\log{B})^{\lvert J\rvert-2}}{(\lvert J\rvert-2)!},

where
\tilde{c}_{X,H}=\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{J}(\widetilde{X})}(\mathbf{d})\lim_{\bm{s}\to\mathbf{d}}{H^{\ast}(\bm{s},1)\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}
.
By (
2.4
),

\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{J}(\widetilde{X})}=\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{J}(X)}
.
Also, Lemma
2.12
(1)
\Rightarrow
(2) for
\widetilde{H}^{\ast}_{v}(\pi^{\ast}{\bm{s}},1)
on
\widetilde{X}
, followed by Lemma
2.12
(2)
\Rightarrow
(3) for
X
, gives
\lim_{\bm{s}\to\mathbf{d}}{H^{\ast}(\bm{s},1)\prod_{\mathfrak{j}\in J}(s_{\mathfrak{j}}-\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{j}})}=\tau(X,\mathsf{H})
.
Meanwhile,
\lvert J\rvert-2=\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))-1
by Proposition
2.1
.
Therefore,
\tilde{c}_{X,H}/(\lvert J\rvert-2)!

equals Peyre’s constant
\mathcal{A}_{X,\mathsf{H}}
(
2.9
).

Still,
\widetilde{\mathsf{Z}}_{1}
remains.
Let
\bm{s}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}s(\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{u})+\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{d}+2\mathbf{u}+(s-1)(\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{u})
;
note that
\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{J}
by Proposition
2.2
.
Define
\mathfrak{m}
,
\mathfrak{n}
,
\mathfrak{K}
,
\mathfrak{L}
,
\mathsf{c}_{\ell}
,
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
,
\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}
in terms of
X
as in §
5
, even if
D
does not have strict normal crossings.
Let
\upsilon_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}+\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{m}(k)}\geq 1

for
k\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{K}\}
,
and
\nu_{\ell}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{n}(\ell)}+\mathsf{u}_{\mathfrak{n}(\ell)}\geq 1

for
\ell\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}\}
.
Let
\Psi_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}m_{1}^{\upsilon_{1}}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}^{\upsilon_{\mathfrak{K}}}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}^{\nu_{j}}

and
\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}B/\Psi_{\ell^{\prime}}

for
\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}
.
By Lemmas
5.4
,
5.8
, and
5.10
for
\widetilde{X}
, we have (since
1=\mathcal{P}_{1}+\mathcal{P}_{2}+\mathcal{P}_{3}
)

\int_{\Re(s)=\sigma}w^{\vee}(s)B^{s-1}\widetilde{\mathsf{Z}}_{1}(\pi^{\ast}{\bm{s}},1_{G})\,dt\ll B^{-\delta}+\sum_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}}\,\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}}\geq 1}\,\sum_{n_{\ell}\geq 1:\,\ell\geq 1,\;\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}\,\frac{(\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\delta}+\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}^{-\delta})^{-1}(\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\delta}+\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{-\delta})^{-1}}{m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}}

for some
\delta=\delta_{X}>0
,
where the factor of
(\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\delta}+\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{-\delta})^{-1}
comes from the bound

\int_{\Re(s)=\sigma}w^{\vee}(s)\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{s-1}h_{\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{s},m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}},(n_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}})\,dt\ll(\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\delta}+\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{-\delta})^{-1}

proven by shifting
\Re(s)=\sigma
to
\Re(s)=1\pm\delta
.

Suppose
\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\}
.
Let
\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}=q
;
then
\lvert\{\ell\geq 1:\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}\}\rvert=\lvert J^{q}_{1}\rvert
.
Let
A,P\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
The exponents
\upsilon_{i}
,
\nu_{j}
in
\Psi_{\ell^{\prime}}
are positive,
and the exponent vectors of
\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}
,
\Psi_{\ell^{\prime}}
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
,
so the number of tuples of
M_{k},N_{\ell}\in\{2^{e}:e\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}

admitting
m_{k}\in[M_{k},2M_{k})
,

n_{\ell}\in[N_{\ell},2N_{\ell})

with
\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}\in[A,2A)
,

\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}\in[P,2P)

is
\ll(\log(2+B/P))^{\mathfrak{K}+\lvert J^{q}_{1}\rvert-2}
.
Thus

\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathfrak{K}}\geq 1}\,\sum_{n_{\ell}\geq 1:\,\ell\geq 1,\;\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}\,\frac{(\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\delta}+\alpha_{\ell^{\prime}}^{-\delta})^{-1}(\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\delta}+\varrho_{\ell^{\prime}}^{-\delta})^{-1}}{m_{1}\cdots m_{\mathfrak{K}}\prod_{\ell\geq 1:\,\mathsf{c}_{\ell}=\mathsf{c}_{\ell^{\prime}}}n_{\ell}}\ll\sum_{A,P\in\{2^{e}:e\in\mathbb{Z}\}}\frac{(\log(2+B/P))^{\mathfrak{K}+\lvert J^{q}_{1}\rvert-2}}{(A^{\delta}+A^{-\delta})(P^{\delta}+P^{-\delta})},

which is
\ll(\log{B})^{\mathfrak{K}+\lvert J^{q}_{1}\rvert-2}
.
But
\mathfrak{K}=\lvert J^{\ast}_{2}\rvert
,
so
\mathfrak{K}+\lvert J^{q}_{1}\rvert\leq\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))

by Proposition
3.4
.
Summing over
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
,
and using the
\mathbb{C}\pi^{\ast}{\mathbf{u}}
-invariance of
\widetilde{\mathsf{Z}}_{1}(\bm{z},1_{G})
for
\Re(\bm{z})
large
(which follows from (
2.31
), (
2.32
) for
\widetilde{X}
),
we get (
1.3
), in view of (
6.1
), (
6.2
).

The statement (
1.2
) follows from
(
1.3
)
and the fact that any continuous function on a compact interval can be approximated from above and below by functions in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
.

It would be interesting to know whether ergodic methods
(cf.
[
29
,
21
,
57
,
22
,
30
,
48
]
)
could prove Theorem
1.1
,
either quantitatively or up to
o(1)
.
Another natural question is whether universal torsors
(cf.
[
45
]
)
could be used in this setting.

7.
Examples

Classification of
X
is open.
Some examples can be found in
[
52
]
*§1;
see also
[
15
]
, which is however written in terms of left actions rather than right actions, which are equivalent via the map
g\mapsto g^{-1}
.
One can obtain further examples
by blowing up along closed
G
-invariant subschemes,
or by taking orbit closures
in projective
G
-representations
(cf.
[
47
]
*first two paragraphs of §1)
or in tensor products thereof.
Such operations might not keep
X
split.
However, we can verify the hypotheses of Theorem
1.1

in each example below.

We focus on constructions where interesting behavior with special divisors may occur.

Example 7.1
.

Embed
G
in
X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
by
(a,b)\mapsto([1:a],[a:b])
.
The left
G
-action on
G
does not extend to
X
.
The right
G
-action does:

([x_{0}:x_{1}],[t_{0}:t_{1}])(u_{1},u_{2})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}([x_{0}:x_{1}u_{1}],[t_{0}u_{1}:t_{0}u_{2}+t_{1}]).

Here
a=\frac{x_{1}}{x_{0}}
,
b=\frac{x_{1}t_{1}}{x_{0}t_{0}}
,
and
D=\{x_{1}=0\}\cup\{x_{0}=0\}\cup\{t_{0}=0\}
.
Thus
X
is split,
and
D
has strict normal crossings.
Moreover,
\lvert J\rvert=3
and

\mathfrak{L}=\lvert J^{0}_{1}\rvert=\mathfrak{K}=\lvert J^{\ast}_{2}\rvert=\lvert J_{3}\rvert=1
.

Example 7.2
.

Via universal torsors,
Manin’s conjecture has been resolved in essentially full generality
for singular quartic del Pezzo surfaces of singularity type

\mathbf{A}_{3}+\mathbf{A}_{1}
;
papers on the topic include
[
17
,
5
,
14
,
26
,
16
]
.
Let
S\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{4}_{\mathbb{Q}}

be the split form

x_{0}^{2}+x_{0}x_{3}+x_{2}x_{4}=x_{1}x_{3}-x_{2}^{2}=0.

Then
S
admits a unique structure as an equivariant compactification of
G

[
15
]
*Lemma 15,
but is neither toric nor a vector group compactification

[
13
]
*§5,
nor a bi-equivariant compactification of
G

[
52
]
*Proposition 1.6.
To realize this structure,
embed
G
in
S
by
(a,b)\mapsto[1-b:a^{2}:a:1:-(1-b)(2-b)/a]
,
with right
G
-action

[x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}:x_{3}:x_{4}](u_{1},u_{2})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[x_{0}-x_{2}u_{2}:x_{1}u_{1}^{2}:x_{2}u_{1}:x_{3}:(x_{4}+(2x_{0}+x_{3})u_{2}-x_{2}u_{2}^{2})/u_{1}].

Here
a=\frac{x_{1}}{x_{2}}=\frac{x_{2}}{x_{3}}

and
b=\frac{x_{3}-x_{0}}{x_{3}}
.
Moreover,
1-b=\frac{x_{0}}{x_{3}}

and
b-2=\frac{-x_{0}-x_{3}}{x_{3}}=\frac{x_{2}x_{4}}{x_{0}x_{3}}=\frac{ax_{4}}{x_{0}}
.

The singular points of
S
are

\mathbf{A}_{3}=[0:0:0:0:1]

and
\mathbf{A}_{1}=[0:1:0:0:0]
.
By
[
17
]
*§8,
the lines on
S
are
\ell_{1}=\{x_{0}=x_{1}=x_{2}=0\}
,

\ell_{2}=\{x_{0}+x_{3}=x_{1}=x_{2}=0\}
,
and
\ell_{3}=\{x_{0}=x_{2}=x_{3}=0\}
.
Here
\mathbf{A}_{3}\in\ell_{1}\cap\ell_{2}\cap\ell_{3}

and
\mathbf{A}_{1}\in\ell_{3}
.
We note that set-theoretically,
\ell_{1}\cup\ell_{2}\cup\ell_{3}=\{x_{2}=0\}\cap S

and
\{x_{2}\neq 0\}\cap S=\{x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}\neq 0\}\cap S=G
.

The surface
S
is normal.
Since
x_{2}
cuts out
\ell_{1}
on the open set
\{(x_{0}+x_{3})x_{3}\neq 0\}
,
and
x_{0}
cuts out
\ell_{1}
on the open set
\{x_{3}x_{4}\neq 0\}
,
we have
\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_{1}}(a)=\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_{1}}(b-1)=1
.
Similarly,
x_{2}
cuts out
\ell_{2}
on
\{x_{0}x_{3}\neq 0\}
,
and
x_{0}+x_{3}
cuts out
\ell_{2}
on
\{x_{3}x_{4}\neq 0\}
,
so
\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_{2}}(a)=\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_{2}}(b-2)=1
.
Therefore,
J^{1}_{1}\neq\emptyset

and
J^{2}_{1}\neq\emptyset
.
It turns out that
J^{\ast}_{2}\neq\emptyset
as well, as we will see below.

Let
\pi_{1}\colon S^{\prime}\to S
be the blowup of
S
along
\mathbf{A}_{3}\cup\mathbf{A}_{1}
.
Then
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathbf{A}_{1})=\ell_{0}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, say.
Also,
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathbf{A}_{3})=\ell_{4}\cup\ell_{5}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}}\cup_{[1:0:0]}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, say.
Let
\pi_{2}\colon S^{\prime\prime}\to S^{\prime}
be the blowup of
S^{\prime}

along the nodal singularity
\ell_{4}\cap\ell_{5}\in\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathbf{A}_{3})
.
Then
\pi_{2}^{-1}(\ell_{4}\cap\ell_{5})=\ell_{6}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, say.
Moreover,
S^{\prime\prime}
is smooth.
This can all be checked explicitly, as we now describe.

Let
\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime}_{3}
be the strict transforms of
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}
under
\pi_{1}
.
Since
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\in\mathbb{P}^{4}_{\mathbb{Q}}
are distinct lines,

\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime}_{3}
are pairwise disjoint.
Also,
\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2}
are disjoint from
\ell_{0}
,
since their images under
\pi_{1}
are disjoint.
On the other hand,
\ell^{\prime}_{3}\subseteq\{x_{0}=x_{2}=0\}
intersects
\ell_{0}

in the affine chart

(7.1)

\{((y_{0}x_{4},y_{2}x_{4},x_{4}),[y_{0}:y_{2}:1])\in\mathbb{A}^{3}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}:y_{0}^{2}+y_{0}y_{2}^{2}x_{4}+y_{2}=0\}\subseteq S^{\prime}

(given by
x_{1}=y_{4}=1
and
x_{3}=x_{2}^{2}
)
at the scheme
\{y_{0}=y_{2}=0\}\cap\{x_{4}=y_{0}^{2}+y_{2}=0\}
,
which is a reduced point.
Similarly,
\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime}_{3}
intersect
\ell_{4}\cup\ell_{5}

in the affine chart

\{((y_{0}z,y_{1}z,(1-y_{1})z),[y_{0}:y_{1}:1-y_{1}])\in\mathbb{A}^{3}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}:y_{1}(1-y_{1})-(y_{0}+1-y_{1})^{2}y_{0}^{2}z^{2}=0\}\subseteq S^{\prime}

(given by
x_{4}=y_{1}+y_{3}=1
,
x_{2}=-x_{0}^{2}-x_{0}x_{3}
, and
x_{1}+x_{3}=z
)
at the schemes

\begin{split}\{y_{0}=y_{1}=0\}\cap\{z=y_{1}(1-y_{1})=0\}&=\{y_{0}=y_{1}=z=0\}\\
\{y_{0}=-1,\;y_{1}=0\}\cap\{z=y_{1}(1-y_{1})=0\}&=\{y_{0}=-1,\;y_{1}=z=0\}\\
\{y_{0}=0,\;y_{1}=1\}\cap\{z=y_{1}(1-y_{1})=0\}&=\{y_{0}=z=0,\;y_{1}=1\},\end{split}

respectively.
By symmetry, we may identify
\ell_{4}
and
\ell_{5}
with the closures in
S^{\prime}

of
\{z=y_{1}=0\}
and
\{z=1-y_{1}=0\}
, respectively.
Then
\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2}
intersect
\ell_{4}\setminus\ell_{5}

at the reduced points
(\bm{0},[0:0:1]),(\bm{0},[-1:0:1])
, respectively,
and
\ell^{\prime}_{3}
intersects
\ell_{5}\setminus\ell_{4}

at the reduced point
(\bm{0},[0:1:0])
.
We can also check that
\ell_{4},\ell_{5}
intersect
in the affine chart

\{((x_{0},y_{1}x_{0},y_{3}x_{0}),[1:y_{1}:y_{3}])\in\mathbb{A}^{3}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}:y_{1}y_{3}-(1+y_{3})^{2}x_{0}^{2}=0\}\subseteq S^{\prime}

(given by
x_{4}=y_{0}=1
and
x_{2}=-x_{0}^{2}-x_{0}x_{3}
)
at the reduced point
(\bm{0},[1:0:0])
.

Let
\ell^{\prime}_{0},\ell^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime\prime}_{3},\ell^{\prime}_{4},\ell^{\prime}_{5}
be the strict transforms of
\ell^{\prime}_{0},\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime}_{3},\ell_{4},\ell_{5}
under
\pi_{2}
.
Then
\ell^{\prime}_{0},\ell^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime\prime}_{3}
are disjoint from
\ell_{6}
.
Also,
\ell^{\prime}_{4}\cap\ell_{6}
and
\ell^{\prime}_{5}\cap\ell_{6}
are distinct, reduced
\mathbb{Q}
-points.
We conclude that
X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}S^{\prime\prime}
is split,
and that
D=\ell^{\prime}_{0}\cup\ell^{\prime\prime}_{1}\cup\ell^{\prime\prime}_{2}\cup\ell^{\prime\prime}_{3}\cup\ell^{\prime}_{4}\cup\ell^{\prime}_{5}\cup\ell_{6}
has strict normal crossings.
So Theorem
1.1
applies to
X
.
But in the chart (
7.1
),
we have
y_{0}^{2}+y_{2}(1+y_{0}y_{2}x_{4})=0
,
so

a=\frac{x_{1}}{x_{2}}=\frac{1}{y_{2}x_{4}}=-\frac{1+y_{0}y_{2}x_{4}}{y_{0}^{2}x_{4}},\quad b=\frac{x_{3}-x_{0}}{x_{3}}=1-\frac{y_{0}x_{4}}{(y_{2}x_{4})^{2}}=1-\frac{(1+y_{0}y_{2}x_{4})^{2}}{y_{0}^{3}x_{4}},

where
y_{0}
cuts out
\ell^{\prime}_{3}
and
x_{4}
cuts out
\ell_{0}
.
In particular,
\operatorname{ord}_{\ell^{\prime}_{0}}(a)=\operatorname{ord}_{\ell^{\prime}_{0}}(b)=-1
,
so
J^{\ast}_{2}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus (
1.5
) fails,
and one cannot apply
[
52
]
*Theorem 5.1 to
X
.

Remark 7.3
.

Theorem
1.1

does not give any new examples of Manin’s conjecture for

generalized del Pezzo surfaces

in the sense of
[
15
]
*§4.
Nonetheless, Theorem
1.1

is in general new for other rational surfaces.
Our
X
generally satisfies

\mathfrak{K}\mathfrak{L}>0
,
and is generally
not del Pezzo,
not bi-equivariant,
not toric,
and not a vector group compactification.
To make
\operatorname{rank}(\operatorname{Pic}(X))
arbitrarily large, one could repeatedly apply blowups, centered at
G
-invariant
\mathbb{Q}
-points, to
X
in Example
7.2
.
This keeps
X
split, because the tangent line to a smooth
\mathbb{Q}
-curve at a
\mathbb{Q}
-point is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.
The resulting cover would suffice by
[
52
]
*Proposition 1.3.

We end by discussing an explicit projective representation
related to
[
9
]
.

Example 7.4
.

Let
G
act on
\mathbb{P}(\{q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]:\deg{q}\leq n\})\cong\mathbb{P}^{n}

by
q(x)\mapsto q(ax+b)
.
Suppose
n\geq 3
,
and choose a set
R\in\binom{\mathbb{Q}}{n}

on which
G(\mathbb{Q})
acts faithfully.
The closure
C
of the
G
-orbit of
[\prod_{\varrho\in R}(x-\varrho)]\in\mathbb{P}^{n}
is a singular equivariant compactification of
G
.
Its boundary includes
the twisted curve
[(Ax+B)^{n}]
via
(a,b)=(\lambda A,\lambda B)
with
\lambda\to\infty
,
and the line
[Ax+B]
via
(a,b)=(\epsilon A,\varrho+\epsilon B)
with
\varrho\in R
,
\epsilon\to 0
.
These are the only boundary components, since
C
is the image of the map

X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Bl}_{\{[0:\rho:1]:\,\rho\in R\}}(\mathbb{P}^{2})\to\mathbb{P}^{n}

resolving the rational map

\mathbb{P}^{2}\ni[a:b:c]\mapsto[{\textstyle\prod_{\rho\in R}(ax+b-c\rho)}]\in\mathbb{P}^{n}.

The
G
-action
[a:b:c](u_{1},u_{2})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[au_{1}:au_{2}+b:c]
,
being trivial on the line
\{a=0\}
,
makes
X\to C
an equivariant resolution of singularities.
Here
X
is split,

D=\{c=0\}\cup\{a=0\}
has strict normal crossings,

\lvert J\rvert=n+2
,

\mathfrak{K}=\lvert J^{\ast}_{2}\rvert=1
,

\lvert J^{\varrho}_{1}\rvert=1
(for
\varrho\in R
),
and
\mathfrak{L}=\lvert R\rvert=n
.
Incidentally, one can also realize
X
as a vector group compactification,
so that Manin’s conjecture for
X
is given by
[
9
]

(or
[
7
]
).
Theorem
1.1
gives a new proof.

This
X
is reminiscent of

[
20
]
*Example 1.8 on
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-orbits of binary forms,
whose geometry is explained in
[
31
,
51
]
.
But our groups, height functions, and points differ.
It would be interesting to find a common generalization of
[
51
]
and our work.
